# Renderer Landscape Audit — 2026-09-02

**Date:** 2026-09-02 (fork tip `afdf5f4909`, Batch 1381; fork synced to upstream v1.144). **Revised:** 2026-09-03 by Erchirion (lane A) at clone HEAD `01226c648a` (Batch 1404) — claim census rebuilt from the verification re-run, PlayCanvas / Babylon.js / Bevy-wgpu-naga mapped against the fork, gap list re-derived (§4, §5, appendix).
**Previous landscape audit:** [THREEJS_TECH_MINE_2026-07-05.md](THREEJS_TECH_MINE_2026-07-05.md) (2026-07-05). Subsystem-scoped audits since then: [VOXEL_POINTCLOUD_GSPLAT_AUDIT_2026-08-09.md](VOXEL_POINTCLOUD_GSPLAT_AUDIT_2026-08-09.md), [REFERENCE_VISUALS_CATALOG_2026-08-09.md](REFERENCE_VISUALS_CATALOG_2026-08-09.md).
**Targets:** (1) three.js `WebGPURenderer` + TSL; (2) luma.gl 9 / deck.gl 9; (3) the WebGPU platform (W3C spec, Chrome/Edge Dawn, Firefox wgpu, Safari WebKit); (4) CesiumJS upstream + cesium-native, 3D Tiles / glTF specs, MapLibre, iTwin.js, NASA 3DTilesRendererJS. Since 2026-09-03 also (5) PlayCanvas engine 2.x, (6) Babylon.js WebGPU engine, (7) Bevy / wgpu / naga — their claims were collected in the same 2026-09-02 pass but were never mapped against the fork until the revision; their per-claim findings live in the appendix (A.5–A.7) rather than in §2 / §3.
**Method:** every claim was checked against a primary source (spec text, repository source file, release note, PR) by one or two independent verifier votes, and every fork comparison was read against the fork tree at `file:line`. No claim is dropped: each carries its votes in the appendix, and the refuted and split ones are listed with the verifiers' reasoning.
**Disclosure (rewritten 2026-09-03):** the first verification pass (2026-09-02) dispatched 368 agents; 133 of them died before returning a verdict, and the document as first written counted their claims as refuted or unverifiable — the failure-as-verdict defect. That is where the original line ("178 claims were collected; 94 were refuted or unverifiable and are not in this document; 84 survive — 68 CONFIRMED, 16 PLAUSIBLE") came from: it described the harness, not the claims — and the body anchored 83, not 84. The re-run the same evening (`wf_6bacb2f9-033`, 368 agents, 235 results) verified all 177 claims in the collectors' banks: **171 KEPT, 2 REFUTED, 4 SPLIT, 0 unverified** — PlayCanvas 25/25 kept; luma.gl / deck.gl 20 kept, 1 refuted, 4 split (two votes each); WebGPU platform 25/25; CesiumJS upstream + geospatial 25/25; three.js 25/25; Bevy / wgpu / naga 27/27; Babylon.js 24 kept, 1 refuted. Every vote, URL, quote and fork mapping is in the appendix; the joined data is `F:/Dev/GH/cesium-webgpu-worker-archive/arch-lenses-2026-09-03/landscape-salvage/` (`landscape-verified-claims.json`, `SALVAGE_TABLE.md`, raw votes in `group-01.json`). A **PLAUSIBLE** vote means the primary source was reached but one detail rests on a secondary source, a 404'd companion page or an unreleased version; such claims still must not be briefed as premises without re-derivation (Principle 10). The inline CONFIRMED / PLAUSIBLE marks in §2 are the first pass's and are superseded by the appendix vote column (one changed: LUMA-T2, PLAUSIBLE → SPLIT). The 13 three.js claims the first pass verified but never read against the fork (THREE-F04..F07, G01..G03, P01..P04, T01, T02) remain unmapped and are listed as such.
**Status authority:** none. This is an evidence and dispatch-view input. Row status stays with the campaign queues and `FIX_QUEUE_2026-08-27_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md`; the proposed rows in §6 are proposals for `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md`, not launched work (campaign numbering stays add-only; C17 stays PROPOSED).

---

## 1. Executive summary

1. **Direction A — one renderer, one shader source, backends below the line.** three.js confines all divergence under an abstract `Backend` and compiles one TSL node graph to WGSL or GLSL ([THREE-A01](#three-a01)–[A04](#three-a04)); luma.gl assembles one unified WGSL with `@binding(auto)` and named injection hooks ([LUMA-A4](#luma-a4), [A5](#luma-a5)); MapLibre sequences UBO-first WebGL2 before a WebGPU backend behind one interface ([C17](#c17)).
2. **Direction B — GPU-driven everything.** Compute-built cluster light lists ([THREE-F01](#three-f01)), GPU hierarchy traversal with a screen-space-error metric feeding indirect draws ([LUMA-F2](#luma-f2)), GPU-native splat sorting and residency ([LUMA-F4](#luma-f4), [C23](#c23)), frame-graph pass ordering with graph-owned transients ([LUMA-A6](#luma-a6)), and a TSL software rasterizer ([C22](#c22)) — all without mesh shaders or multi-draw-indirect, which remain outside the web ([WGPU-18](#wgpu-18), [WGPU-19](#wgpu-19)).
3. **Direction C — a platform at Candidate Recommendation that grows by feature detection.** WebGPU and WGSL are CR Drafts ([WGPU-01](#wgpu-01), [WGPU-02](#wgpu-02)); immediates, compatibility mode, transient attachments, `primitive_index`, `linear_indexing`, subgroup size control, dual-source blending on Firefox, and a Dawn-prototyped bindless resource table all arrived since July ([WGPU-04](#wgpu-04)–[WGPU-17](#wgpu-17)); f64 is not coming ([WGPU-25](#wgpu-25)).
4. **Where the fork stands on A:** the `GraphicsContext` + `FeatureRenderer` shape is the same as three.js's and MapLibre's, and upstream still has no renderer abstraction to converge on ([C02](#c02)); but the fork keeps two live scene executors above the context boundary ([THREE-A01](#three-a01) row) and authors every shader twice ([THREE-A03](#three-a03), [C20](#c20)).
5. **Where the fork stands on B:** Forward+ clustered lighting already exceeds three.js r185 on capacity ([C21](#c21)); pipeline keys fold module identity as three.js and luma do ([THREE-A05](#three-a05), [LUMA-A3](#luma-a3)); but GPU traversal, scene-level indirect draw generation, GPU splat sort and a frame graph are all scaffold or absent ([LUMA-F1](#luma-f1), [F2](#luma-f2), [F4](#luma-f4), [A6](#luma-a6)).
6. **Where the fork stands on C:** the optional-feature list is negotiated per adapter, but no `ShaderDefine` bit is ever populated from `navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures`, so every new WGSL extension lands as a string-prepend outside the module cache ([WGPU-02](#wgpu-02) row); `primitive_index` is treated as core ([WGPU-03](#wgpu-03)); the transient-attachment gate can never match ([WGPU-08](#wgpu-08)); dual-source blending is requested but unwired ([WGPU-13](#wgpu-13)).
7. **The RTE rule is vindicated and now carries a new risk.** No platform f64 relief ([WGPU-25](#wgpu-25)); deck.gl's per-viewport rebase is weaker than the fork's per-vertex high/low split ([DECK-G1](#deck-g1)); but luma documents that the Metal compiler reassociates error-free transforms on Apple adapters ([LUMA-G3](#luma-g3)) and the fork has neither an integer-controlled `twoSum` nor an Apple probe lane.
8. **Upstream moved under the fork.** CesiumJS 1.145 (2026-09-01) rewrote `ClippingPolygons` with holes and draped vectors onto 3D Tiles ([C01](#c01), [C15](#c15)); the fork's WGSL SDF-atlas path has no hole concept and WebGPU polygon draping is absent even on terrain; the 1.146 edge-vertex-array leak is present in the fork's WebGL lane ([C13](#c13)).
9. **Ranked gap list (§4):** the 1.145 sync, Apple-adapter precision, extension-detected define bits, dual-source OIT, the snap gates, the voxel default shader, then the three XL structural items (GPU-driven traversal/draw generation, splat GPU path, frame graph) and the single-scene-executor consolidation.
10. **What not to copy (§5):** a TSL-style single-source rewrite, deck.gl's precision model, compatibility mode as a WebGL replacement, the TSL software rasterizer, `instance_index`-only picking, per-pass submits, and any bindless build on a Canary flag.
11. **Revision 2026-09-03.** Three more engines were mapped against the fork at `01226c648a` — PlayCanvas, Babylon.js, Bevy / wgpu / naga; 77 claims read, 76 mapped, one refuted ([appendix A.5–A.7](#appendix--claim-census)). The HIGH list G1–G10 is unchanged: the new mappings reinforce G7 ([PC-14](#census-pc-14), [BJS-17](#census-bjs-17), [WGPU-PERF-01](#census-wgpu-perf-01)), G8 ([PC-10](#census-pc-10), [PC-11](#census-pc-11), [PC-13](#census-pc-13), [BJS-14](#census-bjs-14)) and G9 ([PC-01](#census-pc-01), [BJS-05](#census-bjs-05)), add one counter-signal to G9 ([BEVY-ARCH-01](#census-bevy-arch-01)), add sixteen MEDIUM candidates (§4) and six "do not copy" items (§5).

---

## 2. Per-target findings

### 2.1 three.js `WebGPURenderer` + TSL (mrdoob/three.js)

**Latest release:** r185, 2026-07-01 ([releases/r185](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/releases/tag/r185)). r186 is in `dev` (native splat renderer merged) but was not a GitHub release on 2026-09-02 ([C23](#c23), secondary source).
**What changed architecturally since the 2026-07-05 audit:** r183 renamed `PostProcessing` to `RenderPipeline` (node-graph post-processing with MRT and automatic pass fusion); r184 added `DynamicLighting`; r185 added `ClusteredLighting` (removing `TiledLighting`), restored WebXR on the WebGPU backend, introduced cached/reset descriptor classes, surfaced uncaptured GPU errors and WGSL diagnostics, and merged a TSL software rasterizer.

<a id="three-a01"></a>**THREE-A01** (CONFIRMED) — `WebGPURenderer` is one shared renderer over pluggable backends: WebGPU by default, silent fallback to a WebGL 2 backend, `forceWebGL` pins WebGL 2. Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/WebGPURenderer.html> — "By default, the renderer tries to use a WebGPU backend if the browser supports WebGPU. If not, `WebGPURenderer` falls backs to a WebGL 2 backend."

<a id="three-a02"></a>**THREE-A02** (CONFIRMED) — All backend-specific behaviour is confined to an abstract `Backend` (beginRender/finishRender, draw, compute, createRenderPipeline, createBindings, getRenderCacheKey, createNodeBuilder, isOccluded, …); the common `Renderer` holds rendering logic. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/common/Backend.js> — "This abstract base class defines an interface that encapsulates all backend-related logic. Derived classes for each backend must implement the interface."

<a id="three-a03"></a>**THREE-A03** (CONFIRMED) — Shaders are authored once as a TSL node graph and compiled by `WGSLNodeBuilder` or `GLSLNodeBuilder`; no dual hand-written source, no textual preprocessor/defines layer. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/wiki/Three.js-Shading-Language> — "Currently `NodeBuilder` has two extended classes, the `WGSLNodeBuilder` aimed at WebGPU and `GLSLNodeBuilder` aimed at WebGL2."

<a id="three-a04"></a>**THREE-A04** (CONFIRMED) — `WGSLNodeBuilder` emits bind-group bindings and vertex-buffer layouts alongside shader text, and emits `enable <directive>;` (subgroups, subgroups-f16, f16, clip_distances, dual_source_blending) only when a node requests it. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/nodes/WGSLNodeBuilder.js> — "A node builder targeting WGSL. This module generates WGSL shader code from node materials and also generates the respective bindings and vertex buffer definitions."

<a id="three-a05"></a>**THREE-A05** (CONFIRMED) — Render pipelines are cached under vertex-program id + fragment-program id + backend render cache key; programs are reused across pipelines; pipelines are reference-counted and released at `usedTimes === 0`. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/common/Pipelines.js> — `stageVertex.id + ',' + stageFragment.id + ',' + this.backend.getRenderCacheKey()`.

<a id="three-a06"></a>**THREE-A06** (CONFIRMED) — Bind-group layouts are deduplicated through a cache keyed on the combination of layout entries. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/utils/WebGPUBindingUtils.js> — "A cache that maps combinations of layout entries to existing bind group layouts."

<a id="three-a07"></a>**THREE-A07** (CONFIRMED) — Command encoding is per render context / compute group (beginRender creates the encoder, finishRender finishes and submits); submission reuses a module-scoped single-element array (r185 "Optimize submit() calls", #33513). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/utils/WebGPUUtils.js> — "Submits a single GPU command to the device queue using a shared, module-scoped array to avoid per-call array allocations." Also <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/WebGPUBackend.js>.

<a id="three-a08"></a>**THREE-A08** (CONFIRMED) — r185 introduced dedicated, cached descriptor classes with `reset()` (render pipeline, pipeline layout, command encoder, render-bundle encoder, …) to cut per-frame allocation. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33525> — "caches the descriptor instances whenever possible to reduce per frame object allocation and GC overhead."

<a id="three-a09"></a>**THREE-A09** (PLAUSIBLE) — Post-processing is a node graph: `RenderPipeline` (renamed from `PostProcessing` in r183) takes an `outputNode` chain, has built-in MRT support and automatically combines passes; `EffectComposer` is unsupported on `WebGPURenderer`. Source: <https://threejs.org/manual/en/webgpurenderer.html> — "`WebGPURenderer` comes with a new post-processing stack with built-in Multiple Render Targets (MRT) support and automatic pass combination thanks to the node material." (The r183 rename is from the manual, not re-derived from the r183 changelog.)

<a id="three-f01"></a>**THREE-F01** (CONFIRMED) — r185 added `ClusteredLighting` (Forward+): screen tiles × exponential depth slices, compute-built per-cluster light lists (defaults 32 px tiles, 24 slices, 64 lights/cluster, 1024 lights); `TiledLighting` was removed in the same release (#33751). Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33406> — "partitions the view frustum into a 3D cluster grid (screen tiles × exponential depth slices)"; <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/releases/tag/r185>.

<a id="three-f02"></a>**THREE-F02** (CONFIRMED) — r184 added `DynamicLighting` (#33042): analytic lights batched into uniform arrays so light-count changes do not recompile materials (defaults 8 directional / 16 point / 16 spot / 4 hemisphere). Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/DynamicLighting.html> — "A custom lighting implementation that batches supported analytic lights into uniform arrays so light count changes do not recompile materials."; <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33042>.

<a id="three-f03"></a>**THREE-F03** (CONFIRMED) — r185 restored WebXR on the WebGPU backend (#33583, funded by Meta); `XRManager.getWebGPUBinding()` and a `multiview` renderer option exist. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33583> — "This PR brings the implementation back. Currently only WebGL is supported for WebXR."

### 2.2 luma.gl 9 / deck.gl 9 (visgl)

**Latest versions:** luma.gl 9.4.0-beta.6 (published 2026-09-02; 9.4 whats-new targets August 2026); luma.gl 9.3 (2026-03-31); deck.gl 9.3.11 still pins luma.gl 9.3, deck.gl 9.4 is in alphas ([C19](#c19); <https://registry.npmjs.org/@luma.gl/core>, <https://registry.npmjs.org/deck.gl>).
**What changed architecturally:** 9.4 moved all draw state (pipeline, bindings, vertex arrays, direct/indirect draws, render bundles) onto `RenderPass`; unified WGSL assembly with `@binding(auto)`, hook functions and named injections; an experimental frame-graph (`GPUCommandGraph`), GPU scene draw generation, GPU virtual-geometry selection, a splats package, a WebGPU-first post-processing set, WBOIT, integer-controlled fp64 on Apple adapters, and a packed-binary64 subtraction helper.

<a id="luma-a1"></a>**LUMA-A1** (CONFIRMED) — The command model is deferred on WebGPU (CommandEncoder → finish() → CommandBuffer → submit()) and effectively immediate on WebGL under the same API shape. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-guide/gpu/gpu-commands.md> — "On WebGPU, command encoding is truly deferred. … On WebGL, luma.gl preserves the same API shape where practical, but rendering remains effectively immediate."

<a id="luma-a2"></a>**LUMA-A2** (CONFIRMED) — In 9.4 all draw state moved from `RenderPipeline` onto `RenderPass`; the old APIs are deprecated compatibility paths. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "`RenderPass` now owns pipeline, binding, vertex-array, direct-draw, indirect-draw, and render-bundle commands. The former `RenderPipeline` draw and binding APIs remain as deprecated compatibility paths."

<a id="luma-a3"></a>**LUMA-A3** (CONFIRMED) — `PipelineFactory` caches pipelines per device keyed by a hash of pipeline inputs plus device type, with reference counting and a retain-at-zero policy. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/pipeline-factory.md> — "When the reference count reaches zero, the pipeline is either destroyed or retained depending on `device.props._destroyPipelines`."

<a id="luma-a4"></a>**LUMA-A4** (CONFIRMED) — WGSL is assembled as ONE unified source with all entry points; modules declare `@binding(auto)`, rewritten to concrete numbers before reflection/compile; app bindings from 0, module bindings from 100+. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/shadertools/wgsl-support.md> — "Shadertools rewrites `@binding(auto)` to concrete binding numbers before the final WGSL is reflected and compiled by WebGPU."

<a id="luma-a5"></a>**LUMA-A5** (PLAUSIBLE) — 9.4 adds WGSL hook functions and named injections (`vs:#main-start`, `fs:#main-end`) plus a `ShaderPlugin` unit; the preprocessor accepts numeric/boolean `#if` and capability defines evaluated before binding assignment. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "`ShaderAssembler` now applies registered hook functions and standard named injections such as `vs:#main-start` and `fs:#main-end` while assembling unified WGSL shaders." (The numeric-`#if`/capability-define detail is from the same page's surrounding text, not an independent source.)

<a id="luma-a6"></a>**LUMA-A6** (CONFIRMED) — `GPUCommandGraph` is a frame-graph: fixed-capacity buffer/texture resources plus ordered compute/render/copy nodes; declared uses drive ordering and transient lifetime; compile once, `encode()` each frame into the caller's encoder. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/gpu-core/gpu-command-graph.md> — "GPUCommandGraph<Parameters> declares fixed-capacity WebGPU buffer and texture resources plus ordered compute, render, and copy nodes."

<a id="luma-p1"></a>**LUMA-P1** (CONFIRMED) — 9.4 adds render bundles (WebGL2-unsupported); rebuild on command-list / bound-resource / pipeline / attachment-compatibility change, not on buffer/texture content change. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/resources/render-bundle-encoder.md> — "Render bundles allow expensive CPU-side command recording and validation to be performed once and reused, improving performance when many identical draw calls are repeated frame after frame."

<a id="luma-p2"></a>**LUMA-P2** (CONFIRMED) — 9.4.0-alpha.1 skips redundant uniform-buffer writes in core. Source: <https://github.com/visgl/luma.gl/releases/tag/v9.4.0-alpha.1> — "fix(core): Avoid redundant uniform buffer writes (#2644)".

<a id="luma-p3"></a>**LUMA-P3** (CONFIRMED) — Portable timestamp `QuerySet` gated on `device.features.has('timestamp-query')`, backed by `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` on WebGL, results asynchronous. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/resources/query-set.md> — "Timestamp queries are portable when `device.features.has('timestamp-query')` is `true`. On WebGL this is backed by [`EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`]."

<a id="deck-p4"></a>**DECK-P4** (CONFIRMED) — deck.gl 9.4 picking uses `instance_index` instead of per-object picking colours for most layers. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "Picking has been optimized. Most layers now use shader builtins (`instance_index`) instead of picking color buffers, reducing GPU memory usage and layer initialization costs."

<a id="luma-f1"></a>**LUMA-F1** (CONFIRMED) — `GPUSceneDrawGeneration` converts visible `GPUScene` records into bounded indirect draw commands on the GPU; requires `indirect-first-instance`; WebGPU has no portable bindless multi-draw, so one indirect draw per command slot. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/gpu-core/gpu-scene-draw-generation.md> — "`GPUSceneDrawGeneration` turns active, visible `GPUScene` records into bounded indirect draw commands."

<a id="luma-f2"></a>**LUMA-F2** (CONFIRMED) — GPU virtual-geometry selection: bounding-sphere frustum culling plus a screen-space-error metric refines a hierarchy whose cluster IDs may be meshlets, point-cloud nodes, impostors or terrain patches; counts feed indirect draws. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/gpu-core/gpu-virtual-geometry-selection.md> — "Cluster IDs can address meshlets, point-cloud nodes, impostors, terrain patches, or application-defined geometry records."

<a id="luma-f4"></a>**LUMA-F4** (PLAUSIBLE) — `@luma.gl/splats` renders Gaussian splats GPU-natively on WebGPU (project, sort, draw per streamed batch through a command graph; bounded residency, hierarchy traversal, foveated LOD, GPU picking); WebGL2 gets only a prepared `SplatRenderer` path. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/splats/README.md> — "The graph renderer, GPU ordering, hierarchy traversal, and storage-backed features require WebGPU. Use the prepared SplatRenderer path when WebGL 2 compatibility or caller-provided source ordering is required." (Beta-stage package; the feature list is from its README, not from an executed run.)

<a id="luma-f5"></a>**LUMA-F5** (CONFIRMED) — 9.4 ships a WebGPU-first composable post-processing set (depth-aware blur, SSAO, temporally stabilised GTAO, SSGI, outlines, TAA, motion blur, temporally stabilised SSR, height fog, clustered volumetric lighting, GPU-driven adaptive exposure, HDR-safe multiscale bloom). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> (line 194, "## Version 9.4") — "New WebGPU-first composable pipelines provide depth-aware blur, SSAO, temporally stabilized GTAO, colored screen-space diffuse global illumination, outlines, temporal AA, motion blur, roughness-aware temporally stabilized screen-space reflections, compact height fog, bounded clustered participating-media lighting with camera-aware history, GPU-driven adaptive exposure, and HDR-safe successively filtered multiscale bloom."

<a id="luma-f6"></a>**LUMA-F6** (CONFIRMED) — Experimental WBOIT renderer on WebGPU and WebGL2 with two `rgba16float` accumulation/revealage targets; A-buffer renderer as the alternative. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/wboit-renderer.md> — "WBOITRenderer implements weighted blended order-independent transparency on WebGPU and WebGL2."

<a id="deck-g1"></a>**DECK-G1** (CONFIRMED) — deck.gl compensates for missing f64 with a viewport-determined dynamic translation of common-space positions; offset coordinate systems trade accuracy for a linearised local projection. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/developer-guide/coordinate-systems.md> — "To compensate for the lack of 64-bit floats in WebGL2/WebGPU, deck.gl may apply a dynamic translation to common-space positions, determined by the viewport, to improve the precision of projection."

<a id="luma-g2"></a>**LUMA-G2** (CONFIRMED) — luma's fp64 is double-single (hi+lo f32, ~48 significant bits); the full function library is GLSL-only; WGSL gets only `fp64arithmetic` (add/sub/mul/div/sqrt). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/shadertools/shader-modules/fp64.md> — "The full `fp64` function library is GLSL-only. It does not provide WGSL source, so WGSL shaders cannot call `exp_fp64`, `log_fp64`, or its trigonometric functions."

<a id="luma-g3"></a>**LUMA-G3** (CONFIRMED) — On Apple WebGPU adapters luma switches double-single arithmetic to integer-controlled `twoSum`/`twoProd`/renormalisation because the Metal compiler may reassociate/fuse float ops; `LUMA_FP64_INTEGER_ARITHMETIC` forces or disables it. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "WGSL double-single arithmetic automatically uses integer-controlled `twoSum`, `twoProd`, and renormalization on Apple WebGPU adapters, avoiding Metal compiler reassociation while retaining the existing `vec2f` API."

<a id="luma-g4"></a>**LUMA-G4** (CONFIRMED) — Large-coordinate rendering needs one exact operation — `localPosition = round_f32(exact(worldPosition - worldOrigin))` — implemented by subtracting packed binary64 significands/exponents with integer ops in WGSL and rounding once. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-guide/shaders/gpu-floating-point-precision.md> — "Large-coordinate rendering often does not need general fp64 arithmetic. It needs this operation: `localPosition = round_f32(exact(worldPosition - worldOrigin))`".

<a id="luma-t1"></a>**LUMA-T1** (CONFIRMED) — Resource `id` is copied into the WebGPU object `label`; `luma.log` offers persistent debug flags/log levels; WebGL dev-tools/Spector.js integration lives behind a separate `@luma.gl/webgl/debug` entry. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/developer-guide/debugging.md> — "`id` is copied into the WebGPU object `label` field which is designed to support debugging".

<a id="luma-t2"></a>**LUMA-T2** (PLAUSIBLE) — `SnapshotTestRunner` does browser-driven golden-image pixel diffs with tolerance/threshold; deck.gl enabled render tests on WebGPU in the 9.4 alphas; luma 9.4 adds feature-level-specific WebGPU test devices. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/test-utils/snapshot-test-runner.md> — "pixel-diffing with a pre-defined 'golden image'". (The deck.gl alpha render-test enablement is from the deck.gl releases page, <https://github.com/visgl/deck.gl/releases>, not re-derived from a PR.)

### 2.3 The WebGPU platform (W3C, Dawn, wgpu, WebKit)

**Latest versions:** WebGPU API W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft 2026-09-01; WGSL CRD 2026-08-31; Chrome 152 stable 2026-08-25; Firefox 155 2026-09-01; Safari 26.2 2025-12-12; wgpu v30.0.0 2026-07-01.
**What changed architecturally:** both specs reached CR; language extensions are runtime-detected via `navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures`; Compatibility Mode shipped (Chrome 146); Immediates (push-constant equivalent) shipped (Chrome 149-150); transient attachments, `linear_indexing`, `subgroup-size-control`, `texture-formats-tier1/2`, `primitive_index` and dual-source blending (Firefox) shipped; bindless is a Draft proposal with a live Dawn prototype; multi-view is being redesigned; mesh shaders and f64 remain Milestone 4+.

<a id="wgpu-01"></a>**WGPU-01** (CONFIRMED) — The WebGPU API is a W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft dated 1 September 2026. Source: <https://www.w3.org/TR/webgpu/> — "W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft, 1 September 2026".

<a id="wgpu-02"></a>**WGPU-02** (CONFIRMED) — WGSL is a CRD dated 31 August 2026 enumerating runtime-detectable language extensions (readonly_and_readwrite_storage_textures, packed_4x8_integer_dot_product, unrestricted_pointer_parameters, pointer_composite_access, uniform_buffer_standard_layout, subgroup_id, subgroup_uniformity, texture_and_sampler_let, texture_formats_tier1, linear_indexing, immediate_address_space, fragment_depth, buffer_view, swizzle_assignment) alongside enable-extensions (f16, clip_distances, dual_source_blending, subgroups, primitive_index, subgroup_size_control); authoring is feature-detected via `navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures`. Source: <https://www.w3.org/TR/WGSL/> — "W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft, 31 August 2026".

<a id="wgpu-03"></a>**WGPU-03** (CONFIRMED) — `GPUFeatureName` now includes "subgroups", "texture-formats-tier1", "texture-formats-tier2", "primitive-index", "texture-component-swizzle", "subgroup-size-control", "texture-compression-unaligned" and "core-features-and-limits". Source: <https://gpuweb.github.io/gpuweb/> — `"core-features-and-limits" ... "subgroups" "texture-formats-tier1" "texture-formats-tier2" "primitive-index" "texture-component-swizzle" "subgroup-size-control" "texture-compression-unaligned"`.

<a id="wgpu-04"></a>**WGPU-04** (PLAUSIBLE) — Chrome 146 (2026-02-25) shipped Compatibility Mode (`featureLevel: "compatibility"`) on OpenGL ES 3.1-class hardware. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-146> — "This mode lets you run WebGPU on older graphics APIs like OpenGL ES 3.1." (Milestone-per-platform detail is from the Intent to Ship, not re-derived.)

<a id="wgpu-05"></a>**WGPU-05** (CONFIRMED) — Compatibility Mode restrictions: no cube-array textures; bound array views must cover the whole array; identical blend state across colour attachments; no compressed-texture copies; no `textureLoad` on depth textures; no integer multisampled formats; `maxStorageBuffersInVertexStage` / `maxStorageTexturesInFragmentStage` default to 0 and 4. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/blob/main/proposals/compatibility-mode.md> — "Array texture views used in bind groups must consist of the entire array".

<a id="wgpu-06"></a>**WGPU-06** (CONFIRMED) — Chrome 149-150 (2026-06-17) shipped Immediates: `var<immediate>` plus `setImmediates()` on pass encoders, detected via the `immediate_address_space` language extension. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-149-150> — "Call setImmediates() in JavaScript before a draw call to provide this data".

<a id="wgpu-07"></a>**WGPU-07** (PLAUSIBLE) — The immediates spec PR (gpuweb #5423, merged 2026-05-07): `maxImmediateSize` 64 bytes; available on all core devices; after a render bundle executes, immediate data is uninitialized. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/pull/5423> — "Specify immediate data API and WGSL <immediate> address space". (The 64-byte and post-bundle rules are read from the PR diff; the merged spec text was not separately quoted.)

<a id="wgpu-08"></a>**WGPU-08** (PLAUSIBLE) — Chrome 146 added `GPUTextureUsage.TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT` for memoryless attachments; Chrome 149-150 tightened validation (empty `viewFormats`, fixed view usage, not usable as `resolveTarget`). Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-146> — "By declaring a texture as transient (or 'memoryless'), the GPU knows that it only needs the texture's contents temporarily." (The 149-150 tightening could not be confirmed: the -148/-149/-150 companion pages 404'd.)

<a id="wgpu-09"></a>**WGPU-09** (CONFIRMED) — Chrome 147-148 (2026-04-22) shipped `linear_indexing` (`global_invocation_index`, `workgroup_index`) and Linux WebGPU on modern NVIDIA/Wayland. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-147-148> — "global_invocation_index: The current invocation's linear position within the total compute shader grid".

<a id="wgpu-10"></a>**WGPU-10** (CONFIRMED) — Chrome 152 stable (2026-08-25) added the optional "subgroup-size-control" feature. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/152> — "Adds the optional GPU feature "subgroup-size-control" that allows explicitly setting the subgroup size in a compute shader."

<a id="wgpu-11"></a>**WGPU-11** (CONFIRMED) — Chrome 144 (2026-01-07) made `writeBuffer`/`writeTexture` up to 2× faster, shipped `subgroup_id` and `uniform_buffer_standard_layout`, and began WebGPU on Linux (Intel Gen12+). Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-144> — "Performance gains up to 2X better than the previous version, depending on the size of data".

<a id="wgpu-12"></a>**WGPU-12** (CONFIRMED) — Chrome 142 (2025-10-22) shipped `texture-formats-tier1` (r16unorm/rg16unorm/rgba16unorm families with render/storage) and the WGSL `primitive_index` built-in. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-142> — "The primitive_index is a built-in WGSL value that uniquely identifies the current primitive being processed by a fragment shader."

<a id="wgpu-13"></a>**WGPU-13** (CONFIRMED) — Firefox 155 (2026-09-01) added the `dual-source-blending` feature on desktop. Source: <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/155> — "The WebGPU API now supports the dual-source-blending feature on desktop, which can be requested in GPUAdapter.requestDevice()."

<a id="wgpu-14"></a>**WGPU-14** (CONFIRMED) — Implementation Status (2026-08-13): Chrome on Mac/Windows/ChromeOS (113), Android (121), Linux Intel Gen12+ (144), Linux NVIDIA/Wayland (147); Firefox on Windows (141) and Apple Silicon macOS (145/147), Linux/Android Nightly-only; Safari on macOS/iOS/iPadOS/visionOS 26. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/wiki/Implementation-Status> — "Mozilla expects to ship on Linux in 2026."

<a id="wgpu-15"></a>**WGPU-15** (PLAUSIBLE) — Safari 26.2 (2025-12-12) made WebXR usable with WebGPU on visionOS and allowed `GPUTexture` as depth-stencil/resolve attachments; the 2026-04-01 gpuweb minutes confirm the binding shipped on Vision Pro and is prototyped in Chromium. Source: <https://webkit.org/blog/17640/webkit-features-for-safari-26-2/> — "Now with Safari 26.2 on visionOS, WebXR supports WebGPU." (The Chromium prototype status is from meeting minutes.)

<a id="wgpu-16"></a>**WGPU-16** (CONFIRMED) — The bindless proposal (Draft, 2025-10-13) introduces `GPUResourceTable`: sparse heterogeneous table with insert/update/remove, one active table per encoder, `setResourceUsage()`, default resources for invalid indices; the 2026-03-10 WGSL minutes place barycentrics after bindless. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/blob/main/proposals/bindless.md> — "a variable-size, sparse and (totally or partially) heterogenous set of bindings"; <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/wiki/GPU-Web-2026%E2%80%9003%E2%80%9010-WGSL>.

<a id="wgpu-17"></a>**WGPU-17** (CONFIRMED) — Dawn main carries a `ResourceTable` frontend (`src/dawn/native/ResourceTable.h`) with commits through August 2026. Source: <https://dawn.googlesource.com/dawn/+/refs/heads/main/src/dawn/native/ResourceTable.h> — "ResourceTableBase implements the frontend tracking for GPUResourceTable, a sparse array of heterogeneous resources that can be accessed in shaders."

<a id="wgpu-18"></a>**WGPU-18** (CONFIRMED) — Mesh shaders remain outside WebGPU: gpuweb #3015 open at Milestone 4+ with no proposal; wgpu v30.0.0 (2026-07-01) added native mesh shaders, not exposed to the web. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/issues/3015> — "Milestone 4+"; <https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/releases/tag/v30.0.0>.

<a id="wgpu-19"></a>**WGPU-19** (PLAUSIBLE) — Multi-draw indirect is non-standard: Dawn `wgpu::FeatureName::MultiDrawIndirect` with `maxDrawCount` + optional count buffer; Chrome exposes `chromium-experimental-multi-draw-indirect` behind unsafe-WebGPU; gpuweb #5175 at Milestone 3. Source: <https://dawn.googlesource.com/dawn/+/HEAD/docs/dawn/features/multi_draw_indirect.md> — "The number of draw calls executed is min(maxDrawCount, countBuffer)". (The Chrome flag name and #5175 milestone are from the Dawn doc's cross-references.)

<a id="wgpu-20"></a>**WGPU-20** (CONFIRMED) — Timestamp-query values are quantized to lower precision for security/privacy. Source: <https://gpuweb.github.io/gpuweb/#timestamp> — "To mitigate security and privacy concerns, the timing query values are aligned to a lower precision"; also <https://www.w3.org/TR/webgpu/#timestamp>.

<a id="wgpu-21"></a>**WGPU-21** (CONFIRMED) — Render bundles amortize JS draw-call overhead: encoded once, replayed via `executeBundles()`. Source: <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/GPURenderBundle> — "Reusing pre-recoded commands can significantly improve app performance in situations where JavaScript draw call overhead is a bottleneck."

<a id="wgpu-22"></a>**WGPU-22** (CONFIRMED) — Multi-view is being redesigned: the 2026-04-01 meeting closed #4806/#4109 in favour of dynamic layer/viewport selection (#4812, up to 16 layers + 16 viewports) plus a vertex-amplification bug; variable-rate rendering judged irreconcilable across hardware. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/wiki/GPU-Web-2026%E2%80%9004%E2%80%9001> — "up to 16 array layer + 16 viewports".

<a id="wgpu-23"></a>**WGPU-23** (CONFIRMED) — WebGPU Inspector (brendan-duncan) inspects live GPU objects with stack traces, captures frames with per-pass outputs, supports live shader editing, multi-frame captures to self-contained files, and CPU-submit vs GPU-time plots. Source: <https://github.com/brendan-duncan/webgpu_inspector> — "Inspection, profiling, and graphics debugging browser extension for WebGPU."

<a id="wgpu-24"></a>**WGPU-24** (CONFIRMED) — The gpuweb CTS is normative, embeddable in WPT or standalone, and Dawn rolls it continuously. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/cts> — "The contents of this test suite are considered normative; implementations must pass them to be WebGPU-conformant."

<a id="wgpu-25"></a>**WGPU-25** (CONFIRMED) — No double precision: WGSL defines only f32 and f16; gpuweb #2805 (2022-04-28, citing GIS) remains open at Milestone 4+. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/issues/2805> — "Double precision floats (IEEE-754 binary64) are useful for various applications".

### 2.4 CesiumJS upstream, cesium-native, specs, and the other geospatial engines

**Latest versions:** CesiumJS 1.145 (2026-09-01); cesium-native 0.64.0 (2026-09-01, in Cesium for Unreal 2.29.1); MapLibre GL JS 6.7.0 (2026-09-02, no WebGPU); deck.gl 9.3.11; KHR_gaussian_splatting Release Candidate.
**What changed architecturally:** upstream still has no WebGPU renderer; 1.144/1.145 added `Scene.snap`, vector draping onto terrain then 3D Tiles, a rewritten `ClippingPolygons` algorithm with holes, edge-rendering heap reductions and (1.146 unreleased) an edge vertex-array leak fix; cesium-native added area paging and general culling volumes; three.js gained a TSL software rasterizer and (r186, unreleased) a GPU counting-sort splat renderer.

<a id="c01"></a>**C01** (CONFIRMED) — CesiumJS 1.145 (published 2026-09-01T18:51:41Z): vector-tile draping onto 3D Tiles, rewritten `ClippingPolygons` with hole support, experimental `IonSnapService`. Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/releases/tag/1.145> — "`ClippingPolygons` now use an algorithm, based on the techniques used for vector tiles, that vastly improves quality across distance scales. Warm-up cost is also modestly decreased."

<a id="c02"></a>**C02** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream has no WebGPU renderer: #4989 (opened 2017-02-10) is Open on 2026-09-02. Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/issues/4989> — "This is a long-term roadmap issue to track development of NXT, the NeXT generation graphics API for the Web".

<a id="c03"></a>**C03** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream's model renderer is a three-level pipeline-stage architecture (model/node/primitive stages) mutating `RenderResources` around a `ShaderBuilder` before `ModelDrawCommands.buildModelDrawCommand`. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/Model/ModelSceneGraph.js> — "an in memory representation of the scene graph for a {@link Model}".

<a id="c04"></a>**C04** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream shader composition is string assembly (`ShaderBuilder` defines/structs/functions; `ShaderSource` czm_ dependency scan, `#define` lines, `#version 300 es`). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Renderer/ShaderBuilder.js> — "Add a #define macro to one or both of the shaders. These lines will appear at the top of the final shader source."

<a id="c05"></a>**C05** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream `ShaderCache` keys on vertex key + fragment key + sorted attribute locations, with reference counting and deferred destruction. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Renderer/ShaderCache.js> — "Returns a shader program from the cache, or creates and caches a new shader program, given the GLSL vertex and fragment shader source and attribute locations."

<a id="c06"></a>**C06** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream emulates 64-bit positions via `EncodedCartesian3` high/low float32 split attributes. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Core/EncodedCartesian3.js> — "Encodes a 64-bit floating-point value as two floating-point values that, when converted to 32-bit floating-point and added, approximate the original input."

<a id="c07"></a>**C07** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream splat sorting is CPU-side: `GaussianSplatSorter` runs a WASM radix sort in `TaskProcessor` workers. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/GaussianSplatSorter.js> — "Sorts Gaussian splats using a radix sort algorithm. Sorted by distance from the camera."

<a id="c08"></a>**C08** (CONFIRMED) — `GaussianSplatPrimitive` aggregates selected tiles into one primitive, packs attributes into an RGBA uint texture read by splat ID, renders premultiplied (depth test on, write off, cull off), re-sorts on camera thresholds. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/GaussianSplatPrimitive.js> — "The texture uses an RGBA unsigned-integer format and nearest-neighbor sampling."

<a id="c09"></a>**C09** (PLAUSIBLE) — KHR_gaussian_splatting (Release Candidate on 2026-09-02, announced 2026-02-03): POINTS primitives with POSITION/ROTATION/SCALE/OPACITY/SH_DEGREE_l_COEF_n, back-to-front sorting + alpha blending, compression extensions must extend it. Source: <https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_gaussian_splatting> — "The mode property of the mesh primitive MUST be POINTS (0)." (RC status read from the spec page banner; the announcement date is secondary.)

<a id="c10"></a>**C10** (CONFIRMED) — 3DTILES_content_voxels (Draft, `voxels` branch; the main-branch URL 404s): glTF with EXT_primitive_voxels + EXT_structural_metadata; dimensions and padding; box/region/cylinder bounding volumes. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/3d-tiles/voxels/extensions/3DTILES_content_voxels/README.md> — "Voxels are stored as glTFs with the `EXT_primitive_voxels` extension and are typically paired with `EXT_structural_metadata`".

<a id="c11"></a>**C11** (CONFIRMED) — Upstream `VoxelPrimitive` ray-marches with a step-size multiplier over an octree in node textures + megatextures; box/ellipsoid/cylinder; default-generated CustomShader; experimental API. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/VoxelPrimitive.js> — "The step size multiplier used during raymarching. The lower the value, the higher the rendering quality, but also the worse the performance."

<a id="c12"></a>**C12** (PLAUSIBLE) — `Scene.snap` (1.144; `surfacePosition` in 1.145) reuses pickBegin/pickEnd with an RGBA32F framebuffer carrying pick ID, is-edge flag and linear eye depth; only Model commands with a `snapId` render via a DerivedCommand snap shader; edges preferred over surfaces. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/Snapping.js> — "an offscreen pick render (it reuses the pick render machinery via `pickBegin`/`pickEnd`) that targets a dedicated RGBA32F framebuffer instead of the RGBA8 pick framebuffer." (The 1.145 `surfacePosition` addition is from the release notes; the framebuffer detail is from `main`.)

<a id="c13"></a>**C13** (PLAUSIBLE) — Edge rendering for EXT_mesh_primitive_edge_visibility uses an `EdgeFramebuffer` with MRT attachments; 1.144 cut its JS heap use; unreleased 1.146 fixes a GPU memory leak of the edge vertex array. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/CHANGES.md> — "Fixed a GPU memory leak where the edge vertex array created for `EXT_mesh_primitive_edge_visibility` rendering was never destroyed when draw commands were rebuilt or the model was destroyed." (The "up to 19×" figure is from the release blog, secondary.)

<a id="c14"></a>**C14** (CONFIRMED) — 1.144 fixed a one-frame black flash from a stale framebuffer-binding cache after `Framebuffer` construction; also a fatal error when a post-process stage selected more features than the maximum texture size. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/CHANGES.md> — "Fixed a one-frame black flash caused by `Framebuffer` construction leaving the context's framebuffer binding cache stale, making subsequent draws render to the wrong framebuffer for the remainder of the frame."

<a id="c15"></a>**C15** (CONFIRMED) — 1.145 drapes clamped vector polygons/polylines onto 3D Tiles via a `heightReference` option on `BufferPrimitiveCollection` / `BufferPolygonCollection` / `BufferPolylineCollection`, `MVTDataProvider` and `GeoJsonPrimitive`. Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/releases/tag/1.145> — "Added support for draping clamped vector tile polygons and polylines onto 3D Tiles, with a new `heightReference` option and matching read-only property on `BufferPrimitiveCollection`, inherited by `BufferPolygonCollection` and `BufferPolylineCollection`."; <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/1.145/CHANGES.md>.

<a id="c16"></a>**C16** (CONFIRMED) — cesium-native v0.64.0 (2026-09-01): area paging (fixed-LOD region selection), `ViewState` culling generalised to arbitrary culling volumes, `BoundingVolume` intersection tests. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium-native/main/CHANGES.md> — "Added support for "area paging": selection of a region of terrain tiles at a fixed level of detail."

<a id="c17"></a>**C17** (PLAUSIBLE) — MapLibre GL JS graphics-modernization roadmap: WebGL2 immutable textures/layout qualifiers → Drawable + UBO architecture → integer attributes/instancing → WebGPU backend (WGSL port + WebGPU render tests) sharing all non-GPU code; GL JS 6.7.0 ships no WebGPU; maplibre-native has WebGPU on Android/iOS. Source: <https://maplibre.org/roadmap/maplibre-gl-js/graphics-modernization/> — "Each backend (OpenGL, Metal, WebGPU) implements the same rendering interface." (Roadmap page, not shipped code.)

<a id="c18"></a>**C18** (CONFIRMED) — deck.gl v9 WebGPU is not production-ready: only IconLayer, LineLayer, PointCloudLayer, ScatterplotLayer and PathLayer have WGSL; picking, shader hooks/extensions, post-processing and base-map integration are WebGL-only. Source: <https://deck.gl/docs/developer-guide/webgpu> — "WebGPU support in deck.gl v9 is still a work in progress and is not production ready."

<a id="c19"></a>**C19** (CONFIRMED) — luma.gl 9.4 (beta.6 on 2026-09-02; deck.gl 9.3.11 pins 9.3): `RenderPass` owns draw state with bundles and indirect draws; `@binding(auto)`; experimental `@luma.gl/splats`; 9.3 (2026-03-31) added multi-canvas via `device.createPresentationContext()`. Source: <https://luma.gl/docs/whats-new> — "RenderPass owns draw state and supports render bundles, direct draws, and indirect draws."; <https://registry.npmjs.org/@luma.gl/core>; <https://registry.npmjs.org/deck.gl>.

<a id="c20"></a>**C20** (CONFIRMED) — TSL authors shaders as JS node graphs compiled to WGSL or GLSL from one source; nodes rewrite their own output snippet and de-duplicate expressions. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/wiki/Three.js-Shading-Language> — "is also capable of encoding code into different outputs such as `WGSL`/`GLSL` - `WebGPU`/`WebGL`".

<a id="c21"></a>**C21** (PLAUSIBLE) — three.js r185 `ClusteredLighting` details: sphere-vs-AABB compute pass, materials evaluate only lights reaching the fragment; defaults 32 px, 24 slices, 64 lights/cluster, 1024 lights. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33406> — "Adds a `ClusteredLighting` addon that partitions the view frustum into a 3D cluster grid (screen tiles × exponential depth slices). A compute pass builds a per-cluster light list via sphere-vs-AABB tests". (Same PR as THREE-F01; the per-fragment evaluation claim is from the PR description.)

<a id="c22"></a>**C22** (CONFIRMED) — three.js merged (2026-05-21) an experimental Nanite-style GPU-driven software rasterizer in TSL compute (rasterization, culling, deferred texture sampling on the GPU; 1B+ triangles in the demo). Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33605> — "This PR introduces a experimental, GPU-Driven Software Rasterizer implemented in TSL via compute shaders."

<a id="c23"></a>**C23** (CONFIRMED, secondary source) — three.js r186 gains a native Gaussian splat renderer (WebGPU + TSL) whose sort is a GPU counting sort: 4096 depth bins, four compute passes (reset, histogram, prefix sum, scatter); PLY/SPLAT/SPZ/KSPLAT/glTF KHR_gaussian_splatting. Source: <https://radiancefields.com/three.js-merges-a-native-gaussian-splat-renderer-for-webgpu-in-r186> — "quantizes splat depth into bins, 4096 by default, and dispatches four TSL compute passes from compute(renderer), a reset, a histogram, a prefix sum and a scatter." (r186 was not a GitHub release on 2026-09-02.)

<a id="c24"></a>**C24** (PLAUSIBLE) — r185 surfaces uncaptured GPU errors and WGSL diagnostics, adds WebXR on WebGPU, and caches TSL function nodes per backend. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/releases/tag/r185> — "Surface uncaptured GPU errors and WGSL diagnostics. #33418". (Per-backend node caching is from the release note title only.)

<a id="c25"></a>**C25** (PLAUSIBLE) — CesiumJS rebuilt Sandcastle as a TypeScript workspace package (Vite + React + iTwinUI + Monaco); replaced the legacy Sandcastle in October 2025; AI semantic search (2026-03) and Sandcastle Copilot (2026-07). Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/issues/12566> — "We plan to create a new workspace/package within our mono-repo to keep the new Sandcastle project separated but co-located with CesiumJS." (Dates of the search/Copilot additions are from blog posts.)

## 3. Comparison matrix

Fork status: **HAS** (equivalent or stronger, live), **PARTIAL** (present but incomplete, scaffolded, or one-backend), **MISSING**. Evidence is `packages/engine/Source/…` unless prefixed; `R/` = `Renderer/WebGPU/`, `S/` = `Shaders/WebGPU/`.

PlayCanvas, Babylon.js and Bevy / wgpu / naga (mapped 2026-09-03 at `01226c648a`) have no table in this section; their per-claim fork status, `file:line` evidence and meaning are in appendix A.5–A.7, and the §4 addenda cite them by `census-` anchor.

### 3.1 three.js

| Claim | Fork status | Evidence | Gap | Value | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| THREE-A01 shared renderer over backends | PARTIAL | `Renderer/ContextFactory.ts:220-306` (WEBGPU→WEBGL attempt plan, `console.warn`, `strictRenderer`), `RendererType.ts:213`; `ARCHITECTURE.md:31-61`. ## 4. Ranked gap list

Ranking: HIGH-value rows first, ordered by (unblocks-others, correctness risk, effort). Each entry names the demonstrating target, the fork's current state, what adoption means here, and the fork rules it binds (SR ids from `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md` §0.2).

**Re-derived 2026-09-03** with PlayCanvas, Babylon.js and Bevy / wgpu / naga in view (appendix A.5–A.7): no rank changed and no gap joined the HIGH list. Each row that a new mapping bears on carries a dated addendum naming the mapping; the MEDIUM list below gained the rows those mappings surfaced. The two mappings that came closest to adding a HIGH gap were [WGPU-TOOL-01](#census-wgpu-tool-01) (no command-stream record/replay) and [NAGA-ARCH-01](#census-naga-arch-01) (no fleet-wide offline WGSL validation); both are tooling with a runner home and no correctness exposure, so they rank MEDIUM under the (unblocks-others, correctness risk, effort) order.

### G1 — Take the 1.145 sync and re-derive the two WGSL twins it invalidates (C01, C15; unlocks C12, C13, C14, C09)

- **Demonstrated by:** CesiumJS 1.145 ([C01](#c01), [C15](#c15)).
- **Fork state:** synced to 1.144 (`06bc894d30`; `CHANGES.md:21`). `R/WebGPUClippingPolygonCollection.ts` + `S/Compute/PolygonSignedDistance.wgsl` implement the pre-1.145 SDF-atlas algorithm with no hole concept (only a doc comment at `Scene/ClippingPolygon.js:160`). `heightReference` is absent from every Buffer* collection, `MVTDataProvider.js` and `GeoJsonPrimitive.js` (0 hits); WebGPU polygon draping is absent even on terrain (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:14093`, Batch 959); polyline draping rides the C11-213 storage-buffer bake with gate F still owed (`FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1013`).
- **Architecturally:** a two-parent merge per the Upstream Sync Procedure, then **one** WGSL re-derivation in the FeatureRenderer lane. **Corrected 2026-09-04 against [`UPSTREAM_SYNC_PLAN_1.145_2026-09-04.md`](UPSTREAM_SYNC_PLAN_1.145_2026-09-04.md) §7.3** (refutation CONFIRMED by lane U's station-3 reviewer). This bullet previously read "two WGSL re-derivations (clipping-polygon holes in the SDF atlas; polygon draping onto the shared classification depth target)" and gated the sync on re-running the reopened `S3` discriminator. Both halves were wrong. **The SDF-atlas remedy is stale:** 1.145 added holes to the **vector** path and **deleted** the SDF one, so the two twins collapse to one — the WGSL twin of `vectorClip`, sharing the polygon tables with the draping row. **The `S3` gate is REFUTED:** `C12-29` S3 is `NEW-ECLIPSE-OCCLUSION-EFFECTS` (`QUEUE_2026-07-19_CAMPAIGN12.md:36`), eclipse occlusion and shadow contrast, and `R-2026-09-02-5` funds its exposure-sweep discriminator — neither is a classification depth target and neither is touched by this sync. `S3` stays live work under its own ruling; it is not a gate here, and a gate that cannot fail for the right reason is worse than no gate.
- **Binds:** SR-2 (both backends — the merge lands WebGL-only by construction, so every merged renderer feature must be filed as a WGSL twin row, not silently carried), SR-4 (any new `ShaderDefine` bit is add-only), SR-3 (merged code keeps upstream comments; fork twins follow C16), SR-6/SR-7 (holes and draping are pixel-verifiable: probe first, capture both backends), SR-5 (no engine landing while the Q-152 / C15-G7 Edge job runs).
- **Also in this row:** port the 1.146 edge vertex-array destroy into the WebGL path (`ModelDrawCommand.js:900` assigns, nothing destroys; the WebGPU emitter already frees at `R/WebGPUEdgeVisibilityEmitter.ts:1519`) — [C13](#c13); locate the post-process selected-features max-texture-size fix ([C14](#c14)); re-check the KHR_gaussian_splatting attribute set against the RC text ([C09](#c09)).

### G2 — Guard the RTE error-free transforms against Metal reassociation and add an Apple-adapter lane (LUMA-G3)

- **Demonstrated by:** luma.gl 9.4 ([LUMA-G3](#luma-g3)) — integer-controlled `twoSum`/`twoProd` selected automatically on Apple adapters because the Metal compiler is permitted to reassociate and fuse.
- **Fork state:** `S/chunks/functions/csm_translateRelativeToEye.wgsl:36-49` computes `highDifference + lowDifference` as plain f32 arithmetic; `S/Compute/ComputeInstanceScaffold.wgsl:99-105` `csm_twoSum` is a plain Knuth two-sum; 0 hits for any contraction guard; no Apple/Safari/macOS lane in `Tools/` (CLAUDE.md fixes Playwright to Edge on Windows; `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1378` mentions Safari only for a worker rAF fallback).
- **Architecturally:** this is the fork's hard 64-bit rule ([WGPU-25](#wgpu-25), `ARCHITECTURE.md:310-317`) being silently voided on one vendor's compiler. The fix shape is a `ShaderDefine` bit (`RTE_INTEGER_EFT` or similar, add-only) selected at device init from the adapter vendor, with the `//>>else` branch keeping today's float path; the WebGL lane is unaffected (GLSL on Metal goes through ANGLE, a different contraction contract — must be measured, not assumed).
- **Binds:** SR-2 (WebGL twin: measure whether ANGLE-on-Metal exhibits the same loss before writing a GLSL guard), SR-4, SR-7 (the spec must assert the low bits survive on a Metal device — a source-text assertion certifies nothing here), SR-8 (precision is the metric: report bits retained, not a pass/fail), SR-6.
- **Risk if ignored:** every Safari and Chrome-on-macOS user of the WebGPU globe may see the FAR-camera L0-tile jitter class the fork fixed at Batch 350 (`FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:516`) reappear, with no probe able to detect it.
- **2026-09-03 addendum:** no newly mapped engine touches Metal reassociation, so the row's premise rests on LUMA-G3 alone. What the three add is context: Babylon re-offsets on the CPU ([BJS-22](#census-bjs-22), HAS — opposite design), Bevy's `big_space` is CPU floating origin with `SHADER_F64` native-only ([GEO-01](#census-geo-01), HAS), and PlayCanvas has no RTE at all ([PC-25](#census-pc-25), NOT-APPLICABLE). All three confirm that GPU-side high/low RTE is the only portable planet-scale route — which is why this row guards it rather than replaces it. Rank unchanged.

### G3 — Populate `ShaderDefine` bits from `navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures` and route `enable` emission through the preprocessor (WGPU-02, WGPU-03, THREE-A04; enabler for WGPU-06/09/10/12/17)

- **Demonstrated by:** the WGSL CRD's feature-detected extension model ([WGPU-02](#wgpu-02)); three.js's per-shader `enable` emission ([THREE-A04](#three-a04)).
- **Fork state:** `R/WebGPUShaderDefines.ts:71-863` has one device-derived bit (`GLOBE_IMAGERY_REDUCED :401`, set from `device.limits` at `R/WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:675-676`); **0 hits** for `wgslLanguageFeatures` in `packages/engine/Source`; `enable` directives are string-prepended per renderer (`R/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:159`, `R/WebGPUPerformanceManager.ts:977`, `R/WebGPUGlobeSurfaceShaders.ts:480`, `R/WebGPUPointCloudLODProcessor.ts:305`) or duplicated into 13 `*_f16.wgsl` twin files; `R/WebGPUPrimitiveIndexUtils.ts:33-36` and `csm_primitiveIndex.wgsl:20-21` treat `primitive_index` as core although the enum now lists an optional `"primitive-index"` feature ([WGPU-03](#wgpu-03)); five new `GPUFeatureName` members are unrequested (`R/WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:41-75`).
- **Architecturally:** a `WebGPULanguageFeatures` probe at device init writes a small set of add-only `ShaderDefineHi` bits (free bits exist, `:942-1117`); `R/WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts` gains an `enable`-emission step keyed on those bits, so extension variants become distinct `(sourceId, defines)` module-cache entries instead of prepends that bypass the cache (`R/WebGPUShaderModuleCache.ts` keys on defines, so a prepend under the same mask is exactly the aliasing class Batch 825 closed at the pipeline level). Every later platform row (immediates, `linear_indexing`, `subgroup_size_control`, `texture_and_sampler_let`, `uniform_buffer_standard_layout`) lands as one bit + one `//>>ifdef` block.
- **Binds:** SR-4 (add-only; the `_f16` twin files stay until each is proven byte-identical to its ifdef form), SR-2 (WebGL has no analogue; this is renderer-internal), SR-7 (`pipeline-key-aliasing.spec.mjs` STRUCTURAL group must extend to the new bits), SR-12 (assert the bits the runtime derives on a real adapter, not an injected feature set).

### G4 — Wire dual-source blending as the single-pass translucent composite (WGPU-13, LUMA-F6)

- **Demonstrated by:** Chrome and now Firefox 155 shipping `dual-source-blending` ([WGPU-13](#wgpu-13)); luma's WBOIT support predicate ([LUMA-F6](#luma-f6)).
- **Fork state:** the feature is requested (`R/WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:55`) and exposed (`R/WebGPUContext.ts:7016`) but `R/WebGPUOIT.ts:9-12` states the dual-source variant is not implemented; 0 hits for `@blend_src`; the MRT WBOIT path is contained OFF (`R/WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:952`, FAR-003) and has no producer (`R/WebGPUSceneRendererTranslucentPass.ts:133-183`); no capability predicate exists on the WebGPU side while `Scene/OIT.js:32` has one for WebGL; `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:815,1323` (WGF-2).
- **Architecturally:** dual-source lets weighted-blended OIT run in one pass with one colour target, sidestepping the multi-frustum MSAA topology FAR-404 plans for the MRT path. It stays WebGPU-additive with the WebGL sorted-blend default as parity (WebGL2 has no dual-source blending — architecturally impossible, the SR-2 exception). The predicate must key on `device.features.has("dual-source-blending")` and on `rgba16float` renderable/blendable.
- **Binds:** SR-1 (off by default until byte-identical-off is proven — the same contract MS-15 states), SR-2 exception documented in the row, SR-6 (translucent scenes captured both backends), SR-7 (inertness mutant on the blend-state selection), SR-4 (an `OIT_DUAL_SOURCE` bit).

### G5 — Close the WebGPU snap gates and port `surfacePosition` (C12)

- **Demonstrated by:** CesiumJS 1.144/1.145 `Scene.snap` ([C12](#c12)).
- **Fork state:** the WebGPU snap tier exists (`R/WebGPUSnapPayload.ts:59` rg32uint payload; `R/WebGPUSnapFramebuffer.ts:29`; `fragmentSnapMain`; `Scene/Scene.js:3647-3648,6089,6120`) and C11-212 is PARTIAL (`QUEUE_2026-07-18_CAMPAIGN11.md:1436-1477`) with owed gates listed at `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1057` (SCENE2D slice depth, moving camera/cursor across DPR/projection, edge `isEdge` on both backends); `surfacePosition` is absent from `Scene/Snapping.js`.
- **Architecturally:** no new design — the row is the Edge certification the fork already owes plus a small API port after G1. It rides the pick-readback lane Q-141 was re-tiered to (R-2026-09-02-2), so it sequences after the readback measurement.
- **Binds:** SR-17 (one Edge job at a time; `--serve-built`), SR-6, SR-2 (`surfacePosition` lands on both backends in the same batch).
- **2026-09-03 addendum:** Babylon is async-only for picking and never had the seam ([BJS-13](#census-bjs-13), HAS); the fork keeps the synchronous `scene.pick()` contract on top of a one-frame-stale readback with a one-shot cold-pick warning — the Batch 1374 / Q-141 class this row already sequences behind. PlayCanvas's v2.21 Windows/NVIDIA splat-pick device hang ([PC-19](#census-pc-19), HAS) has no counterpart record here; a targeted Edge/NVIDIA probe of the splat pick path is the cheap hazard check to fold into the certification leg. Rank unchanged.

### G6 — Finish the WebGPU voxel path to upstream's default shader before adding the additive skip (C11, C10)

- **Demonstrated by:** upstream `VoxelPrimitive` ([C11](#c11)); 3DTILES_content_voxels ([C10](#c10)).
- **Fork state:** WebGPU march preserves `stepSize` (`R/WebGPUVoxelRenderer.ts:229,830-837`), shapes and padding are live (`:308-347`; `R/WebGPUVoxelDataUpload.ts:54,106-107`), but the metadata-typed default CustomShader and GLSL user shaders are unrenderable (`FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:622`; `DEFERRED_WORK.md:9873`), traversal stops at level 2 (C11-100), and C18-A2 empty-space skip is pending on C11-100 (`QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:247,292`).
- **Architecturally:** this is queued work (C11-100 sliced, C18-A2) — the audit adds only the ordering constraint that C18-A2 must default to parity with the fixed-step march (SR-1) and a cross-runtime reference (cesium-native 0.56 renders the same extension) as the oracle for the level-2 cap.
- **Binds:** SR-1, SR-2 (default shader on both backends), SR-13 (the GLSL customShader no-op is missing functionality — the naga hatch, `R/WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts`, is the tracked next piece, not an inline warn).

### G7 — GPU-driven traversal and draw generation for tiles (LUMA-F1, LUMA-F2, WGPU-18/19, C16, C22)

- **Demonstrated by:** luma's `GPUVirtualGeometrySelection` + `GPUSceneDrawGeneration` ([LUMA-F2](#luma-f2), [LUMA-F1](#luma-f1)); three.js's TSL rasterizer ([C22](#c22)); cesium-native's general culling volumes ([C16](#c16)).
- **Fork state:** `R/WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:1-25` SCAFFOLDED, `R/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:36-45` INDIRECT mode zeroes instanceCount with `gpuCullingHint` 'never' (`Scene/Scene.js:559`, FAR-003); the one live GPU-generated indirect draw is point-cloud LOD (`R/WebGPUPointCloudRenderer.ts:2397-2431`); `_drawCountBuffer` is CPU-written and never consumed (`R/WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:236,387`); tile/voxel/splat selection is CPU-side (`R/WebGPUVoxelRenderer.ts:2090-2165`; `ARCHITECTURE.md:816-836` "genuinely unbuilt"); the culler accepts exactly six planes (`R/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:257,338-340`).
- **Architecturally:** this is the meshlet track's Phase M3 (`MS-10` cluster residency + cull + indirect; `MS-11` per-view fanout; `MS-12` Hi-Z) generalised to terrain patches and 3D Tiles, which is exactly what luma's cluster-ID abstraction does. The fork's own plan already confirms the one-indirect-per-slot premise (FAR-502) and the research verdict on software raster (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:4622`). No new track: the audit's contribution is (a) an arbitrary-plane culling-volume input to the culler so the CPU `CullingVolume` and the GPU culler accept the same shape, and (b) re-examining the packed-32-bit visbuffer trade-off in `MS-00` against C22's demonstration before Tier-1 rows are briefed.
- **Binds:** SR-1 (the CPU traversal stays the default; the GPU path is additive and default-off with byte-identical-off), SR-2 (WebGL keeps CPU traversal — WebGPU-only by architectural impossibility, documented), SR-8/SR-9 (multi-metric acceptance on the moving-altitude campaign), the `C11-168` launch gate and M-16.
- **2026-09-03 addendum:** PlayCanvas's indirect-draw slots ([PC-14](#census-pc-14), PARTIAL) carry the same 4×/5×u32 entry layout as `WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts` and add what the fork lacks: 3×u32 compute-dispatch entries and a WebGL2 `WEBGL_multi_draw` / CPU-loop fallback — the "works everywhere" half of the meshlet track, now added to this row's scope. Babylon ([BJS-17](#census-bjs-17), HAS) and Bevy ([BEVY-ARCH-03](#census-bevy-arch-03), [WGPU-PERF-01](#census-wgpu-perf-01), both PARTIAL) confirm the browser bound this row already states: one indirect draw per surviving batch, no GPU-side draw-list compaction. Findegil also found that `WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:5,13` reads as "a single drawIndexedIndirect() … multi-draw-indirect call" while `:255-273` issues one call per draw — a C16 comment-accuracy lead, not a behaviour defect. Rank unchanged.

### G8 — The GPU splat path: compute sort first, residency behind the C18-S7 ruling (LUMA-F4, C07, C23)

- **Demonstrated by:** `@luma.gl/splats` ([LUMA-F4](#luma-f4), PLAUSIBLE); three.js r186's four-pass counting sort ([C23](#c23), secondary source).
- **Fork state:** `R/WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:84-91,139-166` consumes the shared WASM radix-sort permutation (C15-G2..G5 landed, pixel-confirmed at Batch 881; `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:624`); no GPU sort (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:2047`); G6 PARTIAL, G7/G8 PENDING (`QUEUE_2026-08-02_CAMPAIGN15.md:17-23`); C18-S3 GPU radix sort gated post-G8 with GPUSorting (MIT) as the only allowed reference, C18-S7 LOD/streaming awaiting a non-goal ruling (`QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:273,276,280`).
- **Architecturally:** the compute sort is a backend-specific replacement inside the GAUSSIAN_SPLAT feature renderer (WebGL keeps WASM — the Scene Logic Extractor pattern already hoisted the sort above the branch at C15-G2). Counting sort over 4096 depth bins (C23) is simpler than a full radix sort and fits the fork's storage-buffer layout (Option B). Residency/traversal/foveated LOD is the C18-S7 question and stays behind its ruling; nothing here changes the gates.
- **Binds:** SR-16 (C15 queue is the sole authority for G0..G8; Wave S is gated post-G8), C18-S0 licence pass (done; any derivation from luma or three.js needs its own determination), SR-2 (WASM path remains the WebGL and fallback path), SR-6 (sort order is pixel-verifiable via the `sh_unit_cube` baseline).
- **2026-09-03 addendum:** PlayCanvas is now the closest prior art: GPU sort + frustum cull with a scene-wide budget ([PC-10](#census-pc-10), PARTIAL — the fork has f16-packed splat data and an SSE-coupled texture budget, but sort and cull stay CPU/WASM); a PlayCanvas (MIT) compute radix sort with a portable 4-bit path and a vendor (NVIDIA OneSweep) fast path ([PC-11](#census-pc-11), MISSING — the tree has bitonic/scan primitives and subgroup utilities but no radix sort; a `C18-S0` licence-review candidate beside GPUSorting); a published WebGL-vs-WebGPU scaling curve — parity at 1M, 2.6× at 10M, 5.7× at 35M ([PC-13](#census-pc-13), MISSING as a measurement) — that is a ready multi-metric acceptance bar the fork has no instrument for yet; and the removal of its software splat rasterizer ([PC-12](#census-pc-12), §5 item 12). Babylon ([BJS-14](#census-bjs-14), PARTIAL) names the residency half — budget-driven detail apportioning and per-camera LOD — which stays behind the C18-S7 ruling. Rank and gates unchanged.

### G9 — A frame graph that owns pass order and transient lifetime (LUMA-A6, THREE-A09, THREE-A07)

- **Demonstrated by:** luma's `GPUCommandGraph` ([LUMA-A6](#luma-a6)); three.js's `RenderPipeline` fusion ([THREE-A09](#three-a09)).
- **Fork state:** no graph; passes hand-ordered across `R/WebGPUSceneRenderer*.ts`; `R/WebGPUAttachmentDemandRegistry.ts:12-17` observational with `forceSceneMRT` true; 51 `queue.submit(` sites in 34 files; `R/WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:1-45` fixed chain; FAR-400..406 specified, only FAR-405/408 prep landed (`FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md:51-78,714-776`).
- **Architecturally:** already the fork's ADR-4 / FAR-40x plan. luma's ownership rule ("compiled graph owns transients, borrows imports") and its compile-once/encode-per-frame model are the reference for the pick/depth/MRT targets coupled across globe, classification, CSM and TAA; the FAR-408 depth-version defect is the concrete symptom. Ordering: observe-only compiler (FAR-401) before any authority switch.
- **Binds:** SR-1, SR-10 (the observational registry is scaffolding for this — not dead code), SR-8, the DX-wave decomposition rows (a graph cannot land on 5,140-line files; R-2026-09-02-4 released the three renderers to C16-10).
- **2026-09-03 addendum:** two engines have the graph and one removed it. PlayCanvas compiles a `FrameGraph` with adjacent-pass merging ([PC-01](#census-pc-01), MISSING) and builds it before culling so transient resources come from a known graph ([PC-02](#census-pc-02), PARTIAL); Babylon's frame graph adds handle-based texture aliasing ([BJS-05](#census-bjs-05), MISSING; [BJS-06](#census-bjs-06), PARTIAL) and describes itself as not yet complete; Bevy replaced its `RenderGraph` with ordered ECS schedules ([BEVY-ARCH-01](#census-bevy-arch-01), HAS — the fork runs Bevy's shape today). The counter-signal does not demote the row: the pass-count, MRT-demand and transient-lifetime couplings that motivate FAR-40x are real, and the attachment-demand registry is the graph's first input on both PlayCanvas's and Babylon's account. It does set the bar FAR-401's observe-only compiler must clear — a graph has to buy adjacent-pass merging and transient aliasing the ordered chain cannot, or it is not worth its DAG. Rank unchanged.

### G10 — One scene executor over the backend boundary (THREE-A01, THREE-A02)

- **Demonstrated by:** three.js's single `Renderer` over an abstract `Backend` ([THREE-A01](#three-a01), [THREE-A02](#three-a02)).
- **Fork state:** `Scene/Scene.js:457-486` instantiates `WebGPUSceneRenderer` (5,140 lines) as `_alternateSceneRenderer` beside `SceneRenderer.js`; 12 Scene files reference `isWebGPU`; `GraphicsContext.ts:728-1805` covers frame/draw/clear/readback but not pipeline keys, shader generation or compute; ADR-5 (`FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md:215-217`) records the intent.
- **Architecturally:** the fork's own Principle 2 taken to its conclusion. It depends on G9 (a graph gives the shared executor something to schedule), on the DX decomposition, and on FAR-300 semantic packets. Not dispatchable this wave; recorded so the gap list is honest about where the field went.
- **Binds:** SR-1 (no behavioural change on either backend — byte-identical captures on both), SR-2, SR-14/SR-7 at every slice.

- **THREE-A06 / A05 / LUMA-A3:** the dead `getOrCreateBindGroupLayout` cache (`R/WebGPUContext.ts:5615-5629`, 0 callers) should either gain callers (collapsing `pl:` identity) or be removed under SR-10; pipeline refcounting alongside LRU.
- **THREE-A08 / THREE-A07 / LUMA-P2:** cached `reset()` descriptors, one allocation-free submit helper, a byte-equality guard in `R/WebGPUUniformGroupManager.ts:297-315` / `R/WebGPURingBufferAllocator.ts:303-361` — three allocation rows for the moving-altitude campaign, judged under SR-8.
- **WGPU-08:** fix the transient-attachment gate (`R/WebGPUFramebufferManager.ts:54-69` keys on feature names that do not exist; key on the usage constant + a probe) — S effort, real VRAM win on tile-based GPUs.

- **WGPU-20:** state the timestamp quantization floor in `CesiumDebug.gpuPassCost()` output.
- **LUMA-A4 / LUMA-A5:** an app/module binding-range convention for chunks and named injection points for custom shaders (`WGSLShaderBuilder.js:131` `_nextBinding` is unused scaffolding).
- **WGPU-04/05, LUMA-T2:** one compat-mode probe leg (`featureLevel: "compatibility"` is plumbed end-to-end but never run).
- **WGPU-14:** a stable-Firefox (wgpu) lane — needs a non-Playwright driver; the fork's Edge-only rule was about Playwright's Nightly bundle, not about Firefox stable.
- **WGPU-23:** a WebGPU Inspector capture leg banked beside the PNGs.
- **DECK-P4 / WGPU-12:** integer pick attachments (FAR-409) and `primitive_index` per-triangle pick.
- **WGPU-06/07:** immediates for per-tile indices, settled together with the bundle policy.
- **C03:** WGSL stages as entries in the `pipelineStages` traversal.
- **LUMA-G4:** a spike on packed-binary64 exact subtraction for the FAR-camera L0 class.
- **THREE-F03 / WGPU-15 / WGPU-22:** WebXR-on-WebGPU — XL, absent on both backends; per-view `UniformState` is the enabling structure.
- **PC-05:** extend `scheduleTextureDestroy` to a typed `scheduleDestroy(GPUBuffer | GPUQuerySet)` — buffers and query sets are destroyed inline by their owners (`WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:279,982` keeps its own retired set) and `WebGPUEffectsBindGroup.js:483-496` re-implements the queue locally ([PC-05](#census-pc-05)); S effort, the simplification PlayCanvas made across v2.16→v2.19.
- **PC-08 / LUMA-A4:** reflection-derived `@group/@binding` for authored WGSL — the reflection path (`WebGPUBindGroupReflection.ts`) feeds only the compat translator; a real refactor, because the per-renderer bind-group caches key on the explicit numbers ([PC-08](#census-pc-08)).
- **PC-09:** one preprocessor instead of two — the bitmask `//>>ifdef` path is production and the include-capable `WGSLShaderPreprocessor.ts` is used by one shipped `#import` in 324 files ([PC-09](#census-pc-09)); a DX row.
- **PC-18:** world-space PCSS penumbra (hardware depth bias excluded) and VSM as additive CSM rows; splat shadow casting to C15-G / C18-S ([PC-18](#census-pc-18)).
- **PC-21:** extend the model camera arena's dynamic-offset-slice pattern (`WebGPUModelCameraArena.ts`) to the globe and collection camera bind groups; `CesiumDebug.cacheStats()` is the metric ([PC-21](#census-pc-21)).
- **PC-23 / C24:** the `uncapturederror` sentinel plus a `bare` device profile that refuses every optional feature, so the no-f16 / no-timestamp fallbacks can be probed on demand without special hardware ([PC-23](#census-pc-23)).
- **PC-24:** a per-variant minified-size gate and an API-surface diff with an npm runner home — the CLAUDE.md 7.1 / 5.6 / 6.4 MB table has no regression check ([PC-24](#census-pc-24); a Tools spec with a runner home under R-2026-08-29-1).
- **PC-20:** whether a mapped staging ring + copy encoder submitted ahead of the frame beats per-page `queue.writeBuffer` is a measurable question, not a known gap ([PC-20](#census-pc-20)); judge under SR-8.
- **BJS-03:** a state-ordered pipeline-key tree only if `cpuPassCost` ever attributes cost to `generateCacheKey`; not before ([BJS-03](#census-bjs-03)).
- **BJS-16:** guards in `WebGPURenderBundleManager` for the recording-incompatible calls (viewport, scissor, stencil reference, occlusion queries) before a second producer adopts bundles ([BJS-16](#census-bjs-16)).
- **BJS-18:** a public batched `createRenderPipelineAsync(options[])` prewarm entry on `WebGPURenderPipelineCache` ([BJS-18](#census-bjs-18)).
- **BEVY-FEAT-05:** an integrator-facing device-loss policy (recover / stop / ignore) on `WebGPUContext` over the existing `WebGPUDeviceLossRecovery` retry budget; probe-testable ([BEVY-FEAT-05](#census-bevy-feat-05)).
- **NAGA-ARCH-01:** a fleet-wide offline `validate_wgsl` sweep over `Shaders/WebGPU/**/*.wgsl` with an npm runner home — the substrate exists (`WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts:90`, `Tools/shader-pipeline/naga-wasm-tools/`) and a handful of cloud specs already call it; only the runner is missing ([NAGA-ARCH-01](#census-naga-arch-01)).
- **WGPU-TOOL-01:** command-stream record/replay (a JSON draw/compute list replayed in a Playwright harness) for bit-reproducible regression cases — the one tooling gap none of G1–G10 covers ([WGPU-TOOL-01](#census-wgpu-tool-01)).
- **BEVY-FEAT-06:** a fragment-list bounded-sort OIT as the correctness-over-memory alternative to weighted-blended, expressible in WebGPU storage buffers ([BEVY-FEAT-06](#census-bevy-feat-06)); evaluate beside G4, not instead of it.
- **BEVY-FEAT-07 / PC-17:** vignette, lens distortion and CAS sharpening are the only post effects in the new engines' lists the fork lacks; cosmetic, LOW ([BEVY-FEAT-07](#census-bevy-feat-07), [PC-17](#census-pc-17)). WebXR-on-WebGPU is now absent versus three of the seven targets ([PC-16](#census-pc-16), [BJS-21](#census-bjs-21), THREE-F03 above); PlayCanvas's `XrBridge` over device-loss handling is the reference shape when the XL row is ever funded.
