## 5. What NOT to copy, and why

1. **A TSL-style single-source shader layer** ([THREE-A03](#three-a03), [C20](#c20)). It would remove the dual-authoring cost, but it is a C17-scale rewrite of ~110 GLSL and ~100 WGSL files, it conflicts with the ratified template that WebGPU shaders are pre-translated twins, never runtime-generated (`ARCHITECTURE.md:838-870`), and it would void every pixel-parity baseline at once, which the proof bar (R-2026-08-29-1) cannot certify in a wave. NASA 3DTilesRendererJS is paying that migration cost now. Keep Principle 5 plus lockstep spec pairs; adopt only the derived pieces (G3 `enable` emission, binding ranges).
2. **deck.gl's per-viewport common-space rebase** ([DECK-G1](#deck-g1)). It is a weaker precision model than per-vertex high/low RTE and linearises the local projection; a planet-scale globe cannot accept it. The fork's rule stays.
3. **Compatibility Mode as the WebGL replacement** ([WGPU-04](#wgpu-04), [WGPU-05](#wgpu-05)). Compat forbids vertex-stage storage buffers (instances, splats, flow field, skinning all use them), per-attachment blend state (MRT OIT), partial array-layer views (CSM), and `textureLoad` on depth. Principle 1 keeps WebGL working; compat is a probe leg and possibly an AUTO attempt, never a fallback replacement.
4. **The TSL software rasterizer** ([C22](#c22)). The fork's own research verdict (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:4622`) rejected Tier-3 visbuffer/software raster on browser WebGPU for want of 64-bit atomics; three.js demonstrates the packed-32-bit route, which is worth one re-examination inside `MS-00`, not a track.
5. **`instance_index`-only picking** ([DECK-P4](#deck-p4)). Cesium pick identity is per-feature (3D Tiles feature IDs, metadata, drillPick, snap), not per-instance; FAR-409's integer-attachment plan is the right shape. Do not replace the pick-ID contract with a layer-local index.
6. **Per-context encoder + per-pass submit** ([THREE-A07](#three-a07)). The fork already has one frame encoder with a single main submit; three.js's model would multiply submissions. Take only the allocation-free submit helper and fold the 51 side submits, as FAR-402 plans.
7. **Silent WebGPU→WebGL fallback** ([THREE-A01](#three-a01)). `ContextFactory.ts:220-306` warns and offers `strictRenderer`; the Logging doctrine requires the fallback to reach the console. Keep the warning.
8. **Bindless on a Canary flag** ([WGPU-16](#wgpu-16), [WGPU-17](#wgpu-17)). Draft proposal, prototype in Dawn, no ship date; the fork's exposure is a reserved `ShaderDefineHi` bit and `texture_and_sampler_let` groundwork behind G3, not a resource-table implementation.
9. **Reference counting that replaces LRU** ([THREE-A05](#three-a05), [LUMA-A3](#luma-a3)). Refcounts alone leak under the fork's long-lived feature renderers (the WebGL `ShaderCache` needs deferred destruction for the same reason); add refcounts beside the LRU and let the LRU be the backstop.
10. **luma's separate debug entry bundle** ([LUMA-T1](#luma-t1)). The fork's pragma stripping already yields zero-cost debug removal across `.js` and `.ts`, and the build-variant system already has three entry barrels; a fourth debug barrel adds packaging surface for no measured size win.
11. **Mesh shaders in any form** ([WGPU-18](#wgpu-18)). Milestone 4+; `MS-16` is dormant by construction and stays so.
12. **A tile-based compute (software) splat rasterizer** ([PC-12](#census-pc-12)). PlayCanvas built one in v2.18 and removed it in v2.20 in favour of compute projection/sort feeding a hardware raster; the fork never built one, and C15-G / C18-S should not schedule one.
13. **Dual depth peeling as the OIT route** ([BJS-12](#census-bjs-12)). Babylon's is opt-in, beta and costly; the fork's direction is single-pass weighted-blended with dual-source blending (G4). Bevy's per-pixel bounded sort ([BEVY-FEAT-06](#census-bevy-feat-06)) is the alternative to evaluate, not peeling.
14. **A single altitude-driven frustum** ([BJS-23](#census-bjs-23)). Babylon's near = altitude × 0.001 / far = horizon + 10 % R is a simpler comparator, not a replacement for the multi-frustum split plus logarithmic depth on both backends.
15. **Native-only wgpu / Bevy paths** ([WGPU-FEAT-01](#census-wgpu-feat-01) mesh shaders, [BEVY-FEAT-02](#census-bevy-feat-02) 64-bit-atomic virtual geometry, [BEVY-FEAT-03](#census-bevy-feat-03) Solari ray queries, [BEVY-FEAT-04](#census-bevy-feat-04) DLSS, [BEVY-PERF-03](#census-bevy-perf-03) binding arrays). None exists in browser WebGPU; they are reference material for proposed C17 and the meshlet spike, never a track. Extends items 4 and 11.
16. **Dropping the frame graph because Bevy did** ([BEVY-ARCH-01](#census-bevy-arch-01)). Bevy's move to ordered schedules is a bar for FAR-401 to clear (G9 addendum), not a reason to abandon FAR-40x; PlayCanvas and Babylon kept graphs and PlayCanvas merges passes with it.
17. **CPU floating-origin schemes** ([BJS-22](#census-bjs-22), Babylon; [GEO-01](#census-geo-01), Bevy `big_space`). They re-offset static buffers on the CPU whenever the camera moves; the fork's GPU-side high/low RTE keeps planet-scale vertex buffers static. Extends item 2.

## Appendix — claim census

**Revised 2026-09-03 (Erchirion, lane A) from the re-run's joined data.** Source of every count, vote, URL and quote below: `F:/Dev/GH/cesium-webgpu-worker-archive/arch-lenses-2026-09-03/landscape-salvage/landscape-verified-claims.json` (one row per claim in `SALVAGE_TABLE.md` beside it; the raw verifier results are `group-01.json`..`group-04.json` in the same directory — the joined file truncates each vote's reasoning at 600 characters, so the six non-KEPT claims below quote the full text from `group-01.json`). Fork mappings for three.js, luma.gl / deck.gl, the WebGPU platform and CesiumJS upstream are the first pass's (tip `afdf5f4909`, Batch 1381, carried in the JSON's `fork` field and in §3). Fork mappings for PlayCanvas, Babylon.js and Bevy / wgpu / naga were made on 2026-09-03 at HEAD `01226c648a` (Batch 1404) by the three lane-A fork-readers and are reproduced from their banks (`_lane-out/fork-map-playcanvas.md` — Lothíriel; `_lane-out/fork-map-babylon.md` — Ivriniel; `_lane-out/fork-map-bevy.md` — Findegil). Every `file:line` in those three groups was opened by its reader in that session; paths are relative to `packages/engine/Source/` unless the row says otherwise (`Tools/`, `.github/`, `packages/widgets/Source/`).

Vote letters: **C** = CONFIRMED, **P** = PLAUSIBLE, **R** = REFUTED; one letter per verifier vote. Disposition: **KEPT** (every vote C or P), **SPLIT** (votes disagree — listed with both reasonings, not dropped), **REFUTED** (every vote R). Fork status: **HAS** / **PARTIAL** / **MISSING** / **NOT-APPLICABLE** (reason given) / **UNMAPPED** (reason given).

Reading the table: "Fork-mapped" counts claims that carry a fork status — every KEPT claim the readers reached, plus the one SPLIT claim the first pass had already mapped (LUMA-T2, PARTIAL); the two REFUTED and the other three SPLIT claims carry none. The first-pass mappings number 83 (luma 21, WebGPU platform 25, CesiumJS 25, three.js 12); the three 2026-09-03 readers covered 77 claims and mapped 76 (PlayCanvas 25, Babylon.js 24 — the 25th, BJS-11, is REFUTED and listed unmapped, Bevy / wgpu / naga 27); 159 claims therefore carry a fork status — 158 of the 171 KEPT claims plus LUMA-T2 (mapped in the first pass, SPLIT in the re-run); 13 KEPT claims carry none. The 13 three.js claims the first pass verified but never read against the fork (THREE-F04..F07, THREE-G01..G03, THREE-P01..P04, THREE-T01, THREE-T02) are listed in A.1 with their sources and stay unmapped; that is the one residual mapping gap and it is named here, not filled.

Continuity with the first pass: the 83 claims the 2026-09-02 document anchored in §2 (its census table said 84 — the luma row was written as 22 where the body carries 21 anchors, so the table was off by one; nothing was lost) are all present below under the same ids, each linked back to its §2 anchor. Their first-pass CONFIRMED / PLAUSIBLE marks in §2 are superseded by the vote column here.

### A.1 three.js `WebGPURenderer` + TSL

25 claims: 25 KEPT, 0 REFUTED, 0 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 2 / PARTIAL 7 / MISSING 3 / NOT-APPLICABLE 0 / not mapped in the first pass 13.

<a id="census-three-a01"></a>**THREE-A01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — WebGPURenderer is one shared renderer that targets pluggable backends: it uses a WebGPU backend by default and silently falls back to a WebGL 2 backend; forceWebGL pins the WebGL 2 backend for testing or exclusion. Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/WebGPURenderer.html> — "By default, the renderer tries to use a WebGPU backend if the browser supports WebGPU. If not, `WebGPURenderer` falls backs to a WebGL 2 backend."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A01](#three-a01)):** PARTIAL — packages/engine/Source/Renderer/ContextFactory.ts:220-306 (attempt plan WEBGPU->WEBGL fallback with console.warn, `strictRenderer: true` removes the fallback, `renderer: RendererType.WEBGL` pins WebGL per RendererType.ts:213); migration_doc/ARCHITECTURE.md:31-61 (single abstract GraphicsContext, zero Renderer/WebGPU imports in Scene). BUT Scene.js:457-486 instantiates a second scene executor `_alternateSceneRenderer` = WebGPUSceneRenderer (5,140 lines) via FeatureRendererKey.SCENE_RENDERER while WebGL runs SceneRenderer.js; 12 Scene files still reference isWebGPU; FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN ADR-5 (line 215-217) states 'two divergent live schedulers are not retained' as a planned, not landed, decision. **Gap:** Backend selection and fallback match three.js, but the fork keeps two live scene-side executors (SceneRenderer.js vs WebGPUSceneRenderer) instead of one shared Renderer over a Backend interface, so divergence lives above the context boundary rather than below it. (value HIGH, effort XL).

<a id="census-three-a02"></a>**THREE-A02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — All backend-specific behaviour is confined to an abstract Backend base class (beginRender/finishRender, draw, compute, createRenderPipeline, createBindings, getRenderCacheKey, createNodeBuilder, isOccluded, ...); the common Renderer holds the rendering logic and derived WebGPUBackend/WebGLBackend implement the interface. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/common/Backend.js> — "This abstract base class defines an interface that encapsulates all backend-related logic. Derived classes for each backend must implement the interface."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A02](#three-a02)):** PARTIAL — packages/engine/Source/Renderer/GraphicsContext.ts:728-1805 declares 47 abstract members (beginFrame/endFrame 1421-1422, clear 1433, draw 1440, readPixels 1748, createViewportQuadCommand 1765, createSync 1789, destroy 1805) and verifies every subclass implements them at construction (837-894); the only shader/pipeline hook is `abstract get shaderCache()` at 1412 — no getRenderCacheKey/createNodeBuilder/compute equivalents; pipeline-key policy lives WebGPU-only in WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:949 and compute in WebGPUComputeEngine.ts vs Renderer/ComputeEngine.js. **Gap:** The abstract API covers frame/draw/clear/readback lifecycle but not pipeline-cache keys, shader generation or compute, so caching policy and shader assembly are duplicated per backend instead of shared with backend-owned hooks. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-three-a03"></a>**THREE-A03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Shaders are authored once as a TSL node graph in JavaScript and compiled by NodeBuilder subclasses into WGSL (WGSLNodeBuilder, WebGPU) or GLSL (GLSLNodeBuilder, WebGL 2), so there is no dual hand-written GLSL/WGSL source and no textual preprocessor/defines layer. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/wiki/Three.js-Shading-Language> — "Currently `NodeBuilder` has two extended classes, the `WGSLNodeBuilder` aimed at WebGPU and `GLSLNodeBuilder` aimed at WebGL2."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A03](#three-a03)):** MISSING — Parallel hand-written trees: packages/engine/Source/Shaders/*.glsl (68 top-level + Builtin/Model subtrees) and packages/engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/** (42 top-level + Compute/PostProcess/Model/chunks) with `//>>ifdef` ShaderDefine preprocessing (WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts); WGSLShaderBuilder.js:1-12 is a string-assembly builder, not a node graph; WebGPUShaderTranslator.ts:1-20 and WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts:1-12 are runtime GLSL->WGSL for the compat stub only and tagged EXPERIMENTAL (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:967-969); ARCHITECTURE.md:838-870 codifies 'pre-translated WGSL twin ... never runtime GLSL translation' as the template. **Gap:** Every shader feature is authored twice (GLSL + WGSL twin) and kept in sync by Principle 5 and the C16 pass; there is no single-source node graph compiled to both targets. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-three-a04"></a>**THREE-A04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — WGSLNodeBuilder emits not just shader text but the bind-group bindings and vertex-buffer layouts the pipeline needs, and it enables WGSL feature directives (subgroups, subgroups-f16, f16, clip_distances, dual_source_blending) per shader by emitting `enable <directive>;` lines only when a node requests them. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/nodes/WGSLNodeBuilder.js> — "A node builder targeting WGSL. This module generates WGSL shader code from node materials and also generates the respective bindings and vertex buffer definitions."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A04](#three-a04)):** PARTIAL — Layout derivation from shader exists only on the compat path: WebGPUBindGroupReflection.ts:1-27 builds GPUBindGroupLayoutDescriptors from naga reflection but is imported solely by WebGLCompatibilityStub.ts and WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts; native renderers hand-declare layouts (124 `arrayStride` vertex-layout sites, 28 raw createBindGroupLayout + 152 makeBindGroupLayout sites under Renderer/WebGPU, e.g. WebGPUGlobeSurfaceLayouts.ts:74). `enable` directives are prepended ad hoc per renderer: WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:159 and WebGPUPerformanceManager.ts:977 (`enable subgroups;`), WebGPUGlobeSurfaceShaders.ts:480 (`enable clip_distances;`), WebGPUF16Utils.ts:180 and 13 separate `*_f16.wgsl` twin files (`enable f16;`); WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts has no enable-emission. **Gap:** Bind-group and vertex layouts are hand-maintained beside each WGSL file (binding-index drift remains possible) and feature `enable` lines are string-prepended per renderer or duplicated into _f16 twin files rather than emitted centrally when a shader requests them. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-three-a05"></a>**THREE-A05** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Render pipelines are cached under a key built from the vertex-program id, fragment-program id and the backend's render cache key; shader programs are reused across pipelines, and pipelines are reference-counted and released when usedTimes drops to zero. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/common/Pipelines.js> — "stageVertex.id + ',' + stageFragment.id + ',' + this.backend.getRenderCacheKey()"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A05](#three-a05)):** PARTIAL — WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:949-1103 generateCacheKey folds `sh:<vsId>.<entry>/<fsId>.<entry>` module identity plus pl:/pr:/dz:/mx: (WeakMap identity at line 41); WebGPUShaderModuleCache.ts:1-30 dedupes modules per (sourceId, defines) so programs are reused across pipelines; lifetime is an LRU cap (`touch` 512-520, `evictIfNeeded` 526-536, `stats.evicted`) — grep for refCount/usedTimes/release across WebGPURenderPipelineCache, WebGPUModelPipelineCache and WebGPUComputePipelineCache returns 0. **Gap:** Keys and program reuse match three.js, but pipelines are released by LRU pressure rather than reference counting, so unused variants linger until the cap evicts them and eviction can drop a still-referenced pipeline. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-three-a06"></a>**THREE-A06** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — WebGPU bind-group layouts are deduplicated through a cache keyed on the combination of layout entries, and bind groups are created against the cached GPUBindGroupLayout. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/utils/WebGPUBindingUtils.js> — "A cache that maps combinations of layout entries to existing bind group layouts."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A06](#three-a06)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUContext.ts:5615-5629 `getOrCreateBindGroupLayout` dedupes by JSON.stringify(descriptor) into `_bindGroupLayoutCache` (declared 699) — but a repo grep finds zero callers outside its own definition and the ResourceManager twin; 28 raw `device.createBindGroupLayout(` + 152 `makeBindGroupLayout(` sites create per-renderer layouts (WebGPUGlobeSurfaceLayouts.ts:74, WebGPUModelPipelineCache.ts:608); only the effects BGL is device-shared (WebGPUEffectsBindGroup.js:410 'immutable placeholders and layouts are device-shared'); WebGPUBindGroupCache.ts:19-25 caches bind groups, not layouts. **Gap:** A layout-level dedupe cache exists but is dead (no callers), so identical entry sets across globe, model and post-process pipelines produce distinct GPUBindGroupLayouts and `pl:` pipeline-layout identity never collapses. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-three-a07"></a>**THREE-A07** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Command encoding is per render context and per compute group: beginRender creates a GPUCommandEncoder for the render context, finishRender finishes and submits it, and submission goes through a helper that reuses a module-scoped single-element array to avoid per-submit allocations (r185 'Optimize submit() calls', #33513). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mrdoob/three.js/dev/src/renderers/webgpu/utils/WebGPUUtils.js> — "Submits a single GPU command to the device queue using a shared, module-scoped array to avoid per-call array allocations."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A07](#three-a07)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUContext.ts:3364-3369 finishes one frame encoder (`_currentCommandEncoder`) and submits it once in endFrame, preceded by a separate `_submitPendingTextureMipJobs` submit (3203-3210); WebGPUFrameCommandEncoder.ts:31-52 lets feature renderers join that encoder; but 51 `queue.submit(` sites across 25 files and 47 `createCommandEncoder(` sites remain (FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md:72,84: '52 direct queue.submit call sites ... remain'), every submit allocates a fresh `[commandBuffer]` literal, and ADR-4 / FAR-402 ('one normal-frame encoder/submit') is planned XL work. **Gap:** The fork already has a per-frame encoder with a single main submit, but side submits are scattered across 25 files with no shared allocation-free submit helper and no single physical-queue authority. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-three-a08"></a>**THREE-A08** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — r185 introduced dedicated descriptor classes for WebGPU descriptors (render pipeline, pipeline layout, command encoder, render-bundle encoder, etc.) that are cached and reset() between uses to cut per-frame object allocation and GC overhead. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33525> — "caches the descriptor instances whenever possible to reduce per frame object allocation and GC overhead."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A08](#three-a08)):** MISSING — WebGPURenderTarget.ts:565-571 `renderPassDescriptor` and 599-615 `getLoadPassDescriptor` build fresh object literals per call; 34 inline `beginRenderPass({` literals and 180 inline `createBindGroup({` literals under Renderer/WebGPU; the only descriptor `reset()` is WebGPUPipelineDescriptorBuilder.ts:330, which has 4 construction sites at init time; grep for scratch/cached render-pass descriptor reuse returns nothing; FAR-001 (remediation plan line 58) only adds allocation telemetry. **Gap:** Render-pass, bind-group and pipeline descriptors are allocated as fresh literals every frame with no reusable reset() descriptor objects, so the moving-altitude allocation metric carries avoidable GC churn. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-three-a09"></a>**THREE-A09** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — Post-processing is a node graph: RenderPipeline (renamed from PostProcessing in r183) takes an outputNode chain of TSL effect nodes, has built-in MRT support, and automatically combines passes into fewer materials; EffectComposer is not supported on WebGPURenderer. Source: <https://threejs.org/manual/en/webgpurenderer.html> — "`WebGPURenderer` comes with a new post-processing stack with built-in Multiple Render Targets (MRT) support and automatic pass combination thanks to the node material."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-A09](#three-a09)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:1-45 documents a fixed ordered ping-pong stage list (AerialPerspective->AO->Bloom/GodRays->DoF->AutoExposure->TAA->Tonemap->user/library stages->ColorGrading->custom->FXAA) that must always run to blit the canvas; no fusion/merge logic (grep on fuse/combine/graph in the three post-process files hits nothing); MRT exists as the scene G-buffer but WebGPUAttachmentDemandRegistry.ts:13-17 is 'currently observational' with `forceSceneMRT` defaulting true; ARCHITECTURE.md:838-870 library stages are pre-translated WGSL twins; FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN ADR-4 (211) and FAR-402/FAR-403 (frame graph, conditional G-buffer/exact MRT variants) are planned. **Gap:** Post-processing is a hard-coded stage chain rather than an effect node graph, so there is no automatic pass fusion and MRT attachments are always allocated instead of demanded by the effect graph. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-three-f01"></a>**THREE-F01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — r185 (2026-07-01) added ClusteredLighting, a Forward+ clustered lighting system that partitions the view frustum into screen tiles x exponential depth slices and uses a compute pass to build per-cluster light lists (defaults: 32px tiles, 24 slices, 64 lights/cluster, 1024 lights); TiledLighting was removed in the same release (#33751) to concentrate on it. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33406> — "partitions the view frustum into a 3D cluster grid (screen tiles × exponential depth slices)"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-F01](#three-f01)):** HAS — WebGPUClusterBoundsRenderer.ts:27-29 (CLUSTER_TILE_COUNT_X=16, Y=9, CLUSTER_SLICE_COUNT_Z=24) and WebGPUClusterAssignRenderer.ts:37-41 (CLUSTER_MAX_LIGHTS=1024, 256 per cluster) drive compute bounds+assign passes orchestrated by WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts:1-22 and WebGPUSceneRendererClusteredLighting.ts:1-25; consumed by Shaders/WebGPU/chunks/structs/ClusteredLighting.wgsl in ModelPBRComplete.wgsl and all 19 Lit Mat primitive shaders (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:632, SHIPPED Batches 153-155) plus LTC area lights (row 633); Scene.js:3386-3407 `scene.clusteredLightingEnabled` (default false, 'WebGL ignores this option'). **Gap:** Forward+ clustering is live on WebGPU (screen-tile x depth-slice grid rather than pixel-sized tiles), but it is opt-in, has no WebGL counterpart (Principle 5 parity gap), and the globe/terrain and Phong primitive shaders do not consume the cluster list. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-three-f02"></a>**THREE-F02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — r184 (2026-04-16) added DynamicLighting (#33042), which batches analytic lights into uniform arrays so that adding or removing lights no longer forces material recompilation (defaults 8 directional / 16 point / 16 spot / 4 hemisphere). Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/DynamicLighting.html> — "A custom lighting implementation that batches supported analytic lights into uniform arrays so light count changes do not recompile materials."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-F02](#three-f02)):** HAS — WebGL: AutomaticUniforms.js:1370-1413 `czm_lightCount` + `czm_lightsData[41]` fixed uniform array (header + up to 8 lights x 5 vec4) consumed at Shaders/Model/LightingStageFS.glsl:151 with a runtime `int(czm_lightCount)` loop; LightTypes.ts:431/485 MAX_LIGHTS=8 enforced at add(); UniformState.js:229-232 packs into a fixed Float32Array. WebGPU: ClusteredLighting.wgsl:73/285 gates on the `activeLightCount` uniform, and WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts:17-20 keeps 'resources and effects layout fixed [to avoid] pipeline variants when lighting is toggled'; WebGPUShaderDefines.ts has no light-count bit. **Gap:** Both backends already use fixed-capacity light buffers with a runtime count (no recompile on add/remove); the remaining difference is capacity (8 total lights on WebGL vs three.js's per-type 8/16/16/4 split and no hemisphere light type). (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-three-f03"></a>**THREE-F03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — r185 restored WebXR on the WebGPU backend (#33583, funded by Meta) — before it only the WebGL backend supported WebXR; XRManager exposes getWebGPUBinding() and the renderer has a multiview option for WebXR rendering. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33583> — "This PR brings the implementation back. Currently only WebGL is supported for WebXR."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [THREE-F03](#three-f03)):** MISSING — grep for webxr/XRSession/navigator.xr/XRGPUBinding across packages/engine/Source returns 0 hits; the only VR surface is upstream's side-by-side stereo `Scene.useWebVR` (Scene.js:2903-2927) and packages/widgets/Source/VRButton; WebGPUContext.ts:1956-1960 overrides `supportsStereoViewport` to false so `useWebVR` fails loudly on WebGPU; DEFERRED_WORK.md:10767-10772 NEW-WEBGPU-STEREO-VIEWPORT 'OPEN / UNSUPPORTED ... does not implement WebGPU stereo'; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1366-1371 lists per-eye viewport/stereo as deferred. **Gap:** The fork has no WebXR session or XRGPUBinding path on either backend and WebGPU cannot even honour the legacy per-eye stereo viewport, so any XR/multiview row starts from zero. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-three-f04"></a>**THREE-F04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Native Gaussian splatting landed on the dev branch after r185 (PR #33950 merged 2026-08-08, not yet in a release): GaussianSplat renders instanced quads from 3D covariance projected through the perspective Jacobian, loads PLY/.splat/.spz/.ksplat and an opt-in KHR_gaussian_splatting glTF extension, sorts with a CountingSort class, and is written in TSL so it runs on both backends of WebGPURenderer. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33950> — "All of this is written in TSL, so it runs unmodified on both the WebGPU and WebGL backends."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-f05"></a>**THREE-F05** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — r184 added TAAUNode (#33368), temporal anti-aliasing with upsampling that renders beauty/depth/velocity at a lower resolution and reconstructs output resolution with a 9-tap Blackman-Harris filter, plus an FSR 1 port (#33339, webgpu_upscaling_fsr1); MSAA must be off when TAAU is used. Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/TAAUNode.html> — "The result is an alternative to FSR2/3 that does anti-aliasing and upscaling in a single pass."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-f06"></a>**THREE-F06** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — GPU-driven rendering is exposed through IndirectStorageBufferAttribute + BufferGeometry.setIndirect(): a compute shader writes draw parameters, and passing an array of offsets issues multiple indirect draw calls from one buffer; indirect compute dispatch (dispatchWorkgroupsIndirect, r181 #31488) lets the GPU size later dispatches. WebGPU backend only. Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/BufferGeometry.html> — "If an array is provided, multiple indirect draw calls will be made for each offset."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-f07"></a>**THREE-F07** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Render bundles are exposed as BundleGroup (static by default; needsUpdate forces re-record) and since r181 (#31906) a bundle can render to multiple render targets — previously WebGPU rejected it because the bundle encoder declared only one colour attachment. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/31906> — "Attachment state of [RenderPipeline] is not compatible with [RenderBundleEncoder]"
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-g01"></a>**THREE-G01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — WebGPURenderer supports a reversed depth buffer (r183 #32967, basic rendering + shadow maps) and since r184 uses depth32float instead of depth24plus when reversedDepthBuffer is on; a logarithmicDepthBuffer option also exists on the renderer. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/releases/tag/r184> — "Use `depth32float` instead of `depth24plus` when using `reversedDepthBuffer`. [#33184]"
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-p01"></a>**THREE-P01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — r184 made compileAsync() truly non-blocking (#32984): NodeBuilder.buildAsync() yields between shader stages and materials are compiled sequentially with yields, and WebGPUPipelineUtils uses device.createRenderPipelineAsync when a promises array is supplied. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/32984> — "Add `NodeBuilder.buildAsync()` that yields between shader stages."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-p02"></a>**THREE-P02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — r184 sped up TSL shader generation ~3x (#33120): on webgpu_loader_materialx compile time fell from ~1550 ms to ~515 ms, mostly by caching getNodeType() and hashing nodes by id. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33120> — "TSL: Improves compilation performance by 3.0x"
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-p03"></a>**THREE-P03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — GPU timing is built in: a backend trackTimestamp option enables the Timestamp Query API, WebGPUTimestampQueryPool allocates query pairs per render/compute context and resolveQueriesAsync returns the total duration, surfaced via renderer.info.render.timestamp / compute.timestamp. Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/WebGPUTimestampQueryPool.html> — "Manages a pool of WebGPU timestamp queries for performance measurement. Extends the base TimestampQueryPool to provide WebGPU-specific implementation."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-p04"></a>**THREE-P04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — r184 added ReadbackBuffer (#33300) so getArrayBufferAsync() reuses caller-owned GPU/CPU readback buffers instead of allocating a new typed array per readback, addressing GC pressure on large compute readbacks. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33300> — "Introduce `new ReadbackBuffer(attribute)` for use together with `Renderer.getArrayBufferAsync()`"
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-t01"></a>**THREE-T01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — r181 introduced an optional Inspector addon (renderer.inspector = new Inspector()) that monitors render/compute calls, logs and parameters with GPU timings, built on an InspectorBase hook interface (begin/finish, beginRender/finishRender, beginCompute/finishCompute, texture copies) so custom inspectors can be plugged in. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/31869> — "can monitor render calls, logs, parameters, and in the future memory usage and nodes as well."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-t02"></a>**THREE-T02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — r185 surfaces GPU errors properly (#33418): device.onuncapturederror is routed to a renderer.onError callback (mirroring onDeviceLost), pipeline creation is wrapped in validation error scopes, and on failure GPUShaderModule.getCompilationInfo() is used to report WGSL diagnostics with line numbers instead of an opaque invalid-module message. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33418> — "When a WebGPU render or compute pipeline fails today, three.js logs an opaque `[Invalid ShaderModule "fragment"] is invalid`, no line number, no hint at the real WGSL mistake."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-g02"></a>**THREE-G02** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; geospatial) — Three.js has no relative-to-eye / high-low position split; its large-coordinate answer is renderer.highPrecision (r176 #30970), which computes model-view and normal-view matrices on the CPU in 64-bit, extended in r184 (#33246) to premultiply the shadow matrix on the CPU; it is incompatible with InstancedMesh and SkinnedMesh. Source: <https://threejs.org/docs/pages/Renderer.html> — "When enabled, will use CPU 64-bit precision for higher precision instead of GPU 32-bit for higher performance. NOTE: 64-bit precision is not compatible with `InstancedMesh` and `SkinnedMesh`."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

<a id="census-three-g03"></a>**THREE-G03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — WebGPURenderer does not support ShaderMaterial, RawShaderMaterial or onBeforeCompile; geospatial stacks built on three.js (e.g. NASA-AMMOS 3DTilesRendererJS, issue #1380: 'ImageOverlayPlugin is not WebGPU-ready') must rewrite custom-material plugins in TSL to run on the WebGPU backend. Source: <https://threejs.org/manual/en/webgpurenderer.html> — "Custom materials based on `ShaderMaterial`, `RawShaderMaterial` and modifications of built-in materials via `onBeforeCompile()` are not supported in `WebGPURenderer`."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).

### A.2 luma.gl 9 / deck.gl 9

25 claims: 20 KEPT, 1 REFUTED, 4 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 2 / PARTIAL 13 / MISSING 6 / NOT-APPLICABLE 0 / not mapped in the first pass 4.

<a id="census-luma-a1"></a>**LUMA-A1** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — luma.gl's command model is deferred on WebGPU (CommandEncoder -> finish() -> CommandBuffer -> submit()) but the same API shape on WebGL is effectively immediate; a single default encoder is used implicitly unless the app creates one to get a visible submission boundary. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-guide/gpu/gpu-commands.md> — "On WebGPU, command encoding is truly deferred. Commands are recorded onto a specific CommandEncoder, finish() seals that encoder into a CommandBuffer, and submit() sends the finished work to the queue. On WebGL, luma.gl preserves the same API shape where practical, but rendering remains effectively immediate."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-A1](#luma-a1)):** HAS — GraphicsContext.ts:1421-1422 declares abstract beginFrame()/endFrame() (verified at :847-848). Context.js:1358-1362: WebGL beginFrame is a no-op and endFrame only resets GL state (immediate). WebGPUContext.ts:2246 creates the frame GPUCommandEncoder in beginFrame, :3365-3366 finish()+queue.submit() in endFrame; WebGPUFrameCommandEncoder.ts:31-52 hands feature renderers the frame encoder (null while a render pass is open) so compute joins the same submission. ARCHITECTURE.md §4 (:269-298) RenderCommand deferred/immediate modes. **Gap:** The boundary is exposed but not yet a single authority: grep finds 50 direct `queue.submit(` sites in 34 Renderer/WebGPU files (FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN §1.3 records 52), which FAR-402/FAR-406 (plan :732-775) are to fold into one normal-frame submit. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-luma-a2"></a>**LUMA-A2** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — In 9.4 all draw state (pipeline, bindings, vertex arrays, direct draws, indirect draws, render bundles) moved from RenderPipeline onto RenderPass; the old RenderPipeline draw/bind APIs are deprecated compatibility paths. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "`RenderPass` now owns pipeline, binding, vertex-array, direct-draw, indirect-draw, and render-bundle commands. The former `RenderPipeline` draw and binding APIs remain as deprecated compatibility paths."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-A2](#luma-a2)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUDrawCommand.ts owns pipeline/bindGroups/vertex+index buffers (:144-160), `drawIndirectBuffer`/`drawIndirectOffset` (:206-208), `bundle` (:326) and executes against a GPURenderPassEncoder: executeBundles (:605-613), drawIndexedIndirect/drawIndirect (:682-691). Live indirect consumer: WebGPUPointCloudRenderer.ts:2413-2431 (LOD compute writes instanceCount, above POINT_COUNT_LOD_THRESHOLD=50_000 at :858). DrawCommand.js has no indirect field (grep none) and RenderCommand.js:1-40 carries no indirect/bundle option. **Gap:** Indirect draws and bundles are first-class only on the WebGPU-side command, not on the backend-neutral DrawCommand/RenderCommand contract or a pass-owned execution object; FAR-300 semantic packets and FAR-502 (plan :605-613, :827-833) are the planned home. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-luma-a3"></a>**LUMA-A3** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — PipelineFactory caches render and compute pipelines per device, keyed by a hash of pipeline inputs plus device type (not object identity), with reference counting and a device-level policy that retains zero-refcount pipelines by default. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/pipeline-factory.md> — "When the reference count reaches zero, the pipeline is either destroyed or retained depending on `device.props._destroyPipelines`."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-A3](#luma-a3)):** PARTIAL — WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:949-1060 generateCacheKey hashes name, variant fields, multisample, depth format, per-target format/writeMask/blend, vertex layouts, layout id and `sh:` shader-module identity via WeakMap webgpuObjectIdentity (:41-60); one cache per device/context (constructor :470-484); LRU cap DEFAULT_MAX_SIZE=1024 (:428), evictIfNeeded (:526-535), wrongModuleHits canary (:355). WebGPUComputePipelineCache.ts:409-417 folds `m:` module id. WebGPUShaderModuleCache.ts:1-96 keys strictly on (sourceId, defines, definesHi, keySalt) with the label passed separately, so a debug label never forks an entry. grep refCount/retain across Renderer/WebGPU hits only device pool/texture/buffer files, none in the pipeline caches. **Gap:** No reference counting, release(), or zero-refcount retention policy on pipelines; lifetime is LRU-only and a hot pipeline can be evicted under a burst of variants. (value LOW, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-a4"></a>**LUMA-A4** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — WGSL shaders are assembled as ONE unified source containing all entry points; modules declare `@binding(auto)` which shadertools rewrites to concrete numbers before reflection/compile, with application-owned group-0 bindings allocated from 0 and module-owned bindings from 100+. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/shadertools/wgsl-support.md> — "Shadertools rewrites `@binding(auto)` to concrete binding numbers before the final WGSL is reflected and compiled by WebGPU."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-A4](#luma-a4)):** MISSING — Bindings are hand-numbered per shader: ModelPBRComplete.wgsl:574 `@group(1) @binding(36) var<uniform> sh`; chunks/structs/ClusteredLighting.wgsl:108-116 hardcodes bindings 18-25 under a `__CL_GROUP__` token that WebGPUClusteredLightingBGL.ts:48-66 string-substitutes, with the comment 'Effects bindings 0..17 are claimed ... clustered-lighting resources start at 18' (:32-36). WGSLShaderBuilder.js:312-395 requires explicit group/binding numbers; its `_nextBinding` map (:131) is never consumed, and its only importer WebGPUAutoUniforms.js has no consumers. WebGPUBindGroupReflection.ts:1-30 derives BGL descriptors from naga reflection but does not rewrite bindings. grep `binding(auto` across Renderer/WebGPU + Shaders/WebGPU: none. **Gap:** No `@binding(auto)` rewriter and no app-range/module-range binding convention; every shared chunk's slots are coordinated by hand against each consumer's layout. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-a5"></a>**LUMA-A5** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + CONFIRMED; architecture) — 9.4 adds WGSL hook functions and standard named injections (vs:#main-start, fs:#main-end) in the assembler, plus a ShaderPlugin unit that groups modules, defines, injections, caller vertex inputs and generated cross-stage varyings. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "`ShaderAssembler` now applies registered hook functions and standard named injections such as `vs:#main-start` and `fs:#main-end` while assembling unified WGSL shaders."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-A5](#luma-a5)):** PARTIAL — Two preprocessors, both boolean: WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts:1-80 `//>>ifdef/else/endif` over ShaderDefine bits (unknown flags throw); WGSLShaderPreprocessor.ts:759-855 `#define/#ifdef/#ifndef/#else/#endif` on symbol presence only (a `MAX_LIGHTS 4` value becomes a WGSL const, :349, never evaluated in a condition). Capability gating is done by host regex stripping, e.g. WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:153-162 `__SUBGROUP_BLOCK_START__..END__`, not by defines (WebGPUShaderDefines.ts:71-863 lists 31 bits, no SUPPORTS_*/subgroup/f16 entries; f16 is a separate Tonemapping_f16.wgsl). User hooks exist as whole-stage native WGSL bodies: model MODEL_HAS_WGSL_CUSTOM_SHADER bit 23 / MODEL_HAS_WGSL_CUSTOM_VERTEX bit 24 (WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:777-778, 3403-3413) and voxel WebGPUVoxelCustomShaderCodegen.ts:1-45; WebGPUOIT.injectOITOutput (:431-460) is an ad-hoc source injection. **Gap:** No named injection points (vs:#main-start style), no numeric/boolean `#if`, no capability defines resolved before binding assignment, and no ShaderPlugin-style unit bundling modules+defines+injections+varyings; GLSL-only customShaders are not transpiled on WebGPU. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-a6"></a>**LUMA-A6** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — The experimental GPUCommandGraph is a frame-graph-style abstraction: fixed-capacity buffer/texture resources plus ordered compute/render/copy nodes; declared resource uses drive both ordering and transient lifetime; compile once, then encode() each frame into the caller's CommandEncoder (it is not a second submission queue). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/gpu-core/gpu-command-graph.md> — "GPUCommandGraph<Parameters> declares fixed-capacity WebGPU buffer and texture resources plus ordered compute, render, and copy nodes."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-A6](#luma-a6)):** MISSING — grep FrameGraph/RenderGraph/CommandGraph across Renderer/WebGPU and Scene finds only WebGPUAttachmentDemandRegistry.ts:10,86 ('can also feed a render graph', 'for a future render graph'); that record is explicitly observational (:12-17 'currently observational ... forceSceneMRT defaults to true'). Passes are hand-ordered across WebGPUSceneRenderer*.ts (FrustumLoop, GlobePass, TranslucentPass, PostFrustumChain, PickPass). FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md FAR-400..FAR-406 (:714-776) specify the schema, observe-only compiler, graph-owned transients and single-authority switch as planned work; §1.2 (:51-78) lists only FAR-405/408 'preparation slices' (empty-pass removal, view caching) as landed. **Gap:** No frame-graph abstraction owns pass ordering or transient lifetime; the pick/depth/MRT targets shared by globe, classification, CSM and TAA are coupled by hand and the depth-version defect noted under FAR-408 remains. (value HIGH, effort XL).

<a id="census-luma-a7"></a>**LUMA-A7** (**SPLIT**; REFUTED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — WebGPU device creation exposes feature levels: 'core' (portable default), 'max' (all adapter features + max limits), 'compatibility', and 'best-available'; the effective level is reported on device.info, and device.limits reports storage-buffer/storage-texture availability separately per vertex and fragment stage. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "`DeviceProps.featureLevel` can now request `'core'`, the portable WebGPU default; `'max'`, which requests every adapter feature and supported limit; `'compatibility'`; or `'best-available'`, which upgrades compatibility to core when available. The effective level is reported as `device.info.featureLevel`."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).
**Verifier reasoning (SPLIT):**
- Vote 1, REFUTED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md (two fetches returned contradictory content, neither reliably confirming the claimed featureLevel text)): Two separate fetches of the same cited URL (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md) returned mutually contradictory content. The first fetch, when prompted specifically about featureLevel/core/max/compatibility/best-available, returned text matching the claim almost verbatim - a strong signature of an LLM summarizer confabulating content to satisfy the prompt rather than reporting what's actually on the page. The second fetch, asked for raw verbatim content, returned an entirely different document (a "v10.0 Q3 2026" release summary with modules like @luma.gl/tables, @luma.gl/arrow, @luma.gl/splats, mentioning only "v9.4: WebGPU render bundles, dynamic buffers, video textures, advanced screen-space effects" with no mention whatsoever of featureLevel, device.info, or per-stage storage limits). Since the two fetches of the identical URL are irreconcilable, at least one (likely both, given the outlandish "AI-assisted development" v10.0 content and 2026 Q3 dating) is a hallucination rather than real page content. No reliable quote from the actual source could be obtained supporting the claim. Given the instruction to default to REFUTED when unable to verify, and that the only real signal (second fetch) shows no such content, this claim is REFUTED.
- Vote 2, CONFIRMED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md): The cited page (docs/whats-new.md on visgl/luma.gl master) supports the claim as stated. It documents DeviceProps.featureLevel with the four values 'core' (portable default), 'max' (every adapter feature and supported limit), 'compatibility', and 'best-available' (upgrades compatibility to core when available), states the effective level is reported at device.info.featureLevel, and separately confirms device.limits reports storage buffer/storage texture availability separately for vertex and fragment stages — matching all elements of the claim.

<a id="census-luma-p1"></a>**LUMA-P1** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; performance) — 9.4 adds WebGPU render bundles (RenderBundleEncoder -> immutable RenderBundle -> RenderPass.executeBundles()); explicitly WebGL2-unsupported; bundles must be rebuilt when the command list, bound resources, pipeline, or attachment compatibility change, but not when buffer/texture contents change. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/resources/render-bundle-encoder.md> — "Render bundles allow expensive CPU-side command recording and validation to be performed once and reused, improving performance when many identical draw calls are repeated frame after frame."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-P1](#luma-p1)):** PARTIAL — WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts:1-110 caches GPURenderBundles keyed by BundleKey with a BundleEncoderDescriptor {colorFormats, depthStencilFormat, sampleCount} and invalidate/invalidateByPrefix/invalidateAll (:161-187); WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:326,:605-613 replays `command.bundle` via executeBundles. WebGPUEnvironmentRenderer.js:1865-1926 keys the moon bundle on format set + sample count and invalidates only on bind-group replacement (texture upgrade) - contents changes do not rebuild; WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:1534-1544 invalidateAll on MSAA change. FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:547 SHIPPED, :554 globe inline bundle REMOVED as net-negative (rotating dynamic offsets baked in, Batch 292), :895 lists 3 opt-in sites; grep in WebGPUVolumetricFogRenderer.ts shows only the asFreezable registration (:604). D.8 R-7a..f (:1315-1320) defer bundles for tiles/OIT/pick/shadow/vector/buffer passes. **Gap:** The bundle mechanism and the correct invalidation contract exist, but only the environment (moon) draw is bundled in practice; static tile draws are not bundled because per-tile dynamic offsets are baked at record time. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-luma-p2"></a>**LUMA-P2** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; performance) — luma.gl 9.4-alpha.1 added a core fix to skip redundant uniform-buffer writes, alongside the move of draw state to RenderPass and portable feature levels. Source: <https://github.com/visgl/luma.gl/releases/tag/v9.4.0-alpha.1> — "fix(core): Avoid redundant uniform buffer writes (#2644)"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-P2](#luma-p2)):** PARTIAL — Redundant-write guards are per-subsystem: WebGPUMaterialUploadState.js:28-60 version cursor per GPU buffer; WebGPUEffectsStateCache.js:1-40 skips identical uniform payloads (`_skippedWrites`); WebGPUClusterAssignRenderer.ts:212-332 `_lastUploadedLightData` compare; WebGPUOceanRenderer.ts:767-871 paramsDirty. But WebGPUUniformGroupManager.ts:297-315 sets `_perFrameDirty=true` unconditionally in updatePerFrame so flushPerFrame writes every frame, and WebGPURingBufferAllocator.ts:303-361 coalesces dirty pages without comparing bytes (globe camera UB rewritten each frame). grep for a generic byte-equality skip helper (`_lastUploaded`/`bytesEqual`/`skipRedundant`) hits only the cluster renderer. **Gap:** There is no portable-layer 'did the bytes change' guard in the UB pack/upload helpers; each caller still owns its own dirty tracking, so the Batch-717 defect class is prevented by convention rather than by the layer. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-p3"></a>**LUMA-P3** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; performance) — GPU timing is exposed through a portable QuerySet with type 'timestamp' gated on device.features.has('timestamp-query'); on WebGL it is backed by EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2 and results are asynchronous (normally not available in the recording frame). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/resources/query-set.md> — "Timestamp queries are portable when `device.features.has('timestamp-query')` is `true`. On WebGL this is backed by [`EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`]."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-P3](#luma-p3)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:1-90 uses pass `timestampWrites`, gated on hasFeature('timestamp-query') (WebGPUContext.ts:7029-7045), async N-frames-late results, overlap/coverage accounting via WebGPUTimestampAccounting. Counts: WebGPUFrameStatistics.ts:27-35 (drawCallCount, triangleCount); AsyncResourceTelemetry.ts:1-50 p50/p95/p99 per resource kind. Memory: only `gbufferBytes` (WebGPUContext.ts:742,5176); grep for allocation/texture/buffer byte counters in WebGPUTexture/Buffer/ResourceManager finds none, and plan §1.2 FAR-001 (:56) lists 'allocation/upload/lifetime ... counters' as still required. grep `disjoint_timer|TIME_ELAPSED` across packages/engine/Source: no hits - WebGL has no timer-query backing. **Gap:** GPU timing is WebGPU-only (no EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2 lane) and GPU memory is not tracked per allocation, so the multi-metric bar (time + memory + counts) is only two-thirds met and only on one backend. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-deck-p4"></a>**DECK-P4** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; performance) — deck.gl 9.4 picking no longer renders per-object picking colours for most layers; it uses the shader builtin instance_index, cutting GPU memory and layer init cost. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "Picking has been optimized. Most layers now use shader builtins (`instance_index`) instead of picking color buffers, reducing GPU memory usage and layer initialization costs."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [DECK-P4](#deck-p4)):** MISSING — WebGPUPickFramebuffer.ts:1-12 renders a pick-colour pass into rgba8unorm and reads back via copyTextureToBuffer/mapAsync; :79-88 pick attachment must be an 8-bit unorm format. WebGPUPickCommandHelpers.ts:1-60 allocates a PickId colour per primitive (SinglePickIdCache) or per glTF primitive (MultiPickIdCache) through context.createPickId. `@builtin(instance_index)` appears only for instance/splat data indexing (ComputeInstanceRender.wgsl:81, WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:683). FAR-409 (plan :795-806) plans 'backend-neutral ID ranges and WebGPU integer attachment(s)' so 'per-feature results do not require eager creation of N WebGL-shaped pick IDs'; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1258 records R-5 single-buffer GPU picking as analysed and declined. **Gap:** Pick identity is still a per-object colour encoded into an RGBA8 target on both backends; no instance_index/vertex-index picking or integer attachment path exists yet. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-luma-f1"></a>**LUMA-F1** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — GPU-driven rendering: GPUSceneDrawGeneration converts visible GPUScene records into bounded indirect draw commands on the GPU; it requires the optional 'indirect-first-instance' feature and the docs state WebGPU has no portable bindless multi-draw, so the app still records one indirect draw per command slot. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/gpu-core/gpu-scene-draw-generation.md> — "`GPUSceneDrawGeneration` turns active, visible `GPUScene` records into bounded indirect draw commands."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-F1](#luma-f1)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:1-25 implements one indirect record per slot with a single drawIndirect/drawIndexedIndirect per call (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:581 SCAFFOLDED); WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:36-45 CullMode.INDIRECT zeroes instanceCount (inventory :898 SCAFFOLDED, JS/WASM cull active; Scene.js:559 gpuCullingHint defaults 'never' under FAR-003). `indirect-first-instance` is only reported as a capability (WebGPUContext.ts:7021) and inventory D.8:1328 lists it unwired; `chromium-experimental-multi-draw-indirect` D.8:1324 deferred. The one live GPU-generated indirect draw is point-cloud LOD (WebGPUPointCloudRenderer.ts:2397-2431). FAR-502 (plan :827-833) states 'core WebGPU has no multi-draw-indirect command, CPU/render bundles still record a bounded sequence of drawIndirect calls'; QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:133 MS-00 feasibility spike QUEUED. **Gap:** Scene-level GPU draw generation (visible records to bounded indirect slots) exists only for point clouds; 3D Tiles/model cohorts stay CPU-driven until FAR-500..503 land, though the one-indirect-per-slot premise is already confirmed in the fork's own plan. (value HIGH, effort XL).

<a id="census-luma-f2"></a>**LUMA-F2** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Virtual-geometry selection runs on the GPU: conservative bounding-sphere frustum culling plus a screen-space-error metric (maximumScreenSpaceError in pixels) refines a hierarchy whose cluster IDs may be meshlets, point-cloud nodes, impostors or terrain patches, and its counts can feed an indirect draw directly. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/gpu-core/gpu-virtual-geometry-selection.md> — "Cluster IDs can address meshlets, point-cloud nodes, impostors, terrain patches, or application-defined geometry records."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-F2](#luma-f2)):** MISSING — WebGPUPointCloudLODProcessor.ts:1-60 does frustum cull + distance LOD + screen-space density decimation inside one loaded tile and states it is 'NOT a replacement for tile-level LOD' (SHIPPED, FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:621,769). WebGPUVoxelRenderer.ts:2090-2165 computes the SSE refinement ladder on the CPU. ARCHITECTURE.md §10.4 (:816-836) describes GPU traversal + cull + indirect build as the Phase-8b target and says the stack is 'genuinely unbuilt'; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md D.8:1332 lists 'terrain LOD selection, 3D Tile GPU culling' as future compute shaders; QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:246-251 C18-A1 (CLOD) and C18-A6 (ray-guided residency) are queued, not landed. **Gap:** No GPU hierarchy traversal with a maximumScreenSpaceError metric feeding indirect draws; tile/cluster selection is CPU-side (f64) for terrain, 3D Tiles, voxels and splats. (value HIGH, effort XL).

<a id="census-luma-f3"></a>**LUMA-F3** (**SPLIT**; REFUTED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Clustered (many-light) deferred lighting exists as an experimental WebGPU-only compute stage: a 16x9x24 screen/log-depth grid, up to 64 light indices per cluster, MAX_CLUSTERED_POINT_LIGHTS = 512, with overflow compaction and a fallback scan of the light buffer. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/clustered-lighting.md> — "`ClusteredLightGrid` is an experimental WebGPU-only compute stage for many-light deferred rendering."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).
**Verifier reasoning (SPLIT):**
- Vote 1, REFUTED (source checked: Attempted: raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/clustered-lighting.md and its github.com/blob equivalent (both returned suspiciously exact but unverifiable content), plus a directory listing of docs/api-reference/experimental (returned implausible filenames suggesting hallucination), plus docs/whats-new.md (only a generic one-line mention found, none of the specific numbers/claims corroborated), plus WebSearch for "ClusteredLightGrid" and "MAX_CLUSTERED_POINT_LIGHTS" (no independent corroboration of the specifics).): The only "confirmation" of the cited page (raw.githubusercontent.com/.../experimental/clustered-lighting.md) and its exact quoted specs (16x9x24 grid, 64 lights/cluster, MAX_CLUSTERED_POINT_LIGHTS=512, overflow compaction, fallback scan) came from WebFetch calls that returned suspiciously tidy, exact-match text for every specific number in the claim — a known failure mode where WebFetch's summarizer fabricates plausible-sounding content instead of reporting that a page doesn't exist. A directory-listing fetch for the same folder returned a list padded with implausible/unlikely luma.gl experimental doc names (mls-mpm-fluid-simulation.md, spectral-caustics-renderer.md, gpu-dggs.md, gpu-h3.md, spectral-ocean-simulation.md, volumetric-fire-simulation.md) that do not match any known luma.gl documentation structure, further indicating hallucinated output rather than real repo content. Independent WebSearch queries only turned up a single generic one-line mention in docs/whats-new.md ("ClusteredLightGrid bins point lights into a configurable 3D screen-space grid and feeds the clustered deferred resolve pipeline") with no mention of "WebGPU-only," no grid dimensions, no MAX_CLUSTERED_POINT_LIGHTS constant, and no overflow/fallback details — none of the load-bearing specifics in the claim could be independently corroborated. Given the tool's demonstrated tendency to fabricate matching content on repeated identical queries and the absence of any independent source for the specific numbers or the WebGPU-only framing, this cannot be verified and defaults to REFUTED.
- Vote 2, CONFIRMED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/clustered-lighting.md (cross-checked via https://api.github.com/repos/visgl/luma.gl/contents/docs/api-reference/experimental/clustered-lighting.md)): Fetched the cited raw.githubusercontent.com page directly and cross-checked via the GitHub Contents API (independent fetch) to rule out fabrication. Both confirm the file exists at docs/api-reference/experimental/clustered-lighting.md and describes ClusteredLightGrid as an experimental WebGPU compute feature for many-light deferred rendering, with default grid dimensions 16x9x24 (screen tiles x log depth slices), up to 64 light indices per cluster, a 512 max point-light cap, storage buffers (clusterLightCounts/clusterLightIndices) for compaction, and overflow handling via a fallback path. This matches all specific numeric claims (16x9x24, 64, 512) and the WebGPU-only/experimental/compute-stage/overflow-compaction/fallback characterization in the claim, and the quoted sentence ("ClusteredLightGrid is an experimental WebGPU-only compute stage for many-light deferred rendering") is a faithful paraphrase/match of the page's own framing.

<a id="census-luma-f4"></a>**LUMA-F4** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + PLAUSIBLE; feature) — @luma.gl/splats (experimental, 9.4) renders Gaussian splats GPU-natively: WebGPU projects, sorts and draws each streamed batch through a GPU command graph with bounded residency, hierarchy traversal, foveated LOD and GPU picking; WebGL2 gets only a prepared SplatRenderer path with caller-provided ordering. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/splats/README.md> — "The graph renderer, GPU ordering, hierarchy traversal, and storage-backed features require WebGPU. Use the prepared SplatRenderer path when WebGL 2 compatibility or caller-provided source ordering is required."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-F4](#luma-f4)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:1-20 (Kerbl 2023, RTE positioning), :84-91 draws through `sortedIndices[instance_index]` from a storage buffer, :139-166 consumes the WASM radix-sort permutation from the shared worker (comparatorSorts/providedSortUploads counters) - no GPU sort; pickPipeline present (:77). FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:624: C15-G2..G5 landed and pixel-confirmed (Batch 881/895, sh_unit_cube 0.000% mismatch). QUEUE_2026-08-02_CAMPAIGN15.md:17-23: G6 PARTIAL, G7/G8 PENDING. QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:273 C18-S0 license pass DONE (Mip-Splatting/StopThePop research-only; GPUSorting MIT), :276 C18-S3 GPU radix sort gated post-G8, :280 C18-S7 LOD/streaming non-goal ruling request. **Gap:** The fork has a single-batch, CPU/WASM-sorted splat path with GPU picking; it lacks GPU sorting, hierarchy traversal, bounded out-of-core residency and foveated LOD, all of which are gated behind C15-G8 and the C18-S7 ruling. (value HIGH, effort XL).

<a id="census-luma-f5"></a>**LUMA-F5** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — 9.4 ships a WebGPU-first composable post-processing set: depth-aware blur, SSAO, temporally stabilised GTAO, SSGI, outlines, temporal AA, motion blur, temporally stabilised SSR, height fog, clustered volumetric lighting, GPU-driven adaptive exposure and HDR-safe multiscale bloom, consuming app-provided depth/normal/velocity/material textures. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "New WebGPU-first composable pipelines provide depth-aware blur, SSAO, temporally stabilized GTAO, colored screen-space diffuse global illumination, outlines, temporal AA, motion blur, roughness-aware temporally stabilized screen-space reflections, compact height fog, bounded clustered participating-media lighting with camera-aware history, GPU-driven adaptive exposure, and HDR-safe successively filtered multiscale bloom."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-F5](#luma-f5)):** PARTIAL — FEATURE_INVENTORY.md B.5 (:780-812): Bloom, SSAO/GTAO, SSGI (SSILVB), DoF, GodRay, TAA, MotionBlur, SSR, AutoExposure, tonemap/f16, FXAA, Silhouette/EdgeDetection all SHIPPED; WebGPUNPROutlineEffect.ts:1-15 and WebGPUContactShadowsEffect.ts:1-12 read the G-buffer; height fog in WebGPUVolumetricFogRenderer.ts:10. Temporal contract: WebGPUParityManager.ts:1-50 centralises ping-pong phase; WebGPUTAAEffect.ts:379-395 history pair, :575 resetHistory(), :605-620 read/write via parity manager with skip-blend after realloc. grep temporal/history in WebGPUAmbientOcclusionEffect.ts (:173,720 slice rotation only) and ScreenSpaceReflections.wgsl (none); grep cluster in VolumetricFog.wgsl/renderer: none. ARCHITECTURE.md §11.1 (:871) records an OPEN pre-tonemap ordering divergence vs WebGL. **Gap:** Near-complete effect coverage, but GTAO/SSR are not temporally stabilised, there is no clustered volumetric lighting or depth-aware blur primitive, and only TAA uses the shared history-slot contract (the other effects either have no history or manage it inline). (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-f6"></a>**LUMA-F6** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Order-independent transparency is provided as an experimental WBOIT renderer on both WebGPU and WebGL2 using two rgba16float accumulation/revealage targets composited over an opaque colour texture; an A-buffer renderer is the alternative. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/experimental/wboit-renderer.md> — "WBOITRenderer implements weighted blended order-independent transparency on WebGPU and WebGL2."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-F6](#luma-f6)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUOIT.ts:1-19 implements McGuire-Bavoil WBOIT with rgba16float accumulation + r8unorm revealage (:205-223) composited over scene colour (:232+). It is contained OFF (WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:952 `_webgpuOITEnabled=false`, FAR-003) and unreachable for standard translucency because no primitive/model produces an OIT pipeline (WebGPUSceneRendererTranslucentPass.ts:133-183 hasOITPipelines; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:815). grep `features.has|blendable|rgba16float` in WebGPUOIT.ts shows no capability predicate, whereas WebGL OIT.js:32 gates on `colorBufferFloat && depthTexture && floatBlend`. `dual-source-blending` is requested (WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:58) but WGF-2 is not wired; FAR-404 (plan :750-757) plans the multi-frustum MSAA topology. No A-buffer renderer exists (D.5:1259 GPU multi-hit linked list is future). **Gap:** The WebGPU WBOIT path has no format-renderable/blendable support predicate, no live producer, and no A-buffer alternative; the WebGL predicate is the only feature-detection gate in tree. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-deck-f7"></a>**DECK-F7** (**SPLIT**; REFUTED + CONFIRMED; feature) — deck.gl 9.4 (beta) expands WebGPU to Arc/Column/GridCell/Path/Polygon/SolidPolygon/Tile/Tile3D/ScreenGrid/Hexagon/Contour/Heatmap layers, MVTLayer with tile clipping, and adds a `visgl:webgl-only` export condition that strips WebGPU branches and WGSL from bundles; WebGPU remains experimental and not production-recommended. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "Applications that only target WebGL2 can instead configure their bundler to resolve the custom export condition `visgl:webgl-only`; supported deck.gl packages will then use alternate builds with WebGPU branches and WGSL shader sources removed, reducing their contribution to bundle size without changing the imported APIs."
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).
**Verifier reasoning (SPLIT):**
- Vote 1, REFUTED (source checked: https://deck.gl/docs/whats-new (live site) and https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md (three inconsistent fetch attempts); https://registry.npmjs.org/@deck.gl/core for version confirmation): The cited URL (raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md) could not be verified reliably: three separate fetches of the identical URL returned three mutually inconsistent fabricated summaries (different quoted "exact" text, different release dates for v9.4, differing layer lists and even differing claims about which file/version is "latest"), indicating the fetcher was hallucinating content rather than returning real page text — likely because the raw file either doesn't exist at that path or doesn't contain this content. Cross-checking against the live, real deck.gl docs site (deck.gl/docs/whats-new) — a much more reliable source than the possibly-nonexistent raw markdown page — shows no v9.4 release notes at all; the most recent documented version is v9.3 (April 2026), and it explicitly states no mention of 'visgl:webgl-only' or a v9.4 WebGPU expansion exists in that documentation. npm registry data does confirm a 9.4.0-beta.1 tag exists, so a 9.4 beta is real, but the specific claimed content (webgl-only export condition stripping WGSL, the specific layer list, MVTLayer tile clipping) is not substantiated by any consistently-reproducible source text. Given the requirement to default to REFUTED when unable to verify, and given the live docs site (the more trustworthy of the two sources checked) contradicts the claim's existence, this is REFUTED.
- Vote 2, CONFIRMED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md): The cited page (docs/whats-new.md on visgl/deck.gl master) contains a v9.4 section confirming: WebGPU support extended to ArcLayer, ColumnLayer, GridCellLayer, PathLayer, PolygonLayer, SolidPolygonLayer, TileLayer, Tile3DLayer, ScreenGridLayer, HexagonLayer, ContourLayer, and HeatmapLayer; MVTLayer renders on WebGPU with tile clipping for circle/path/polygon sublayers; a `visgl:webgl-only` custom export condition that resolves to alternate builds with WebGPU branches and WGSL shader sources removed; and a statement that WebGPU support remains experimental and not yet recommended for production. All elements of the claim (version scope, capabilities, export condition, and experimental caveat) are supported by the page content.

<a id="census-deck-g1"></a>**DECK-G1** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; geospatial) — deck.gl compensates for the absence of f64 on WebGL2/WebGPU by dynamically translating common-space positions by a viewport-determined offset before projection (an RTE-like origin rebase), and its offset coordinate systems (METER_OFFSETS/LNGLAT_OFFSETS) trade accuracy for a linearised local projection. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/developer-guide/coordinate-systems.md> — "To compensate for the lack of 64-bit floats in WebGL2/WebGPU, deck.gl may apply a dynamic translation to common-space positions, determined by the viewport, to improve the precision of projection."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [DECK-G1](#deck-g1)):** HAS — ARCHITECTURE.md §5 (:300-316) mandates per-vertex positionHigh/positionLow with `mvpRelativeToEye * translateRelativeToEye(...)`; chunks/functions/csm_translateRelativeToEye.wgsl:26-50 subtracts the encoded camera in the split domain; WebGPURTEAssertions.ts:1-40 pragma-guarded validators catch swapped high/low and mis-ordered MVP translation zeroing; EncodedCartesian3 performs the CPU split. This is strictly stronger than a per-viewport common-space rebase. **Gap:** None relative to the neighbour; the fork's own residual is ellipsoid-aware RTE for non-WGS84 bodies (ARCHITECTURE.md §5.4, FEAT-3DT2-03), which is an orthogonal gap. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-luma-g2"></a>**LUMA-G2** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; geospatial) — luma's fp64 module is a double-single (hi+lo f32) representation giving roughly 48 significant bits; the full function library (exp/log/trig) is GLSL-only, and WebGPU shaders get only the lower-level fp64arithmetic WGSL helpers (add/sub/mul/div/sqrt). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/shadertools/shader-modules/fp64.md> — "The full `fp64` function library is GLSL-only. It does not provide WGSL source, so WGSL shaders cannot call `exp_fp64`, `log_fp64`, or its trigonometric functions."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-G2](#luma-g2)):** PARTIAL — Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/ComputeInstanceScaffold.wgsl:60-135 defines df64 (hi+lo f32, ~46 mantissa bits) helpers csm_df64, csm_df64_add, csm_df64_add_f32, csm_df64_mul, csm_df64_mul_f32, csm_df64_sin, csm_df64_cos, csm_df64_split built on csm_twoSum/csm_twoProd; grep `csm_df64` finds no other consumer and the chunks/functions library (listing) carries no fp64 chunk. No df64 div/sqrt/exp/log exists in WGSL; GLSL czm_ paths have no WGSL twin either. **Gap:** The double-single helpers live only inside the compute-instance scaffold, are not a shared csm_ chunk, and lack div/sqrt/exp/log; no ~48-bit precision bar is measured for the RTE path. (value LOW, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-g3"></a>**LUMA-G3** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; geospatial) — On Apple WebGPU adapters luma automatically switches double-single arithmetic to integer-controlled twoSum/twoProd/renormalisation because the Metal compiler is permitted to reassociate/fuse float ops, breaking error-free transforms; LUMA_FP64_INTEGER_ARITHMETIC can force or disable the mode. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/whats-new.md> — "WGSL double-single arithmetic automatically uses integer-controlled `twoSum`, `twoProd`, and renormalization on Apple WebGPU adapters, avoiding Metal compiler reassociation while retaining the existing `vec2f` API. The `LUMA_FP64_INTEGER_ARITHMETIC` shader define can force or disable the mode."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-G3](#luma-g3)):** MISSING — grep -i metal|apple|reassoc|fma|precise across Renderer/WebGPU and Shaders/WebGPU returns only NDC-range and subgroup-width comments (BillboardCollection.wgsl:185, FrustumCull.wgsl:16,152, PointCloudLOD.wgsl:201). csm_twoSum (ComputeInstanceScaffold.wgsl:99-105) and csm_translateRelativeToEye (:36-49, highDifference + lowDifference) are plain float error-free transforms with no integer-controlled variant or contraction guard. Tools grep for Apple/macOS/Safari lanes hits only vendored Rust crates and incidental words; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md D.9:1378 mentions Safari only for a worker rAF fallback. CLAUDE.md fixes Playwright to Edge on Windows. **Gap:** No protection against Metal reassociation/FMA contraction destroying the low bits of the high/low subtraction, and no Apple-adapter probe lane exists to detect it. (value HIGH, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-g4"></a>**LUMA-G4** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; geospatial) — luma's precision guide argues large-coordinate rendering needs one exact operation - localPosition = round_f32(exact(worldPosition - worldOrigin)) - and implements it by subtracting packed binary64 significands/exponents with integer ops in WGSL and rounding once, rather than general fp64 arithmetic. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-guide/shaders/gpu-floating-point-precision.md> — "Large-coordinate rendering often does not need general fp64 arithmetic. It needs this operation: `localPosition = round_f32(exact(worldPosition - worldOrigin))`"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-G4](#luma-g4)):** MISSING — grep significand|packed double|unpackDouble|f64 across Shaders/WebGPU and Renderer/WebGPU finds no packed-binary64 helper; positions and camera are split on the CPU (EncodedCartesian3) and reconstructed by csm_translateRelativeToEye.wgsl:11-14,36-49. GlobeTerrain.wgsl:1125-1140 documents f32 mantissa overflow in ray-sphere math mitigated by 1/radius scaling; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:516 notes the Batch-350 RTC switch addressed the coarse-L0-tile jitter class. **Gap:** No exact packed-double subtraction in-shader; large-coordinate precision relies entirely on CPU-split hi/lo pairs, leaving the u32-pair/round-once encoding untried for the FAR-camera L0 class. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-deck-g5"></a>**DECK-G5** (**REFUTED**; REFUTED + REFUTED; geospatial) — deck.gl's GlobeView is documented as lacking high-precision rendering above zoom 12, supporting only the 'lnglat' coordinate system, and (9.4) enabling back-face culling by default through a new per-View `parameters` prop, while TerrainLayer/TerrainExtension/Tile3DLayer only gained GlobeView support in 9.4. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/globe-view.md> — "No high-precision rendering at high zoom levels (> 12)"
**Fork:** not mapped in the first pass (verified, never read against the fork; listed here so the gap is visible).
**Verifier reasoning (REFUTED):**
- Vote 1, REFUTED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/globe-view.md): The cited page does confirm the "No high-precision rendering at high zoom levels (> 12)" quote and the lnglat-only coordinateSystem restriction. However, the claim's other assertions are not supported: the page attaches no version number to the back-face-culling-by-default behavior (no "9.4" mentioned anywhere in the fetched content), and critically the page explicitly states TerrainLayer "currently do not work in this view" — directly contradicting the claim that TerrainLayer/TerrainExtension/Tile3DLayer "gained GlobeView support in 9.4." Since the claim bundles a directly contradicted assertion (terrain layer support) with the accurate parts, and fabricates a version number not present in the source, the overall claim as stated is not supported by the cited page.
- Vote 2, REFUTED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/deck.gl/master/docs/api-reference/core/globe-view.md): The cited page does confirm two sub-claims verbatim: "No high-precision rendering at high zoom levels (> 12)" and lnglat-only coordinate system support. However the page contains no version/date information at all (no "9.4" anywhere), so the claim's framing as a 9.4-specific documentation set is unsupported. More importantly, two parts of the compound claim are actively contradicted: (1) the page's back-face-culling mention is a workaround suggestion in a "Remarks" section for handling occlusion ("Enable back-face culling via parameters: {cull: true}"), not a statement that GlobeView enables it "by default" as a new 9.4 feature; (2) TerrainLayer is explicitly listed as an UNSUPPORTED layer in GlobeView on this page — the opposite of the claim that TerrainLayer/TerrainExtension/Tile3DLayer "gained GlobeView support in 9.4," and TerrainExtension/Tile3DLayer aren't mentioned at all. Since the source contradicts rather than merely under-specifies key parts of the compound claim, this fails even the PLAUSIBLE bar.

<a id="census-luma-t1"></a>**LUMA-T1** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; tooling) — luma's debug tooling copies each resource `id` into the WebGPU object `label`, exposes a `luma.log` console API with persistent debug flags and log levels, and keeps WebGL developer-tools/Spector.js integration behind a separate `@luma.gl/webgl/debug` entry so debug code stays out of production bundles.
