Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/developer-guide/debugging.md> — "`id` is copied into the WebGPU object `label` field which is designed to support debugging"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-T1](#luma-t1)):** PARTIAL — 1,086 `label:` occurrences across Renderer/WebGPU/*.ts (hand-written per resource; module-cache labels stamp the define mask). GraphicsContext.ts:1952-1971 provides `log(level, message)` with a `[CesiumJS:<type>:<id>]` prefix. scripts/build.js:69 stripPragmaPlugin removes `//>>includeStart('debug')` blocks from .js and .ts; Tools/lint-debug-pragmas.mjs:1-20 enforces the wrap policy; Scene/CesiumDebug.js:1-25 exposes the window.CesiumDebug console API. grep localStorage|URLSearchParams in CesiumDebug.js/SceneDebug.js: none; package.json exports declare no separate debug entry. **Gap:** No persistent debug flags/log levels and no separate debug entry bundle - heavyweight diagnostics are pragma-stripped inline rather than isolated behind a debug import, and labels are not derived from a resource id. (value LOW, effort M).

<a id="census-luma-t2"></a>**LUMA-T2** (**SPLIT**; PLAUSIBLE + REFUTED; tooling) — GPU rendering is regression-tested with SnapshotTestRunner: browser-driven (Puppeteer via @probe.gl/test-utils) render tests pixel-diffed against golden images with configurable tolerance/threshold; deck.gl enabled its render-test suite on WebGPU in the 9.4 alphas, and luma 9.4 adds feature-level-specific WebGPU test devices. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/test-utils/snapshot-test-runner.md> — "pixel-diffing with a pre-defined 'golden image'"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [LUMA-T2](#luma-t2)):** PARTIAL — Tools/visual-regression/capture-and-diff.mjs:1-40 drives Playwright (Edge) over scenes.json and evaluates three gates (WebGL-vs-historical, WebGPU-vs-historical, cross-backend) with per-scene `thresholds`/`expectedMismatch` (scenes.json:3) and a NON_CERTIFYING provenance exit; Tools/lib/webgpu-error-gate.mjs arms device-error gates; 984 files under Tools/visual-regression. WebGPUContext.ts:374-379,1696-1705 accepts `featureLevel: 'core'|'compatibility'`, but grep across Tools/ and Apps/ finds no lane that runs under 'compatibility' (only webgpu-pick-emission-counters.spec.mjs:481 sets 'core' on a fake context). Wave-end gate ruled R-2026-08-29-2 (CLAUDE.md) but per the 2026-09-01 state note it has never produced a receipt. **Gap:** Golden-image gating is in place, but there is no feature-level-specific (compatibility-mode) test device lane and the wave-end smoke+VR gate is not yet an executed, banked receipt. (value MEDIUM, effort S).
**Verifier reasoning (SPLIT):**
- Vote 1, PLAUSIBLE (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/test-utils/snapshot-test-runner.md): The cited page confirms the general SnapshotTestRunner capability: it launches Puppeteer to run browser-based render tests, captures screenshots, and "perform[s] pixel-diffing with a pre-defined 'golden image'" with configurable tolerance/threshold options (matching the quoted text exactly). However, the page describes it as a "browser-based WebGL render tests" utility and contains zero mention of WebGPU, luma 9.4, WebGPU test devices, or deck.gl enabling a render-test suite on WebGPU in 9.4 alphas — those specific version/scope claims are entirely unsupported by this source. The general testing infrastructure claim is right; the WebGPU-specific version/scope claims in the quote are not backed by the cited page.
- Vote 2, REFUTED (source checked: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/visgl/luma.gl/master/docs/api-reference/test-utils/snapshot-test-runner.md): The cited page confirms only the generic SnapshotTestRunner mechanism (Puppeteer-driven WebGL render tests, pixel-diffing against a "golden image", configurable tolerance/threshold/includeAA/etc.) — the quoted phrase is accurate. However the page explicitly frames this as WebGL-only ("browser-based WebGL render tests") and contains no mention whatsoever of WebGPU, of deck.gl enabling its render-test suite on WebGPU in 9.4 alphas, or of luma 9.4 adding feature-level-specific WebGPU test devices. None of the version/capability claims specific to WebGPU are supported by this source.

### A.3 the WebGPU platform (W3C, Dawn, wgpu, WebKit)

25 claims: 25 KEPT, 0 REFUTED, 0 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 2 / PARTIAL 13 / MISSING 10 / NOT-APPLICABLE 0.

<a id="census-wgpu-01"></a>**WGPU-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; platform) — The WebGPU API specification is now a W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft dated 1 September 2026 (TR/2026/CRD-webgpu-20260901), i.e. the API surface the fork targets has reached CR stage rather than Working Draft. Source: <https://www.w3.org/TR/webgpu/> — "W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft, 1 September 2026"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-01](#wgpu-01)):** HAS — packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:41-75 (DESIRED_FEATURES negotiated per adapter; core command/bind-group model assumed); WebGPUDevicePool.ts:814-820 requestDevice with negotiated features/limits; package.json:67 pins @webgpu/types 0.1.69; WebGPUDevicePool.ts:770-775 casts featureLevel because the pinned types lag the spec. **Gap:** The fork already targets the core API with optional features negotiated at device init; the only drift is the @webgpu/types pin trailing the CR so new enum members are string-cast (featureLevel, tier names, maxImmediateSize). (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-02"></a>**WGPU-02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — WGSL is a Candidate Recommendation Draft dated 31 August 2026 and now enumerates a large set of runtime-detectable language extensions (readonly_and_readwrite_storage_textures, packed_4x8_integer_dot_product, unrestricted_pointer_parameters, pointer_composite_access, uniform_buffer_standard_layout, subgroup_id, subgroup_uniformity, texture_and_sampler_let, texture_formats_tier1, linear_indexing, immediate_address_space, fragment_depth, buffer_view, swizzle_assignment) alongside enable-extensions (f16, clip_distances, dual_source_blending, subgroups, primitive_index, subgroup_size_control). Shader authoring is therefore feature-detected via navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures, not versioned. Source: <https://www.w3.org/TR/WGSL/> — "W3C Candidate Recommendation Draft, 31 August 2026"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-02](#wgpu-02)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUShaderDefines.ts:71-863 (lo word full) and :942-1117 (ShaderDefineHi has free bits); GLOBE_IMAGERY_REDUCED (:401) is the only device-derived define, set from device.limits at WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:675-676; zero hits for wgslLanguageFeatures under packages/engine/Source; enable-extensions are handled by ad hoc source prepending (WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:159, WebGPUPointCloudLODProcessor.ts:305, AmbientOcclusionGenerate_f16.wgsl:18). **Gap:** No define bit is populated from navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures at device init; extension-gated variants bypass the ifdef/module-cache system via string prepends, so immediates/linear_indexing/texture_and_sampler_let have no landing slot. (value HIGH, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-03"></a>**WGPU-03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — The spec's GPUFeatureName enum now includes, beyond the v1 set, "subgroups", "texture-formats-tier1", "texture-formats-tier2", "primitive-index", "texture-component-swizzle", "subgroup-size-control", "texture-compression-unaligned" and "core-features-and-limits" (the latter being the switch that distinguishes Core from Compatibility devices). Source: <https://gpuweb.github.io/gpuweb/> — "core-features-and-limits" ... "subgroups" "texture-formats-tier1" "texture-formats-tier2" "primitive-index" "texture-component-swizzle" "subgroup-size-control" "texture-compression-unaligned"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-03](#wgpu-03)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:47 core-features-and-limits, :50 texture-formats-tier1, :66 subgroups requested; zero hits for texture-formats-tier2, "primitive-index", texture-component-swizzle, subgroup-size-control, texture-compression-unaligned; WebGPUPrimitiveIndexUtils.ts:33-36 assumes primitive_index is a core builtin with no feature request or enable directive (csm_primitiveIndex.wgsl:20-21 says the same). **Gap:** Five enum members are unrequested, and primitive_index is treated as core rather than as the optional primitive-index feature + WGSL enable, so the triangulation-debug probe compile will fail on a conforming device. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-04"></a>**WGPU-04** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + PLAUSIBLE; platform) — Chrome 146 (published 2026-02-25) shipped WebGPU Compatibility Mode, an opt-in subset requested via featureLevel: "compatibility" that runs WebGPU on OpenGL ES 3.1-class hardware (initially Android/WebView/ChromeOS/Mac/Windows); the Intent to Ship names milestone 146 for desktop, Android and WebView. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-146> — "This mode lets you run WebGPU on older graphics APIs like OpenGL ES 3.1."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-04](#wgpu-04)):** PARTIAL — RendererType.ts:56 WEBGPU_COMPAT; ContextFactory.ts:149-155 maps it to featureLevel "compatibility"; WebGPUDevicePool.ts:769-775 forwards featureLevel to requestAdapter and :482-519 refuses to share core/compat devices; RendererType.ts:165/204 AUTO attempts are [WEBGPU, WEBGL] with compat only on explicit request (:186-192); FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:503,590. **Gap:** Compat is plumbed end to end but AUTO never tries it before falling back to WebGL, and no probe has ever run the globe under featureLevel "compatibility". (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-05"></a>**WGPU-05** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; platform) — Compatibility Mode imposes concrete restrictions relevant to renderer design: cube-array textures are disallowed, array texture views bound in bind groups must cover the entire array, blend state must be identical across all color attachments, compressed-texture copies are disallowed, textureLoad on depth textures is disallowed, integer multisampled formats are prohibited, and new limits maxStorageBuffersInVertexStage / maxStorageTexturesInFragmentStage restrict those stages (to zero and four respectively by default). Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/blob/main/proposals/compatibility-mode.md> — "Array texture views used in bind groups must consist of the entire array"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-05](#wgpu-05)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUDevicePool.ts:166-170 lists maxStorageBuffersInVertexStage / maxStorageTexturesInFragmentStage for limit negotiation only; vertex-stage storage buffers are live in WebGPUComputeInstanceRenderer.ts:480-486,600-601,750, WebGPUFlowFieldRenderer.ts:386, WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:1150-1157, WebGPUModelDeviceResources.ts:155, WebGPUGroundPolylineRenderer.js:1215; WebGPUCSMRenderer.ts:476-495 binds partial array-layer views; the only compat-shaped probe is probe-globe-default-limits.mjs:1-12 (sampled-texture floor only). **Gap:** No compat-mode probe and no per-FeatureRenderer compat variants, so instances, splats, flow field, model skinning, CSM per-layer views and MRT OIT blend state would all fail validation under compat. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-wgpu-06"></a>**WGPU-06** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Chrome 149-150 (published 2026-06-17) shipped Immediates: a WGSL var<immediate> address space plus setImmediates() on pass encoders, letting small per-draw data be pushed without a buffer or bind group; support is detected via the immediate_address_space WGSL language extension. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-149-150> — "Call setImmediates() in JavaScript before a draw call to provide this data"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-06](#wgpu-06)):** MISSING — Repo-wide grep for setImmediates / var<immediate> / immediate_address_space returns only the maxImmediateSize entry in the limit-negotiation table (WebGPUDevicePool.ts:189); per-tile data rides dynamic-offset uniform buffers (WebGPUGlobeSurfaceLayouts.ts:101-103, WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTileUB.ts). **Gap:** No immediates path exists; per-draw tile/layer indices still go through the tile UB ring and bind-group cache. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-07"></a>**WGPU-07** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — The immediates spec PR (gpuweb #5423) was merged 2026-05-07: maxImmediateSize is 64 bytes, immediate data is a language extension available on all core devices, and after a render bundle executes the immediate data is treated as uninitialized (the most restrictive model, chosen for implementation simplicity). Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/pull/5423> — "Specify immediate data API and WGSL <immediate> address space"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-07](#wgpu-07)):** MISSING — WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:601-612 replays bundles via executeBundles with no immediate re-set logic; WebGPUDevicePool.ts:189 only negotiates maxImmediateSize; no design note on the 64-byte payload or the post-bundle uninitialized rule anywhere in Renderer/WebGPU or ARCHITECTURE.md. **Gap:** No bundle-vs-immediates encoding policy; must be settled before WGPU-06 is adopted since the Moon bundle and any future tile bundles would invalidate immediate state. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-08"></a>**WGPU-08** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + CONFIRMED; performance) — Chrome 146 added GPUTextureUsage.TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT for memoryless (tile-memory-only) render attachments such as depth-stencil and MSAA targets; Chrome 149-150 tightened its validation (viewFormats must be empty, view usage cannot change, and transient attachments cannot be used as a resolveTarget). Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-146> — "By declaring a texture as transient (or 'memoryless'), the GPU knows that it only needs the texture's contents temporarily."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-08](#wgpu-08)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUFramebufferManager.ts:54-69 reads GPUTextureUsage.TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT but only applies it when device.features has "transient-attachments"/"transient-attachment" (names not in the GPUFeatureName enum); applied to MSAA color (:289-293) and unsampled depth (:316) with storeOp discard (:364-386); resolve target stays non-transient (:384). **Gap:** The feature-name gate never matches on any shipping browser, so the transient bit is always 0 at createTexture; detection should key on the usage constant (and viewFormats/resolveTarget rules) with a probe. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-09"></a>**WGPU-09** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Chrome 147-148 (published 2026-04-22) shipped the WGSL linear_indexing language extension exposing global_invocation_index and workgroup_index built-ins, and extended Linux WebGPU support to modern NVIDIA drivers on Wayland. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-147-148> — "global_invocation_index: The current invocation's linear position within the total compute shader grid"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-09](#wgpu-09)):** MISSING — Zero hits for linear_indexing / global_invocation_index / workgroup_index; hand-computed linear ids at ClusterAssign.wgsl:125 and ClusterBounds.wgsl:163; 37 compute WGSL files use global_invocation_id; probe-runtime.mjs:431-445 launches Edge on Windows only. **Gap:** No define bit or chunk for linear_indexing, and no Linux/NVIDIA executor in the probe fleet. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-10"></a>**WGPU-10** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Chrome 152 stable (2026-08-25) added the optional "subgroup-size-control" GPU feature, letting compute shaders explicitly set the subgroup size (WGSL subgroup_size_control enable-extension). Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/152> — "Adds the optional GPU feature "subgroup-size-control" that allows explicitly setting the subgroup size in a compute shader."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-10](#wgpu-10)):** MISSING — WebGPUSubgroupUtils.ts:79-87 assumes subgroup size 32 or adapter minSubgroupSize; zero hits for subgroup-size-control / subgroup_size_control; subgroup kernels at FrustumCull.wgsl:125 and PointCloudLOD.wgsl:215 rely on ballot/countOneBits with whatever size the driver picks. **Gap:** No explicit subgroup size request, so compute-cull and LOD timings carry per-vendor variance. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-11"></a>**WGPU-11** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; performance) — Chrome 144 (published 2026-01-07) made writeBuffer and writeTexture up to 2x faster, shipped the WGSL subgroup_id and uniform_buffer_standard_layout extensions (uniform buffers may use storage-buffer layout rules), and began rolling out WebGPU on Linux starting with Intel Gen12+. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-144> — "Performance gains up to 2X better than the previous version, depending on the size of data"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-11](#wgpu-11)):** PARTIAL — 433 queue.writeBuffer/writeTexture and 52 copyExternalImageToTexture call sites under Renderer/WebGPU benefit automatically; zero hits for uniform_buffer_standard_layout or subgroup_id; CameraUniforms.wgsl:24-44 is already vec4-packed with no implicit pads, EffectsUniforms.wgsl:50 uses array<vec4<f32>,8> (already 16-byte stride). **Gap:** The upload paths benefit for free, but uniform_buffer_standard_layout is unused and the fork's UB structs already avoid stride padding so the remaining win is small. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-12"></a>**WGPU-12** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Chrome 142 (published 2025-10-22) shipped texture-formats-tier1 (r16unorm/rg16unorm/rgba16unorm families with render/storage capabilities, tier2 adding read-write storage) and the WGSL primitive_index fragment built-in, which counts from zero and resets per instance. Source: <https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-webgpu-142> — "The primitive_index is a built-in WGSL value that uniquely identifies the current primitive being processed by a fragment shader."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-12](#wgpu-12)):** PARTIAL — texture-formats-tier1 requested (WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:50); r16unorm family gated at WebGPUContext.ts:283-296 (external-image copy) and WebGPUMipmapGenerator.ts:60-64,115-124; primitive_index chunk (csm_primitiveIndex.wgsl) + WebGPUPrimitiveIndexUtils.ts serve only scene.triangulationDebug (WebGPUContext.ts:1966-1972); heightmaps stay r32float + float32-filterable (WebGPUContext.ts:3578-3584). **Gap:** No per-triangle pick path and no r16unorm heightmap/water-mask render or storage targets; primitive_index is treated as core with no enable directive. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-13"></a>**WGPU-13** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; platform) — Firefox 155 (released 2026-09-01) added the dual-source-blending WebGPU feature on desktop (src1 / one-minus-src1 / src1-alpha / one-minus-src1-alpha blend factors and the WGSL dual_source_blending extension). Source: <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/155> — "The WebGPU API now supports the dual-source-blending feature on desktop, which can be requested in GPUAdapter.requestDevice()."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-13](#wgpu-13)):** MISSING — dual-source-blending is requested (WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:55) and exposed (WebGPUContext.ts:7016) but WebGPUOIT.ts:9-12 states the module does not implement the dual-source variant; zero hits for @blend_src / dual_source_blending in WGSL; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:815 (WGF-2 NOT wired) and :1323 (backlog). **Gap:** Single-pass dual-source OIT / premultiplied composite is not wired, leaving the FAR-003 MRT path (itself unreachable for standard translucency) as the only OIT route. (value HIGH, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-14"></a>**WGPU-14** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; platform) — Per the gpuweb Implementation Status wiki (last updated 2026-08-13): Chrome ships WebGPU on Mac/Windows/ChromeOS (113), Android (121; Imagination 139; Samsung Xclipse targeted 154), Linux Intel Gen12+ (144) and Linux NVIDIA/Wayland (147); Firefox ships on Windows (141) and Apple Silicon macOS (145 on macOS 26, 147 on all macOS) with Linux/Android still Nightly-only; Safari ships on macOS/iOS/iPadOS/visionOS 26. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/wiki/Implementation-Status> — "Mozilla expects to ship on Linux in 2026."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-14](#wgpu-14)):** MISSING — probe-runtime.mjs:431-445 launches Edge only and notes Playwright Firefox is Nightly without WebGPU; capture-and-diff.mjs:691-699 and variant-smoke-test.mjs:655 accept --browser firefox but only Playwright's bundled build; ARCHITECTURE.md:966-968 lists Firefox/Safari coverage as open. **Gap:** No stable-Firefox (wgpu) or Safari (WebKit) WebGPU lane; a non-Playwright driver (WebDriver BiDi / system Firefox) is needed to catch backend-specific WGSL/validation differences. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-15"></a>**WGPU-15** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + CONFIRMED; platform) — Safari 26.2 (published 2025-12-12) made WebXR usable with WebGPU on visionOS and allowed GPUTexture objects to be used directly as depth-stencil and resolve attachments; the gpuweb 2026-04-01 minutes confirm the WebXR/WebGPU binding is shipped to stable on Vision Pro and prototyped in Chromium. Source: <https://webkit.org/blog/17640/webkit-features-for-safari-26-2/> — "Now with Safari 26.2 on visionOS, WebXR supports WebGPU."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-15](#wgpu-15)):** MISSING — Zero hits for WebXR/XRGPUBinding; Scene.js:2915-2924 throws for useWebVR when supportsStereoViewport is false, which WebGPUContext.ts:1960-1962 hard-codes; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1366-1370 (NEW-WEBGPU-STEREO-VIEWPORT, generic multi-View/stereo scheduling open). **Gap:** No per-eye viewport, per-eye UniformState/previousViewProjection, or XR binding on the WebGPU path. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-wgpu-16"></a>**WGPU-16** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — The bindless proposal (Draft, created 2025-10-13) introduces a separate GPUResourceTable object rather than extending bind groups: a sparse heterogeneous table with insert/update/remove, a single active table per encoder, per-resource usage state via setResourceUsage(), and default resources returned for invalid indices; the 2026-03-10 WGSL minutes place other features (e.g. barycentrics) in Milestone 2 after bindless. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/blob/main/proposals/bindless.md> — "a variable-size, sparse and (totally or partially) heterogenous set of bindings"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-16](#wgpu-16)):** MISSING — Zero hits for bindless / ResourceTable / binding_array; imagery is fixed-slot with MAX_IMAGERY_LAYERS = 16 (WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTypes.ts:319) plus 12 non-imagery textures (:330) and a 4-slot reduced layout on default-limit adapters (GlobeTerrain.wgsl:522-535); ShaderDefineHi (WebGPUShaderDefines.ts:942-1117) has free bits for a future variant. **Gap:** No resource-table design or define slot; imagery/material textures remain per-tile bind groups with multi-pass beyond 16 (or 4) layers (the claim's 12-slot figure is the non-imagery budget, not the imagery cap). (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-wgpu-17"></a>**WGPU-17** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; architecture) — Dawn (Chromium's WebGPU) already carries a ResourceTable frontend implementation in main (src/dawn/native/ResourceTable.h, copyright 2025) with active commits through August 2026 (e.g. 2026-08-20 "Avoid dirtying chains for ResourceTable textures", 2026-07-23 "Rename ResourceTable.insert/removeBinding"), so bindless is being prototyped, not just specified. Source: <https://dawn.googlesource.com/dawn/+/refs/heads/main/src/dawn/native/ResourceTable.h> — "ResourceTableBase implements the frontend tracking for GPUResourceTable, a sparse array of heterogeneous resources that can be accessed in shaders."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-17](#wgpu-17)):** MISSING — Zero hits for texture_and_sampler_let; shader chunks bind textures as module-scope vars (GlobeTerrain.wgsl:518-537); probe-runtime.mjs:431 EDGE_LAUNCH_ARGS is only --enable-unsafe-webgpu with no Canary/flag lane. **Gap:** No Canary prototype lane and no WGSL groundwork (texture-as-let) for a bindless imagery/material path. (value LOW, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-18"></a>**WGPU-18** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; feature) — Mesh shaders remain outside WebGPU: gpuweb issue #3015 (opened 2022-06-07) is still open and labeled Milestone 4+, with no spec proposal; by contrast wgpu v30.0.0 (2026-07-01) added full native mesh-shader support including WGSL, which is not exposed to the web. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/issues/3015> — "Milestone 4+"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-18](#wgpu-18)):** PARTIAL — No mesh-shader code (only a Naga comment at WebGPUBindGroupReflection.ts:135); compute cull + indirect draws exist (WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:385-400 writes the indirect buffer; WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:257-273); ARCHITECTURE.md:826-833 says the Phase 8b GPU-resident stack is genuinely unbuilt; CLAUDE.md research-dispatch bullet pre-registers the meshlet feasibility spike. **Gap:** The fork is correctly aligned (no mesh-shader dependency) but cluster/meshlet-level culling with an indirect endpoint is still a design, not code. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-wgpu-19"></a>**WGPU-19** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE + CONFIRMED; feature) — Multi-draw indirect is still non-standard on the web: Dawn exposes it as wgpu::FeatureName::MultiDrawIndirect with multiDrawIndirect/multiDrawIndexedIndirect taking a maxDrawCount plus optional count buffer, executing min(maxDrawCount, countBuffer) draws; on the web it is Chrome's chromium-experimental-multi-draw-indirect behind the unsafe-WebGPU flag, and the gpuweb tracking issue #5175 (maxDrawIndirectCount limit) sits at Milestone 3. Source: <https://dawn.googlesource.com/dawn/+/HEAD/docs/dawn/features/multi_draw_indirect.md> — "The number of draw calls executed is min(maxDrawCount, countBuffer)"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-19](#wgpu-19)):** PARTIAL — Indirect draws are live (WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:689-691, WebGPUPointCloudEyeDomeLighting.ts:1193) but WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:257-273,387 loops drawIndirect per entry; _drawCountBuffer is CPU-written only (:236) and never consumed by a draw; zero hits for multiDrawIndirect / chromium-experimental-multi-draw-indirect; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:581 SCAFFOLDED, :1324 backlog. **Gap:** No MDI feature check or GPU count-buffer endpoint, so culler output still needs a per-draw JS loop. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-20"></a>**WGPU-20** (KEPT; CONFIRMED + CONFIRMED; performance) — The timestamp-query feature records pass begin/end and writeTimestamp values in nanoseconds, but the spec mandates that the values be quantized to a lower precision for security/privacy, so GPU pass timings from the browser are coarser than native. Source: <https://gpuweb.github.io/gpuweb/#timestamp> — "To mitigate security and privacy concerns, the timing query values are aligned to a lower precision"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-20](#wgpu-20)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:9-16 uses timestampWrites, rolling-window avg/min/max (:44-56,:180), readback ring with ringSaturated flag (:113-121); WebGPUTimestampAccounting.ts:62 only mentions sub-nanosecond residue; zero hits for quantization/noise floor in the profiler, CesiumDebug.gpuPassCost (CesiumDebug.js:496-498) or the gpu-timestamp specs. **Gap:** Aggregation exists but no report states the browser's timestamp quantization floor, so short passes are presented at face value. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-wgpu-21"></a>**WGPU-21** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Render bundles are the spec's mechanism for amortizing JavaScript draw-call overhead: commands encoded once via GPURenderBundleEncoder.finish() are replayed with executeBundles() across passes and frames, best when the same objects are drawn the same way with only buffer contents changing. Source: <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/GPURenderBundle> — "Reusing pre-recoded commands can significantly improve app performance in situations where JavaScript draw call overhead is a bottleneck."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-21](#wgpu-21)):** PARTIAL — WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts (cache/eviction/freeze) and WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:597-613 replay command.bundle; the only live producer is the Moon environment pass (WebGPUEnvironmentRenderer.js:1879-1943); FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:554 records the globe inline bundle removed as net-negative (Batch 292), :1224 TILE-ARCH-02 and :1315-1318 R-7a..d open; row :895 ("3 active sites") is stale vs code (no fog bundle hits). **Gap:** Retained terrain tiles and static 3D Tiles content are not bundled; the dynamic-offset tile UB design bakes offsets into bundles, which is what made the globe bundle uncacheable. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-wgpu-22"></a>**WGPU-22** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Multi-view is being redesigned in the working group: the 2026-04-01 meeting agreed to close the old multiple-viewports (#4806) and multi-view rendering (#4109) issues in favor of a dynamic layer/viewport selection proposal (#4812, vertex shader picks among up to 16 array layers and 16 viewports) plus a new vertex-amplification bug, with variable-rate rendering judged irreconcilable across fragment-density-map and shading-rate-attachment hardware. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/wiki/GPU-Web-2026%E2%80%9004%E2%80%9001> — "up to 16 array layer + 16 viewports"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-22](#wgpu-22)):** PARTIAL — Layered targets exist for CSM (WebGPUCSMRenderer.ts:476-495 per-layer views + 2d-array receive view); split-screen is separate contexts via ContextRegistry (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:502); UniformState is single-view (UniformState.js:141-183); WebGPU stereo unsupported (WebGPUContext.ts:1960; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1366-1370). **Gap:** No per-view UniformState or layer/viewport-select structure; each view is a separate context or pass. (value LOW, effort L).

<a id="census-wgpu-23"></a>**WGPU-23** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — WebGPU Inspector (brendan-duncan/webgpu_inspector) is a Chrome/Firefox DevTools extension that inspects live GPU objects with creation stack traces, captures a frame's commands with per-pass outputs and buffer/texture contents, supports live shader editing, records multi-frame captures to self-contained HTML/binary files, and plots CPU submit vs GPU time. Source: <https://github.com/brendan-duncan/webgpu_inspector> — "Inspection, profiling, and graphics debugging browser extension for WebGPU."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-23](#wgpu-23)):** MISSING — Zero hits for webgpu_inspector / frame capture under Tools, Renderer/WebGPU or DEBUGGING_GUIDE.md (only the Cesium Inspector widgets at :84-85,:1435-1441); evidence banks are PNG + JSON pixel diffs (capture-and-diff.mjs, Tools/visual-regression/output). **Gap:** No per-pass command/texture capture in the probe fleet; reviewers get pixels, not pass outputs. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-24"></a>**WGPU-24** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — The gpuweb Conformance Test Suite is normative and embeddable in WPT or runnable standalone in the browser; implementations must pass it to be conformant, and Dawn rolls it continuously (recent Dawn commits are 'Roll third_party/webgpu-cts'). Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/cts> — "The contents of this test suite are considered normative; implementations must pass them to be WebGPU-conformant."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-24](#wgpu-24)):** PARTIAL — Zero hits for webgpu-cts / gpuweb/cts; the fork has its own runner homes (package.json:155-166 node --test groups, :181-182 test-webgpu Karma on EdgeHeadlessCI, :185 Playwright e2e) and the no-spec-without-runner-home rule (CLAUDE.md R-2026-08-29-1). **Gap:** No CTS subset or CTS-derived expectations file pins the validation behaviours the fork relies on across Chrome/Firefox/Safari. (value LOW, effort M).

<a id="census-wgpu-25"></a>**WGPU-25** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — WebGPU/WGSL still has no double precision: the WGSL CRD defines only f32 and f16 (f16 behind the f16 extension), and the f64 request (gpuweb #2805, opened 2022-04-28, explicitly citing GIS) remains open at Milestone 4+ with no proposal. Source: <https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/issues/2805> — "Double precision floats (IEEE-754 binary64) are useful for various applications"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [WGPU-25](#wgpu-25)):** HAS — csm_translateRelativeToEye.wgsl:1-40 and CameraUniforms.wgsl:33-43 (high/low camera + RTE matrices); RTE helpers present in 104 WGSL files; df64 two-float scaffold at ComputeInstanceScaffold.wgsl:33-61; ARCHITECTURE.md:310-317 codifies the rule. **Gap:** None structurally; df64 emulation is opt-in and limited to the compute-instance kernel. (value HIGH, effort S).

### A.4 CesiumJS upstream + the geospatial field

25 claims: 25 KEPT, 0 REFUTED, 0 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 10 / PARTIAL 11 / MISSING 4 / NOT-APPLICABLE 0.

<a id="census-c01"></a>**C01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; platform) — CesiumJS 1.145 is the latest upstream release (GitHub release published 2026-09-01T18:51:41Z). Highlights: vector-tile draping onto 3D Tiles, a rewritten ClippingPolygons algorithm with hole support, and an experimental server-side IonSnapService. Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/releases/tag/1.145> — "`ClippingPolygons` now use an algorithm, based on the techniques used for vector tiles, that vastly improves quality across distance scales. Warm-up cost is also modestly decreased."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C01](#c01)):** PARTIAL — Fork is synced to v1.144 only: commit 06bc894d30 'Batch 958: upstream v1.144 sync (R-2026-08-10-6) - 57 commits merged'; CHANGES.md:21 top section is '## 1.144 - 2026-08-01'; no 1.145 sync commit. WebGPU clipping-polygon parity exists and is live against the 1.144 algorithm: packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUClippingPolygonCollection.ts (SDF atlas) + packages/engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/PolygonSignedDistance.wgsl, consumed by GlobeTerrain.wgsl / ModelPBRComplete.wgsl (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:627,684 SHIPPED). No hole support: grep -i 'hole' over Scene/ClippingPolygon.js, ClippingPolygonCollection.js, WebGPUClippingPolygonCollection.ts, PolygonSignedDistance.wgsl finds only a doc comment at ClippingPolygon.js:160. **Gap:** The 1.145 sync has not been taken, so the rewritten ClippingPolygons algorithm with holes must be merged and then re-derived into the fork's SDF-atlas WGSL path (which currently has no hole concept) before the parity gate can be re-run. (value HIGH, effort L).

<a id="census-c02"></a>**C02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; platform) — Upstream CesiumJS still has no WebGPU renderer: the tracking issue #4989 ('WebGPU renderer', opened 2017-02-10) is Open as of 2026-09-02, and no upstream WebGPU PR surfaced in search of the 1.13x-1.14x cycle. Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/issues/4989> — "This is a long-term roadmap issue to track development of NXT, the NeXT generation graphics API for the Web"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C02](#c02)):** HAS — packages/engine/Source/Renderer/GraphicsContext.ts:728 'export abstract class GraphicsContext', :919 abstract rendererType, :742-746 global ContextRegistry; packages/engine/Source/Renderer/Context.js:394 'class Context extends GraphicsContext'; packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:474 'export class WebGPUContext extends GraphicsContext'; FeatureRendererKey.js carries 52 enumerated keys; migration_doc/ARCHITECTURE.md §1 'Dual-Backend Model' / §3 'Feature Renderer Pattern'. The one-way port cost is visible in practice: Batch 959 (3c076961e0) filed NEW-WEBGPU-VECTOR-POLYGON-DRAPING as WebGL-only right after the 1.144 sync. **Gap:** No mechanism gap - the abstraction is fork-owned and live; the standing cost is that every upstream GLSL/DrawCommand change remains a manual WGSL port with no upstream convergence target. (value HIGH, effort S).

<a id="census-c03"></a>**C03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Upstream's glTF/3D Tiles model renderer is a three-level pipeline-stage architecture (model, node, primitive stages) where each stage's static process() mutates RenderResources wrapping a ShaderBuilder before ModelDrawCommands.buildModelDrawCommand emits the DrawCommand. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/Model/ModelSceneGraph.js> — "an in memory representation of the scene graph for a {@link Model}"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C03](#c03)):** PARTIAL — Upstream stage list retained: packages/engine/Source/Scene/Model/ModelRuntimePrimitive.js:357-361 pushes EdgeVisibilityPipelineStage into pipelineStages. The WGSL twins exist (Scene/Model/CustomShaderWGSLPipelineStage.js, MetadataWGSLPipelineStage.js, MetadataWGSLHelpers.js) but are NOT entries in that stage list: they are imported and invoked from the feature renderer - WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:81,91 and WebGPUModelPipelineCache.ts:119,2327,2709,2988 - and ModelRuntimePrimitive.js has zero references to either WGSL stage (grep). **Gap:** WGSL stages are renderer-side codegen helpers rather than entries in the same pipelineStages traversal, so a new upstream stage (planar fill, edge visibility variants) gets no WGSL slot automatically and Principle 5 parity must be hand-wired per stage. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-c04"></a>**C04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Upstream shader composition is string assembly: ShaderBuilder accumulates #defines, dynamically generated structs/functions and GLSL lines for VS/FS, and ShaderSource resolves czm_ built-ins via a dependency scan, prepends #define lines and #version 300 es for WebGL2. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Renderer/ShaderBuilder.js> — "Add a #define macro to one or both of the shaders. These lines will appear at the top of the final shader source."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C04](#c04)):** HAS — packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUShaderDefines.ts (1320 lines; lo word plus hi-word registry, definesHi / defineHiKeyToNames at :1280-1303), WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts:2-15 ('//>>ifdef', '//>>else', '//>>endif' pure function over (source, defines)), WebGPUShaderModuleCache.ts (269 lines, per-device getOrCreate keyed on sourceId+defines+definesHi+keySalt); ARCHITECTURE.md §6.1-6.3. Upstream ShaderBuilder/ShaderSource retained unchanged for the WebGL lane. **Gap:** None - the fork's bitmask + preprocessor + module cache is the live WGSL composition authority and already exceeds upstream's 32-bit assumption via the hi-word registry. (value HIGH, effort S).

<a id="census-c05"></a>**C05** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Upstream caches compiled shader programs in ShaderCache keyed by vertex cache key + fragment cache key + sorted attribute locations, with reference counting and deferred destruction of released programs. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Renderer/ShaderCache.js> — "Returns a shader program from the cache, or creates and caches a new shader program, given the GLSL vertex and fragment shader source and attribute locations."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C05](#c05)):** HAS — WebGL: packages/engine/Source/Renderer/ShaderCache.js:21 _shadersToRelease, :442-461 destroyReleasedShaderPrograms with reference counting (upstream mechanism intact). WebGPU: packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:53 webgpuObjectIdentity (WeakMap identity), :396-397 'sh:<vsId>.<vsEntry>/<fsId>.<fsEntry>' segment in generateCacheKey (:550,:653,:708), :355/:460 stats.wrongModuleHits canary initialised to 0; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:543 (Batch 825 fold, Edge-verified Batch 828, 0 collisions). **Gap:** None - the WebGPU key is strictly stronger than upstream's (folds module identity + layout + primitive + depth/stencil + multisample); keep wrongModuleHits=0 as the canary when porting upstream shader changes. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-c06"></a>**C06** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — Upstream emulates 64-bit positions on the GPU via EncodedCartesian3, splitting each double into fixed-point high/low float32 parts written as interleaved high-precision vertex attributes (the 'Precisions, Precisions' RTE scheme). Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Core/EncodedCartesian3.js> — "Encodes a 64-bit floating-point value as two floating-point values that, when converted to 32-bit floating-point and added, approximate the original input."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C06](#c06)):** HAS — 170 files under packages/engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU contain 'positionHigh' and 170 contain 'encodedCameraHigh'; 75 .wgsl files carry previousViewProjection (DP-H41 tail). packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURTEAssertions.ts:6-7 guards the high/low packer slot bugs. The Buffer* collections (Scene/BufferPolyline|Polygon|Point|PrimitiveCollection.js + renderBuffer*Collection.js, FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:296-298) have WebGPU renderers that carry the encoding: WebGPUBufferPolylineRenderer.ts (12 positionHigh hits), WebGPUBufferPolygonRenderer.ts (12), WebGPUBufferPointRenderer.ts (15); renderBufferPolylineCollection.js:33-43 declares positionHigh/positionLow attributes for both backends. ARCHITECTURE.md §5 is the rule. **Gap:** None for shipped producers; the 1.145 draped-onto-3D-Tiles vector primitives are not yet in the fork (see C15), so their RTE carry-over into WGSL is future sync work. (value HIGH, effort S).

<a id="census-c07"></a>**C07** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Upstream Gaussian splat rendering sorts on the CPU side: GaussianSplatSorter runs a WebAssembly radix sort (wasm_splats_bg.wasm) inside TaskProcessor workers (concurrency = hardwareConcurrency-1) and returns a sorted index list, transferring position buffers to the worker. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/GaussianSplatSorter.js> — "Sorts Gaussian splats using a radix sort algorithm. Sorted by distance from the camera."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C07](#c07)):** PARTIAL — Scene/GaussianSplatSorter.js (upstream WASM radix sort in TaskProcessor workers) is retained and shared: FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:624 records C15-G2 hoisting the WASM sort above the backend branch and C15-G3/G4 making WebGPU consume 'primitive._indexes' with a sequence guard at the buffer-upload boundary. The GPU/compute alternative is explicitly deferred: WEBGPU_MIGRATION_BACKLOG.md:799 'Gaussian Splat sort ... Radix sort on GPU (similar to PointCloudSort) 2-3 days'; DEFERRED_WORK.md:2047 'The GPU/compute radix sort stays deferred'. Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/GPUSortKeys.wgsl is a draw-command sort-key generator (header lines 1-16), not a splat sorter. **Gap:** WebGPU still rides the worker+WASM round-trip for splat order; a backend-specific compute sort inside the GAUSSIAN_SPLAT feature renderer (WebGL keeping WASM) has not been built. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-c08"></a>**C08** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — GaussianSplatPrimitive aggregates all selected tiles' splat attributes into one primitive, packs them into an RGBA unsigned-integer texture read by splat ID in the vertex shader, renders with pre-multiplied alpha blending (depth test on, depth write off, cull off) and re-sorts only when the camera moves beyond thresholds. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/GaussianSplatPrimitive.js> — "The texture uses an RGBA unsigned-integer format and nearest-neighbor sampling."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C08](#c08)):** HAS — packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:1182-1193 - blend color/alpha {srcFactor:'one', dstFactor:'one-minus-src-alpha'} (premultiplied), primitive cullMode 'none', depthWriteEnabled:false, depthCompare 'less-equal'; data layout is the WASM generate_splat_texture record consumed verbatim as an array<u32> storage buffer (Option B, ShaderDefineHi.SPLAT_PACKED_WASM) instead of the RGBA uint texture, SH bands via a group-0 binding-4 storage buffer (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:624; DEFERRED_WORK.md:1924 C15-G3 correction). Camera-threshold re-sort is shared scene code: Scene/GaussianSplatPrimitive.js:159 '~0.5 degree camera direction change threshold before triggering steady re-sort'. Pixel-confirmed on Edge at Batch 881 (splatCmds=1/1, 27/27 splats). **Gap:** The storage-buffer replacement is already made and live; what remains open is the terminal parity gate C15-G8 and the reachable-but-unverified multi-frustum/classification rows C15-G6/G7 (G7 first ran at Batch 1364 with two WebGPU reds). (value HIGH, effort M).

<a id="census-c09"></a>**C09** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; geospatial) — KHR_gaussian_splatting (Khronos glTF extension; status 'Release Candidate' on the spec page as of 2026-09-02, announced 2026-02-03) stores splats as attributes on POINTS primitives (POSITION, ROTATION, SCALE, OPACITY, SH_DEGREE_l_COEF_n), requires back-to-front sorting + alpha blending, and mandates that compression extensions (SPZ etc.) extend it. Source: <https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_gaussian_splatting> — "The mode property of the mesh primitive MUST be POINTS (0)."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C09](#c09)):** HAS — Scene/GltfLoader.js:2328-2330 reads extensions.KHR_gaussian_splatting and KHR_gaussian_splatting_compression_spz_2; Scene/Model/ModelUtility.js:386-387 lists both as supported; Scene/GltfVertexBufferLoader.js:435-441 recognises 'KHR_gaussian_splatting:ROTATION' alongside the legacy '_ROTATION'/'_SCALE' semantics; Scene/GltfSpzLoader.js:46,73 derives SH degree from the highest 'SH_DEGREE_n_COEF_' attribute. C18-S0 licence pass DONE 2026-08-09 (QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:273, 20 projects vetted in GSPLAT_REFERENCE_VETTING_2026-08-09.md). **Gap:** Loader supports both the namespaced and underscore semantics, but the attribute set should be re-checked against the Release Candidate text (OPACITY naming, POINTS-mode fallback to point-cloud rendering) at the 1.145 sync. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-c10"></a>**C10** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — 3DTILES_content_voxels (Draft, on the CesiumGS/3d-tiles 'voxels' branch; the main-branch URL 404s) declares voxel content whose payload is glTF with EXT_primitive_voxels plus EXT_structural_metadata; grids are defined by dimensions and padding and may use box, region or cylinder bounding volumes. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/3d-tiles/voxels/extensions/3DTILES_content_voxels/README.md> — "Voxels are stored as glTFs with the `EXT_primitive_voxels` extension and are typically paired with `EXT_structural_metadata`"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C10](#c10)):** HAS — Scene/Cesium3DTilesVoxelProvider.js:38-39,82-83,108-109 paddingBefore/paddingAfter and EXT_primitive_voxels handling (also Cesium3DTileContentType.js, ModelUtility.js); WebGPU honours padding: Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUVoxelDataUpload.ts:54,106-107,124-125 (paddingBefore/After in the upload record and u_paddingBefore twin). Cylinder shape live on WebGPU: WebGPUVoxelRenderer.ts:337-347 'shapeType 2 = CYLINDER' with u_cylinderRenderRadiusMinMax / UvAngleRangeOrigin terms, Shaders/WebGPU/Voxels/VoxelIntersection.wgsl:46 csm_intersectCylinder; gate probe-voxel-cylinder.mjs (DEFERRED_WORK.md:879,8110-8121 NEW-VOXEL-CYLINDER-SHAPEUV). **Gap:** Padding and cylinder are implemented; the residual is that non-BOX refinement is root-only and octree traversal stops at level 2 (C11-100, XL, sliced), and no cross-runtime comparison against cesium-native 0.56's voxel output has been banked. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-c11"></a>**C11** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Upstream VoxelPrimitive renders voxels by ray marching with a user-tunable step-size multiplier over an octree encoded in internal/leaf node textures and megatextures holding tile data; box/ellipsoid/cylinder shapes and a default-generated CustomShader are supported; the API is explicitly experimental. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/VoxelPrimitive.js> — "The step size multiplier used during raymarching. The lower the value, the higher the rendering quality, but also the worse the performance."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C11](#c11)):** PARTIAL — WebGPUVoxelRenderer.ts:229 'stepSize: f32' in the uniform struct, :830-837 ray-start dithered by u.stepSize (step-size control preserved); box/ellipsoid/cylinder shapes (:308-347); depth-2 octree + LRU atlas + native-WGSL user customShaders (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:622-623 '2026-07-31 voxel status correction ... WIP / PARTIAL'). Still unreachable on WebGPU: the PR#13517 metadata-typed default shader and GLSL customShader transpile (inventory :622; DEFERRED_WORK.md:9873 'GLSL-only voxel customShaders keep warn+default-gray'). C18-A2 empty-space skip is pending on C11-100 (QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:247,292). **Gap:** WebGPU voxel march matches upstream's fixed-step model but cannot yet render the default-generated metadata CustomShader or GLSL user shaders, traversal stops at level 2, and the additive empty-space skip (C18-A2) is not started. (value HIGH, effort L).

<a id="census-c12"></a>**C12** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Scene.snap (experimental, 1.144; surfacePosition added in 1.145) implements snap-to-geometry picking by reusing pickBegin/pickEnd with a dedicated RGBA32F framebuffer whose pixels carry pick ID, an is-edge flag and linear eye depth; only Model-pipeline commands with a snapId render, via a DerivedCommand-generated snap shader, and edges are preferred over surfaces. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/packages/engine/Source/Scene/Snapping.js> — "an offscreen pick render (it reuses the pick render machinery via `pickBegin`/`pickEnd`) that targets a dedicated RGBA32F framebuffer instead of the RGBA8 pick framebuffer."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C12](#c12)):** PARTIAL — Contrary to the claim's framing, the fork's WebGPU snap tier exists and is live: Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSnapPayload.ts:59 'SNAP_PAYLOAD_FORMAT = "rg32uint"' (compact two-word payload instead of upstream's RGBA32F), WebGPUSnapFramebuffer.ts:29 occluder+payload schedule, fragmentSnapMain in ModelPBRComplete.wgsl, edge payload via WebGPUEdgeVisibilityEmitter (Batch 821), GraphicsContext.createSnapFramebuffer factory; Scene.js:3647-3648,6089,6120 gate on command.snapId for both backends. Status authority QUEUE_2026-07-18_CAMPAIGN11.md:1436-1477 'C11-212 remains PARTIAL'; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1057 lists owed gates (SCENE2D slice depth, moving camera/cursor across DPR/projection, edge isEdge browser gate on both backends). 1.145 'surfacePosition' is absent from Scene/Snapping.js (grep hits only DynamicEnvironmentMapManager.js:741). **Gap:** The WebGPU snap path is implemented but its remaining browser gates (edge isEdge on both backends, SCENE2D, genuinely moving camera) are owed, and the 1.145 surfacePosition addition has not been ported. (value HIGH, effort M).

<a id="census-c13"></a>**C13** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Edge rendering for EXT_mesh_primitive_edge_visibility uses a dedicated EdgeFramebuffer with MRT attachments (edge colour, edge type + feature ID, packed depth); 1.144 cut its JS heap use (up to 19x per the release blog) and the unreleased 1.146 section fixes a GPU memory leak where the edge vertex array was never destroyed. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/CHANGES.md> — "Fixed a GPU memory leak where the edge vertex array created for `EXT_mesh_primitive_edge_visibility` rendering was never destroyed when draw commands were rebuilt or the model was destroyed."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C13](#c13)):** PARTIAL — Both backends have the MRT edge target: Scene/EdgeFramebuffer.js, Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUEdgeFramebuffer.ts, WebGPUEdgeVisibilityEmitter.ts (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:439). 1.144 heap reduction present: CHANGES.md:38 (typed-array edge accessors). WebGPU has an explicit lifetime path: WebGPUEdgeVisibilityEmitter.ts:1519 destroyEdgePrimitiveResources, called from WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:9083. WebGL does NOT: EdgeVisibilityPipelineStage.js:1055-1062 creates the edge VertexArray and ModelDrawCommand.js:900 assigns it to edgeCommand.vertexArray, but grep for 'edgeGeometry' outside those two files returns nothing and ModelDrawCommand.js contains no destroy call - the 1.146 leak is present in the fork's WebGL lane. **Gap:** Port the 1.146 edge-vertex-array destroy into the WebGL command rebuild/destroy path (the WebGPU emitter already frees its buffers), keeping the two backends' resource lifetimes in step. (value MEDIUM, effort S).

<a id="census-c14"></a>**C14** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — CesiumJS 1.144 fixed a one-frame black flash caused by Framebuffer construction leaving the WebGL context's framebuffer-binding cache stale, so later draws in the frame targeted the wrong framebuffer; it also fixed a fatal error when a post-process stage selected more features than the maximum texture size. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium/main/CHANGES.md> — "Fixed a one-frame black flash caused by `Framebuffer` construction leaving the context's framebuffer binding cache stale, making subsequent draws render to the wrong framebuffer for the remainder of the frame."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C14](#c14)):** PARTIAL — The framebuffer-binding-cache fix landed with the 1.144 sync: packages/engine/Source/Renderer/Framebuffer.js:293 'context._currentFramebuffer = undefined;' (git log -S: commit 1b2d11c4e6 'Sync framebuffer binding cache in Framebuffer constructor'), against the cache at Context.js:245-246,726,1366; CHANGES.md:20 carries the entry. The post-process 'more features than maximum texture size' fix could not be located: no matching CHANGES.md entry (grep -i 'maximum texture size|selected features': 0) and no ContextLimits/maximumTextureSize guard in Scene/PostProcessStage.js or Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUUserPostProcessStage.ts (grep: 0). WebGPU selects targets per render-pass descriptor (no persistent binding cache), so no analogous stale-cache path exists. **Gap:** Confirm whether the post-process selected-features max-texture-size fix belongs to 1.144 or 1.145 and port it to both PostProcessStage.js and WebGPUUserPostProcessStage.ts; the WebGL binding-cache fix is already present. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-c15"></a>**C15** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — CesiumJS 1.145 drapes clamped vector-tile polygons and polylines onto 3D Tiles (not just terrain, which landed in 1.144) through a heightReference option on BufferPrimitiveCollection / BufferPolygonCollection / BufferPolylineCollection and on MVTDataProvider and GeoJsonPrimitive. Source: <https://github.com/CesiumGS/cesium/releases/tag/1.145> — "Added support for draping clamped vector tile polygons and polylines onto 3D Tiles, with a new `heightReference` option and matching read-only property on `BufferPrimitiveCollection`, inherited by `BufferPolygonCollection` and `BufferPolylineCollection`."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C15](#c15)):** MISSING — No heightReference option on Scene/BufferPrimitiveCollection.js, BufferPolygonCollection.js, BufferPolylineCollection.js, MVTDataProvider.js or GeoJsonPrimitive.js (grep: 0 hits) - the 1.145 3D-Tiles draping API is absent. The 1.144 terrain precursor is only partly on WebGPU: polylines via the C11-213 storage-buffer bake (WebGPUVectorTileResources.ts, GlobeTerrain.wgsl::vectorPolylineRender; FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:1013, non-regression gate F still owed) while polygon draping is WebGL-only (DEFERRED_WORK.md:14093 NEW-WEBGPU-VECTOR-POLYGON-DRAPING, Batch 959). **Gap:** The fork has neither the 1.145 heightReference API nor WebGPU polygon draping even on terrain, so the 1.145 sync will inherit two WGSL gaps plus a re-verification of the shared classification depth target (C13-41 area). (value HIGH, effort L).

<a id="census-c16"></a>**C16** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — cesium-native v0.64.0 (2026-09-01, shipped in Cesium for Unreal 2.29.1) adds 'area paging' (selecting a region of terrain tiles at a fixed LOD), generalises ViewState culling from frustums to arbitrary culling volumes, and adds BoundingVolume intersection tests. Source: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CesiumGS/cesium-native/main/CHANGES.md> — "Added support for "area paging": selection of a region of terrain tiles at a fixed level of detail."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C16](#c16)):** PARTIAL — GPU cull is frustum-only: Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:3 'Tests arrays of bounding spheres against frustum planes', :257 'Frustum planes: 6 x vec4<f32> = 96 bytes', :338-340 uploadFrustumPlanes(24 floats); WebGPUContextCullerPool.ts pools them; Batch 1375 bracketed the translucent dispatch. CPU tile selection uses upstream's CullingVolume object (Scene/GlobeSurfaceTileProvider.js:972,1048 cullingVolume.computeVisibility) which is already an arbitrary plane list on the CPU. No fixed-LOD area paging in GlobeSurfaceTileProvider.js/QuadtreePrimitive.js (grep areaPaging|fixedLevel: 0). **Gap:** The compute culler accepts exactly six planes rather than an arbitrary culling volume, and there is no fixed-LOD area selection mode to mirror cesium-native 0.64's area paging. (value LOW, effort M).

<a id="census-c17"></a>**C17** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — MapLibre GL JS's graphics-modernization roadmap is four independently shippable phases: WebGL2 immutable textures/layout qualifiers, a Drawable + Uniform Buffer Object architecture mirroring MapLibre Native, integer attributes/instancing, then a WebGPU backend (WGSL port + WebGPU render tests) sharing all non-GPU code across backends. GL JS v6.7.0 (2026-09-02) ships no WebGPU yet; maplibre-native has a WebGPU backend on Android/iOS. Source: <https://maplibre.org/roadmap/maplibre-gl-js/graphics-modernization/> — "Each backend (OpenGL, Metal, WebGPU) implements the same rendering interface."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C17](#c17)):** PARTIAL — The backend-agnostic shape is live (GraphicsContext.ts:728 abstract class; ARCHITECTURE.md §1 'Backend-agnostic Scene code (the central rule)', §3 Feature Renderer Pattern) and the render-test phase exists as Tools/visual-regression/capture-and-diff.mjs + the wave-end gate (R-2026-08-29-2). WebGPU is UBO-first (CameraUniforms chunk, Shaders/WebGPU/chunks/structs/CameraUniforms.js), but the WebGL2 lane never moved to uniform buffers: grep UNIFORM_BUFFER|uniformBlockBinding|bindBufferBase over packages/engine/Source/Renderer/*.js returns 0 hits - WebGL keeps upstream's per-uniform ShaderProgram path. **Gap:** Unlike MapLibre's sequencing, the fork's WebGL2 backend still uploads per-uniform while WebGPU uses UBOs, so there is no shared uniform-block layout across backends to keep the two shader families' uniform surfaces in lockstep. (value LOW, effort L).

<a id="census-c18"></a>**C18** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — deck.gl v9 WebGPU support (selected via luma.gl's webgpuAdapter in deviceProps) is explicitly not production-ready: only IconLayer, LineLayer, PointCloudLayer, ScatterplotLayer and PathLayer have WGSL implementations, and picking, shader hooks/extensions, post-processing and base-map integration are still WebGL-only. Source: <https://deck.gl/docs/developer-guide/webgpu> — "WebGPU support in deck.gl v9 is still a work in progress and is not production ready."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C18](#c18)):** HAS — WebGPU picking live: Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPickFramebuffer.ts, WebGPUSceneRendererPickPass.ts, WebGPUPickTransientReadback.ts (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:918 NEW-PICK-WEBGPU-DEPTH-RECONSTRUCTION SHIPPED, :921 metadata readback SHIPPED, Batch 1374 readback fix). Post-process live: WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts, WebGPUPostProcessStageCollection.ts, WebGPUUserPostProcessStage.ts, WebGPULibraryPostProcessStage.ts. Shader-hook equivalent: Scene/Model/CustomShaderWGSLPipelineStage.js:2-31 native-WGSL CustomShader path (DEFERRED_WORK.md:5971-5979 SHIPPED B473 + Q31 MODIFY/REPLACE), while a GLSL-only CustomShader 'still warns + no-ops by design' (:5975); translation hatch is the pluggable registry Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUShaderTranslator.ts:2-13 + WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:967-968 EXPERIMENTAL, :1353 STUB-NAGA). **Gap:** The fork is ahead of deck.gl on picking/post-process/metadata; the shared open problem is that GLSL CustomShaders still no-op on WebGPU because no translator is registered by default - the naga lane remains an unregistered experimental hatch. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-c19"></a>**C19** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — luma.gl 9.4 (whats-new targets August 2026; only v9.4.0-beta.6 published 2026-09-02, deck.gl 9.3.11 still pins luma.gl 9.3) makes RenderPass own draw state with support for render bundles and indirect draws, adds @binding(auto) to WGSL shader modules, and introduces an experimental @luma.gl/splats module; 9.3 (2026-03-31) added multi-canvas rendering via device.createPresentationContext(). Source: <https://luma.gl/docs/whats-new> — "RenderPass owns draw state and supports render bundles, direct draws, and indirect draws."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C19](#c19)):** PARTIAL — Render bundles: Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:547 SHIPPED; :895 three opt-in sites - environment, volumetric fog, plus globe terrain whose inline bundle was later REMOVED as net-negative, :554 Batch 292). Indirect draws: WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:195-208,357-358,527-530 drawIndirectBuffer/drawIndirectOffset path, live only in the point-cloud LOD lane (WebGPUPointCloudRenderer.ts:2431,2476 cache.lodIndirectBuffer; WebGPUPointCloudEyeDomeLighting.ts:1193 passEncoder.drawIndirect); WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts is SCAFFOLDED (inventory :581). Multi-canvas/multi-context: GraphicsContext.ts:742-746 registry.all (ARCHITECTURE.md §2). No @binding(auto) analogue - bindings are fixed tables, which is a naming convenience not a capability gap. **Gap:** Indirect draws are confined to the point-cloud LOD path and the general IndirectDrawManager is scaffold, and the globe render bundle was measured net-negative, so the fork has not yet converged on luma.gl's three-mode (direct/bundle/indirect) RenderPass model for its main draw lanes. (value MEDIUM, effort L).

<a id="census-c20"></a>**C20** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — three.js's Three Shading Language (TSL) authors shaders as JavaScript node graphs that are compiled to either WGSL (WebGPURenderer) or GLSL (WebGL fallback) from one source, with nodes able to rewrite their own output snippet and de-duplicate repeated expressions. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/wiki/Three.js-Shading-Language> — "is also capable of encoding code into different outputs such as `WGSL`/`GLSL` - `WebGPU`/`WebGL`"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C20](#c20)):** MISSING — The fork authors every shader twice by rule (CLAUDE.md Principle 5; lockstep GLSL/WGSL pairs such as SunBrightPass.wgsl <-> BrightPass.glsl, FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:711) and has no node/graph or single-source shader layer: grep 'node graph|TSL|NodeMaterial|shader graph' over FEATURE_INVENTORY.md and DEFERRED_WORK.md returns 0 rows. The only cross-language mechanism is the experimental pluggable runtime translator (WebGPUShaderTranslator.ts:2-13; WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts; inventory :967-968,:1353 EXPERIMENTAL/STUB-NAGA), which is a GLSL->WGSL transpile, not shared authoring. **Gap:** No single-source shader authoring exists, so each new feature carries a GLSL and a WGSL implementation kept honest only by spec pairs; a TSL-style layer would be a C17-scale rewrite and is not queued. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-c21"></a>**C21** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — three.js r185 (2026-07-01) added ClusteredLighting (Forward+) for WebGPURenderer: the view frustum is partitioned into screen tiles x exponential depth slices, a compute pass builds per-cluster light lists via sphere-vs-AABB tests, and materials evaluate only lights reaching the fragment (defaults 32 px tiles, 24 slices, 64 lights/cluster, 1024 max lights). Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33406> — "Adds a `ClusteredLighting` addon that partitions the view frustum into a 3D cluster grid (screen tiles × exponential depth slices). A compute pass builds a per-cluster light list via sphere-vs-AABB tests"
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C21](#c21)):** HAS — Forward+ clustered lighting SHIPPED Batches 153-155 (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:632): Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUClusterBoundsRenderer.ts + WebGPUClusterAssignRenderer.ts (16x9x24 grid compute passes) + WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts + WebGPUSceneRendererClusteredLighting.ts, ClusterBounds.wgsl / ClusterAssign.wgsl, consumed by ModelPBRComplete (group-3 bindings 18-22) and all 19 Mat*Lit shaders; limits CLUSTER_MAX_LIGHTS = 1024 (WebGPUClusterAssignRenderer.ts:37, ClusterAssign.wgsl:53) and 256 lights per cluster (:41); LTC rect/disk area lights alongside (:633). WebGPU-only: no GLSL twin (grep 'clustered' in Shaders/*.glsl: 0), WebGL stays sun-only by design. **Gap:** Already ahead of three.js r185 on capacity; residuals are the unconsumed Phong primitive shaders and the missing Sandcastle demo (inventory :632,:1271), with WebGL keeping the sun-only parity default. (value LOW, effort S).

<a id="census-c22"></a>**C22** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — three.js merged (2026-05-21) an experimental Nanite-style GPU-driven software rasterizer written in TSL compute shaders that performs triangle rasterization, culling and deferred texture sampling entirely on the GPU, bypassing the hardware pipeline, and renders 1+ billion triangles in the demo. Source: <https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/pull/33605> — "This PR introduces a experimental, GPU-Driven Software Rasterizer implemented in TSL via compute shaders."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C22](#c22)):** MISSING — No software rasterizer or meshlet path in the engine: the meshlet track is a pre-registered feasibility spike held by gates M-16 and C11-168 (migration_doc/QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:12-16,76; nothing launched). DEFERRED_WORK.md:4622 EPIC-MESHLET-VIRTUAL-GEOMETRY records the research verdict: Tier 1 compute cull + indirect draw recommended, Tier 3 visbuffer/compute SW raster 'NOT worth it on browser WebGPU today' because of the missing 64-bit atomics. Building blocks exist but are partial: WebGPUGPUCuller.ts, Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/HiZPyramid.wgsl (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:766 WIP, FORK-41), WebGPUDrawCommand.drawIndirectBuffer. **Gap:** No cluster-cull + indirect-draw path exists for triangle meshes; three.js's TSL software rasterizer demonstrates the packed-32-bit route the fork's own research dismissed for materials, so the M-16 spike should re-examine that trade-off before the Tier-1 rows are briefed. (value MEDIUM, effort XL).

<a id="census-c23"></a>**C23** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — three.js r186 gains a native Gaussian splat renderer (WebGPU + TSL, by Ben Houston) whose sort is a GPU counting sort: depth is quantized into 4096 bins and four compute passes (reset, histogram, prefix sum, scatter) run per sort; formats covered include PLY, SPLAT, SPZ, KSPLAT and glTF KHR_gaussian_splatting. (Secondary source; r186 was not yet the latest GitHub release on 2026-09-02, r185 is.) Source: <https://radiancefields.com/three.js-merges-a-native-gaussian-splat-renderer-for-webgpu-in-r186> — "quantizes splat depth into bins, 4096 by default, and dispatches four TSL compute passes from compute(renderer), a reset, a histogram, a prefix sum and a scatter."
**Fork (first pass, tip `afdf5f4909`; §3 row [C23](#c23)):** MISSING — No compute sort for splats: WEBGPU_MIGRATION_BACKLOG.md:799 'Gaussian Splat sort ... Radix sort on GPU (similar to PointCloudSort) 2-3 days' (deferred); DEFERRED_WORK.md:2047 'The GPU/compute radix sort stays deferred'; the live order comes from the shared WASM radix-sort permutation with a sequence guard (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:624, C15-G4). Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/GPUSortKeys.wgsl is a draw-command key generator (header :1-16), not wired to splats. C18-S3 pre-registers GPUSorting (MIT) as its only allowed reference (QUEUE_2026-08-09_CAMPAIGN18.md:273). **Gap:** A four-pass counting/radix sort on storage buffers inside the WebGPU splat feature renderer does not exist, so WebGPU keeps the worker transfer and multi-frame re-sort latency of the WASM path. (value MEDIUM, effort M).

<a id="census-c24"></a>**C24** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; tooling) — three.js r185 (2026-07-01) surfaces uncaptured GPU errors and WGSL compilation diagnostics from WebGPURenderer, adds WebXR support on the WebGPU backend, and caches TSL function nodes per renderer backend. However no uncaptured-error listener exists anywhere: grep -ri 'uncapturederror' over packages/engine/Source returns 0 hits, despite the CLAUDE.md sentinel doctrine. WebXR on WebGPU is absent: no navigator.xr/XRGPUBinding/XRWebGLLayer in engine or widgets (grep: 0); Scene.js:1287-1290 useWebVR defaults false and VRButton (FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:483) is the legacy split-viewport path. **Gap:** Register a permanent device 'uncapturederror' sentinel (console.error with context ID) so validation errors outside error scopes reach the console; WebXR-on-WebGPU is a separate, larger absent feature. <a id="census-c25"></a>**C25** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; tooling) — CesiumJS rebuilt Sandcastle as a separate TypeScript workspace package inside the monorepo, using Vite (React plugin) with esbuild as fallback, React + iTwinUI, and the Monaco editor; the new app replaced the legacy Sandcastle in October 2025 and gained AI semantic search (2026-03) and Sandcastle Copilot (2026-07).
