### A.5 PlayCanvas engine 2.x

25 claims: 25 KEPT, 0 REFUTED, 0 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 5 / PARTIAL 12 / MISSING 6 / NOT-APPLICABLE 2. Target as recorded by the collector: PlayCanvas engine 2.x WebGPU (github.com/playcanvas/engine); latest version v2.21.4 (2026-08-13).

<a id="census-pc-01"></a>**PC-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — PlayCanvas renders through an explicit frame graph whose nodes are FramePass objects; RenderPass is the GPU-render-target subclass, and non-rendering passes (compute dispatches) are first-class nodes. FrameGraph.compile() merges adjacent passes via per-pass _skipStart/_skipEnd flags. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/frame-pass.js> — "A frame pass represents a node in the frame graph. It encapsulates a unit of work that executes during frame rendering. Subclasses include {@link RenderPass} for GPU render passes with render targets, and non-rendering passes for compute dispatches or other tasks."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — `packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:2170-2300` (beginFrame lifecycle: encoder + on-demand default pass), `:2586-2640` (beginRenderPass), `WebGPUSceneRendererPassRedirect.ts:1-30` (hand-written end-canvas-pass / begin-scene-FB-pass redirect), `WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:1-45` (hand-ordered stage list 0..9), `WebGPUAttachmentDemandRegistry.ts:1-30` **Meaning:** No FramePass/FrameGraph object and no compile-time pass merging (grep `FrameGraph|renderGraph|mergePass` over `Renderer/WebGPU` is empty). Passes are opened imperatively by the scene renderer and post-process chain. The attachment-demand record explicitly says it "can also feed a render graph" but is "currently observational", so the fork has the data contract for a graph and not the graph — the pass-count/MRT-demand work would gain a declared graph with adjacent-pass merging.

<a id="census-pc-02"></a>**PC-02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — As of v2.21.0 the frame graph is built before mesh and shadow-caster culling, and visibility culling was extracted into a dedicated Culler class with request/execute mesh-instance culling phases. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.21.0> — "Build the frame graph before mesh and shadow-caster culling by @mvaligursky in #8965"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `packages/engine/Source/Scene/Scene.js:3972-3980` (`isVisible` per command against `cullingVolume`), `WebGPUContextCullerPool.ts:1-30` (opt-in GPU frustum-culler pool, hint defaults to `'never'`), `WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:1-45` (compute frustum cull with VISIBILITY/INDIRECT/COUNT modes) **Meaning:** Visibility is still decided per command inside the scene's execute loop (WebGL and WebGPU alike); a separate GPU culler exists but is opt-in and per-frustum/per-cascade, not a pre-graph culling phase. There is no "build the frame's pass list, then cull" ordering, so transient pass resources cannot be allocated once from a known graph — the coupling the CSM cast list and pick depth share.

<a id="census-pc-03"></a>**PC-03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — The WebGPU device records into a single lazily-created shared GPUCommandEncoder (labelled 'CommandEncoder-Shared') that is finished at submit(); submit() asserts it is never called inside a render/compute pass, and a monotonically increasing submitVersion counter tracks queue.submit() calls for staging-buffer reuse. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/webgpu/webgpu-graphics-device.js> — "Attempted to submit command buffers while inside a pass. This finishes the parent command encoder and invalidates the active pass"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUContext.ts:2275-2277` (one frame encoder, label "Scene Frame Command Encoder"), `:2606-2617` (debug-build throw if `beginRenderPass` is called while a pass is active; release build ends the old pass), `:3392-3401` (finish + `queue.submit` + per-encoder-segment callback drain), `:2799-2850` (`enqueueAfterCommandEncoderSubmit` keyed on the exact encoder object), `:727,2224` (`_frameCount`) **Meaning:** The shared per-frame encoder and the inside-pass assertion both exist (the assertion is pragma-stripped, PlayCanvas's is debug-only too). The submit-version counter is replaced by encoder-identity callbacks (a lease per encoder segment) rather than a monotonic integer; `_frameCount` is a frame counter, not a submit counter, and the context submits more than once per frame (`:2423`, `:3239`, `:4183`). Staging-buffer reuse proofs therefore hinge on callbacks, not on a comparable integer.

<a id="census-pc-04"></a>**PC-04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Render pipelines are cached by a 15-slot Uint32Array of state hashes (primitive type, shader id, cull mode, depth-state key, blend-state key, two vertex-format hashes, render-target key, three bind-group-format keys, front/back stencil keys, index format, frontFace) folded with FNV-1a; on lookup the full hash array is compared to defeat hash collisions. No createRenderPipelineAsync is used anywhere in the repo. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/webgpu/webgpu-render-pipeline.js> — "The full array of hashes used to lookup the pipeline, used in case of hash collision."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:949-1113` (`generateCacheKey`: name, variant fields, `ms:`, `df:`, `tg:` per-target format/writeMask/blend, `vx:` vertex layouts, `pl:` layout identity, `pr:` primitive, `dz:` depth/stencil, `mx:` multisample, `sh:` shader-module identity, optional `dfn:`), `:53` (`webgpuObjectIdentity`), `:355,460,1265` (`wrongModuleHits` canary), `:760` (`createRenderPipelineAsync`) **Meaning:** Same field coverage as PlayCanvas's 15-slot hash (shader id, cull, depth, blend, vertex format, target, layout, stencil, index format, frontFace), but the fork uses an exact string key so it needs no post-hash collision compare. Divergence worth noting: the fork DOES use `createRenderPipelineAsync` (with sync fallbacks, e.g. `WebGPUGlobeSurfacePipelines.ts:752`), PlayCanvas never does — so the fork carries an extra ready-gate class of bugs PlayCanvas cannot have.

<a id="census-pc-05"></a>**PC-05** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — GPU resource lifetime is handled by deferred destruction: GPUTexture/GPUBuffer/GPUQuerySet objects are queued and destroyed only after the frame's command buffers are submitted (textures since v2.16.0, buffers since v2.19.0 'Defer WebGPU buffer destruction until submit'). Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/webgpu/webgpu-graphics-device.js> — "Array of GPU resources pending destruction. Resources are destroyed after the current command buffers are submitted to ensure they're not in use."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUContext.ts:571-573` (`_pendingTextureDestroys`), `:2950-2975` (`scheduleTextureDestroy`), `:3404-3420` (drained after `queue.submit` via `onSubmittedWorkDone`), `WebGPUSharedImageryRealizations.ts:37-39,165-181` (imagery eviction routes through it); grep `scheduleBufferDestroy|deferBufferDestroy` over `Renderer/WebGPU` empty; `WebGPUEffectsBindGroup.js:483-496` (a second, local `destroyQueued` on `onSubmittedWorkDone`) **Meaning:** Deferred destruction exists device-wide for textures only. Buffers and query sets are still destroyed inline by their owners (`WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:279,982` keeps its own retired-state set), and at least one subsystem re-implements the queue locally. Extending `scheduleTextureDestroy` to a typed `scheduleDestroy(GPUBuffer|GPUQuerySet)` would collapse those per-subsystem guards — the same simplification PlayCanvas made across v2.16→v2.19.

<a id="census-pc-06"></a>**PC-06** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Multi-view (stereo XR) on WebGPU is implemented as a FramePassMultiView wrapper that runs its child passes once per XR view, swapping the backbuffer view to the eye's sub-image; the children are deliberately kept out of the frame graph's pass list so pass merging cannot fuse eye N with eye N+1. GPU-native multiview (@builtin(view_index)) is documented as a future extension path, not implemented. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/scene/renderer/frame-pass-multi-view.js> — "A frame pass that wraps an ordered list of child frame passes and runs them once per XR view. Currently used by the WebGPU XR path"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — grep `view_index|multiview|OVR_multiview|FramePassMultiView` over `packages/engine/Source` matches only the naga wasm binary; `WebGPUContext.ts:2170-2300` (single canvas view per frame) **Meaning:** No per-view pass wrapper and no multiview of any kind; multi-context (`ContextRegistry`) is the only multiplicity. If XR/stereo ever lands, the per-view wrapper that keeps eye passes out of any merge list is the design to adopt from the start (moot until PC-16 changes).

<a id="census-pc-07"></a>**PC-07** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Engine shaders are composed from a chunk library maintained in both GLSL and WGSL (src/scene/shader-lib/glsl and /wgsl), accessed per-device and per-language via ShaderChunks.get(device, SHADERLANGUAGE_*); materials override chunks through material.getShaderChunks(lang).set(). Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/scene/shader-lib/shader-chunks.js> — "A collection of GLSL and WGSL shader chunks, used to generate shaders."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `packages/engine/Source/Shaders/Builtin/Functions/` (96 `czm_*` GLSL builtins) vs `packages/engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/chunks/functions/` (`csm_*` WGSL chunks, listed this session) and `chunks/structs/`; 68 top-level `.glsl` vs 324 `.wgsl` files; `WGSLShaderPreprocessor.ts:1-45` (auto-resolves `csm_*` references, "mirrors Cesium's `czm_*` pattern"); `WebGPUShaderTranslator.ts:1-30` (GLSL→WGSL is a pluggable compat path, not the production route) **Meaning:** Same posture as PlayCanvas: hand-written parallel GLSL and WGSL chunk libraries, with transpilation reserved for a compatibility path. The fork lacks PlayCanvas's per-version chunk-rename migration doc; C16's comment/attribution cleanup is the natural home for one.

<a id="census-pc-08"></a>**PC-08** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — WGSL shaders are authored without @group/@binding: the engine reflects uniforms, textures and storage buffers from the source, builds the bind group format, packs scalar/vector/matrix uniforms into uniform buffers (accessed with a 'uniform.' prefix; structs unsupported), and strips any author-supplied binding annotations. Source: <https://developer.playcanvas.com/user-manual/graphics/shaders/wgsl-reflection/> — "PlayCanvas reflects resources directly from your WGSL shader source: you declare uniforms, textures, and storage buffers without `@group`/`@binding` indices, and the engine parses these declarations, builds the bind group format, and assigns the bindings automatically."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `packages/engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:292,318,510,516-529` and `PBRMetallicRoughness.wgsl:99-101` (explicit `@group/@binding`), `WGSLShaderBuilder.js:1-15` ("Uniforms use @group(G) @binding(B) ... with explicit bind groups"), `WebGPUBindGroupReflection.ts:1-30` (naga reflection → `GPUBindGroupLayoutDescriptor`, for the stub/translator consumer), `WebGPUUniformGroupManager.ts:1-50` (frequency-grouped UB layouts, hand-declared) **Meaning:** Production WGSL is bound by hand and the layouts are maintained JS-side; a reflection path exists but only feeds the WebGL-compat translator spike. Adopting reflection for authored WGSL would remove the binding-number-drift bug class, but the fork's explicit bindings are also what its per-renderer bind-group caches key on, so it is a real refactor rather than a drop-in.

<a id="census-pc-09"></a>**PC-09** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Shader source (GLSL and WGSL) goes through a C-like preprocessor supporting #define/#if/#else/#endif and #include of registered chunks, with per-material defines; GLSL on WebGPU is transpiled to WGSL via a WASM transpiler, which the WGSL chunk set exists to avoid. Source: <https://developer.playcanvas.com/user-manual/graphics/shaders/> — "This preprocessing step follows a typical C-like preprocessor structure, handling directives such as `#define`, `#if`, `#else`, `#endif`, and similar."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts:1-60` (`//>>ifdef/else/endif` over the `ShaderDefine` bitmask; pure; unknown flags throw), `WGSLShaderPreprocessor.ts:1-45` (a second preprocessor with `// #import "path"` chunk includes, auto-`csm_*` resolution, topological ordering and `#define/#ifdef`), grep `#import "` over `Shaders/WebGPU` hits one file (`chunks/functions/csm_distributionGGX.wgsl:15`) **Meaning:** Both halves of the PlayCanvas preprocessor exist, but as two separate systems: the bitmask `//>>ifdef` one is the production path, while the include-capable one is barely used by shipped shaders (one `#import` in 324 files); chunks are still concatenated JS-side. Unifying on one preprocessor with includes is the simplification PC-09's relevance line names.

<a id="census-pc-10"></a>**PC-10** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — v2.17.0 (2026-03-06) introduced a GPU-driven Gaussian-splat sorting and frustum-culling pipeline on WebGPU, plus a scene-wide splat budget for LOD management and f16 in gsplat WGSL shaders. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.17.0> — "GSplat: GPU-driven sorting and frustum culling pipeline on WebGPU by @mvaligursky in #8453"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:139,1547,1957-1960` (production permutation comes from the async WASM radix sort `GaussianSplatSorter.radixSortIndexes`, CPU/worker side), `:715` (CPU footprint cull), `:404-405,495-497` (`unpack2x16float` — f16-packed SH and covariance), `packages/engine/Source/Scene/GaussianSplatSorter.js:29,58` (wasm_splats radix sort), `GaussianSplatPrimitive.js:572-647` (`splatBudgetSSEScale`: texture-size budget inflates SSE) **Meaning:** The fork has f16-packed splat data and a budget that feeds 3D Tiles SSE, but sorting and frustum culling are CPU/WASM, not GPU compute. This is exactly the C15-G / C18 Wave S gap; PlayCanvas v2.17 is the prior art for moving both onto the GPU with a scene-wide budget.

<a id="census-pc-11"></a>**PC-11** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — v2.19.0 (2026-05-28) added a WebGPU compute radix sort with a portable 4-bit path and an NVIDIA OneSweep path, subgroup sizing exposed on the device, and a 'hybrid raster renderer with compute projector' for splats. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.19.0> — "Add WebGPU compute radix sort (4-bit portable + OneSweep NVIDIA) by @mvaligursky in #8620"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — `packages/engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/` listing (`BitonicSortU64.wgsl`, `GPUSortKeys.wgsl`, `PointCloudSort.wgsl`, `DecoupledLookbackScan.wgsl` — no radix sort), `WebGPUGPUSortKeysDispatcher.ts:1-30` (produces 64-bit keys; "the actual key sort is a separate step"), `WebGPUSubgroupUtils.ts:1-40` (subgroup feature detection + prefix-sum WGSL generator) **Meaning:** No WGSL radix sort in the tree — bitonic/scan primitives and subgroup utilities exist, so the building blocks for a OneSweep-style path are present but unassembled. PlayCanvas's MIT radix sort with a portable 4-bit path and a vendor fast path is the component `C18-S0` should license-review before `C15-G` derives from it.

<a id="census-pc-12"></a>**PC-12** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — v2.20.0 (2026-06-23) removed the tile-based compute (software) splat rasterizer introduced in v2.18.0 and standardised on the hybrid path — compute projection/sort feeding a lightweight vertex/fragment raster — auto-selected and promoted to stable on WebGPU. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.20.0> — "Remove the gsplat compute renderer by @mvaligursky in #8921"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** NOT-APPLICABLE — `WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:1-20,1188,1234,1320` (vertex/fragment quad raster per splat; `triangle-list`, `cullMode: "none"`), `Tools/visual-regression/probe-gsplat-parity.mjs:1-25` (C15-G3 stage) **Meaning:** Not applicable as a fork gap: the fork never built a tile-based compute splat rasterizer, so there is nothing to remove. The claim is a design constraint for the pending C15-G/C18-S rows — default to compute-project + hardware raster (the shape PlayCanvas kept) and do not schedule a software rasterizer.

<a id="census-pc-13"></a>**PC-13** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — The v2.19.0 WebGPU splat renderer moves culling, projection and radix sorting to compute shaders and rasterizes from pre-projected data; measured speedups over WebGL2 scale with splat count (parity at 1M, 2.6x at 10M, 5.7x at 35M on M4 Max; ~2x on iPhone 13 Pro Max). Source: <https://blog.playcanvas.com/new-in-supersplat-webgpu-and-streaming-bring-huge-performance-wins/> — "Instead of sorting splats on a worker thread, the new WebGPU renderer hands the heavy lifting to compute shaders — culling invisible splats, projecting the rest, and sorting them with a fast GPU radix sort."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — `Tools/visual-regression/probe-gsplat-parity.mjs:1-25` (dual-mode WebGL-reference / `--expect-webgpu` presence harness; no throughput curve), `Tools/visual-regression/probe-gsplat-frame-variance.mjs`, `gsplat-frame-variance.spec.mjs` (listed) **Meaning:** The fork's gsplat instruments certify presence and frame variance, not a WebGL-vs-WebGPU scaling curve by splat count. PlayCanvas's published curve (parity at 1M, 2.6x at 10M, 5.7x at 35M) is a ready-made multi-metric acceptance bar for C15-G; the fork has no measurement to compare against it yet.

<a id="census-pc-14"></a>**PC-14** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Indirect drawing (MeshInstance.setIndirect, GraphicsDevice.getIndirectDrawSlot, 5x u32 draw entries and 3x u32 dispatch entries) is WebGPU-only and intended to be fed by compute shaders; multi-draw is always available on WebGPU via indirect commands and via WEBGL_multi_draw on WebGL2, with a CPU loop fallback. Source: <https://developer.playcanvas.com/user-manual/graphics/advanced-rendering/indirect-drawing/> — "Indirect drawing is most powerful when combined with compute shaders that generate the draw parameters."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:1-70` (4×u32 / 5×u32 indirect entries, `maxDrawCalls` slots), `WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:190-215,677-691` (`drawIndirectBuffer/Offset`, indexed/non-indexed dispatch; "consumed by the GPU level-of-detail point-cloud renderer"), `WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:36-45` (`CullMode.INDIRECT` writes instanceCount), grep `WEBGL_multi_draw|multiDrawArrays` over `packages/engine/Source/Renderer` empty **Meaning:** Indirect drawing exists on the WebGPU side with the same entry layout and a slot-style manager, and is consumed by the point-cloud LOD path. No compute-dispatch indirect entries (3×u32) and no WebGL2 `WEBGL_multi_draw` / CPU-loop fallback, so the "works everywhere" half of the meshlet track's cluster-cull + indirect path still needs the WebGL side.

<a id="census-pc-15"></a>**PC-15** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Lighting for omni/spot lights is clustered (default since v1.56) with an 8-bit light index (max 254 visible lights), configurable cell grid, and cookie and shadow atlases; v2.19.0 extended clustered lights to the WebGPU multiview/XR frame pass and aligned the WGSL and GLSL clustered-light chunks. Source: <https://developer.playcanvas.com/user-manual/graphics/lighting/clustered-lighting/> — "Internally, a light index is stored using 8 bits, so the maximum number of visible lights at any frame is 254."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts:1-45` (Forward+ clustered punctual + area lights), `WebGPUClusterAssignRenderer.ts:37-41` (`CLUSTER_MAX_LIGHTS = 1024`, 256 per cluster), `WebGPUClusterBoundsRenderer.ts:27-30` (16×9×24 grid), `WebGPUSceneRendererClusteredLighting.ts:1-40`; grep `shadowAtlas|lightCookie|pointLightShadow|cubeShadow` over `Renderer/WebGPU` and `Scene` empty; `Shaders/WebGPU/chunks/functions/csm_samplePointShadow.wgsl` (listed) **Meaning:** Clustered lighting is present (and wider than PlayCanvas's 254-light cap) but it is WebGPU-only and has no cookie atlas or packed shadow atlas for local lights; local-light shadows are per-light. The atlas half is what the proposed C17 epic would take from this design.

<a id="census-pc-16"></a>**PC-16** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — v2.19.0 shipped immersive WebXR on the WebGPU backend through a backend-specific XrBridge (device-loss handling, AR camera colour, depth bridge, texture-array projection layers), including rendering on Apple Vision Pro with MSAA resolve into the XR framebuffer. Device-loss handling and canvas format config (`WebGPUContextCanvasConfig.ts`, listed) are the two pieces an XrBridge would sit on; nothing else to reuse.

<a id="census-pc-17"></a>**PC-17** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Post-processing is a CameraFrame render-pass graph with HDR physically based bloom, SSAO, DoF, TAA, colour grading/LUT, vignette/fringing, tone mapping and sharpening (CAS); it is extensible via a custom compose shader or by assigning custom RenderPass objects directly to camera.framePasses, and v2.21.0 added volumetric fog to CameraFrame. **Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:1-45` (ordered chain: aerial perspective, AO, bloom+god rays, DoF, auto-exposure, heat shimmer/cold optics, TAA in linear domain, tonemap modes, user stages, colour grading LUT, custom stages, FXAA), `WebGPUPostProcessEffects.ts:149-168`, `WebGPUUserPostProcessStage.ts:1-50` (user WGSL stages via `wgslFragmentShader` + schema uniforms + multi-pass), `WebGPUPostProcessStageCollection.ts:1-30` (feature-renderer sync with `scene.postProcessStages`), `WebGPUVolumetricFogRenderer.ts` (listed) **Meaning:** Effect list matches PC-17 (bloom, AO, DoF, TAA, colour grading, tone mapping, volumetric fog) and user extension exists through WGSL stages. Missing from the PlayCanvas list: CAS sharpening and vignette/fringing (grep `Sharpen|CAS` hits only `WebGPUModelInstancing.js`). The extension point is a uniform-driven fragment stage, not a raw pass object — a `framePasses`-style hook would still be new.

<a id="census-pc-18"></a>**PC-18** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Shadows offer PCF1/3/5, VSM16/32 and PCSS (directional only) with up to 4 cascades; v2.20.0 made the PCSS penumbra world-space (breaking), stopped applying hardware depth bias to PCSS, and let the hybrid gsplat renderer cast directional shadows with scalable per-splat culling. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.20.0> — "[BREAKING] World-space PCSS penumbra, plus gsplat & cascade fixes by @mvaligursky in #8818"

World-space PCSS penumbra with the hardware depth-bias exclusion is a concrete additive row for the CSM lane; splat shadow casting belongs to C15-G/C18-S.

<a id="census-pc-19"></a>**PC-19** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Picking: since v2.14.0 the Picker can optionally render depth and return world-space pick points; v2.16.0 added unified Gaussian-splat picking; v2.21.0 fixed a GPU device hang when picking gsplats on Windows/NVIDIA. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.14.0> — "Picker class can (optional) render depth and provide world space pick points by @mvaligursky in https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/pull/8154"

The PlayCanvas v2.21 Windows/NVIDIA splat-pick device hang (#9036) has no counterpart record here — a targeted Edge/NVIDIA probe of the splat pick path is the cheap hazard check the claim recommends.

<a id="census-pc-20"></a>**PC-20** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Non-persistent uniform data is served by DynamicBuffers, a bump allocator over large staging+GPU buffer pairs (initialised at 100 KiB, aligned to minUniformBufferOffsetAlignment); staged data is copied to GPU buffers in a separate command encoder submitted ahead of the frame's rendering command buffers, and staging buffers are recycled via mapAsync after submit. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/dynamic-buffers.js> — "This class utilizes a bump allocator to efficiently allocate aligned memory space from a set of large buffers managed internally."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPURingBufferAllocator.ts:1-25,45-60` (N-page ring, per-frame page advance, 256-byte alignment, overflow pages + circuit breaker), `WebGPUContext.ts:3372-3375` (`_uniformAllocator.flush()` — one `queue.writeBuffer` per dirty page before submit), `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceCameraUB.ts:1-25` (`writeUniformSlice` against the ring), `WebGPUModelCameraArena.ts:120-140` (256 = `minUniformBufferOffsetAlignment` maximum) **Meaning:** Same bump-allocator shape, but the upload path is `queue.writeBuffer` coalesced per page, not a staging→GPU copy encoder submitted ahead of the frame with `mapAsync` recycling. The fork's page rotation (N frames) is the reuse proof instead of a fence. Whether an explicit copy-encoder + mapped staging ring would beat `writeBuffer` here is a measurable question, not a known gap.

<a id="census-pc-21"></a>**PC-21** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — v2.21.0 routed view-level uniforms through the dynamic buffers and removed the dedicated view bind groups on WebGPU, and moved view-level uniforms into uniform buffers on WebGL2 as well. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.21.0> — "Route view uniforms through dynamic buffers; remove view bind groups by @mvaligursky in #8967"
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:292` and `PBRMetallicRoughness.wgsl:99` (camera UB at group 0 binding 0 per renderer), `WebGPUCollectionCameraUB.js:1-40` (per-frustum per-slice camera buffers for collections), `WebGPUModelCameraArena.ts:120-200` (model camera + light blocks as dynamic-offset slices of the ring page, one shared group-0 bind group), `WebGPUUniformGroupManager.ts:1-40` (per-frame / per-material / per-object grouping) **Meaning:** The model path already does what PlayCanvas v2.21 did — view uniforms as dynamic-offset slices of one buffer with a shared bind group. Globe, collections and most other renderers still own a dedicated camera bind group each; extending the arena pattern to them is the bind-group-churn reduction `CesiumDebug.cacheStats()` would show.

<a id="census-pc-22"></a>**PC-22** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — GPU timing uses the 'timestamp-query' feature through a WebgpuQuerySet wrapper whose results are read back via rotating staging buffers (non-blocking); per-pass timestampWrites are attached to render and compute pass descriptors by name, and v2.20.0 corrected an overestimate in reported WebGPU GPU frame time. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/webgpu/webgpu-query-set.js> — "A wrapper over the GpuQuerySet object, allowing timestamp and occlusion queries. The results are copied back using staging buffers to avoid blocking."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:1-40` (`timestampWrites` per named pass), `:193,248-260,538,641` (rotating readback ring, `mapAsync` after submit, non-blocking, skip accounting), `:504` (`resolveQuerySet`), `WebGPUTimestampAccounting.ts:1-30` (union-of-intervals coverage so overlapping passes are not double counted; every frame reaches one terminal outcome), `WebGPUContext.ts:7074-7087` (`timestamp-query` gate) **Meaning:** Equivalent design, and the fork already guards against the over-estimation class PlayCanvas fixed in v2.20 — its accounting reports `summed − covered` overlap as a finding instead of clamping. Nothing to port; PC-22 is a cross-check the fork passes.

<a id="census-pc-23"></a>**PC-23** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — WebGPU validation is done with pushErrorScope/popErrorScope around passes in the debug build only (stripped from release builds), with a skipValidation switch for profiling and duplicate-message capping; v2.19.0 added logging of uncaptured WebGPU errors and GPU vendor/architecture in the feature log, and v2.18.0 added a 'WebGPU Bare' device type for testing without optional features. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/blob/v2.21.4/src/platform/graphics/webgpu/webgpu-debug.js> — "Internal WebGPU debug system. Note that the functions only execute in the debug build, and are stripped out in other builds."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:1087,2268-2280` (one-time validation scope around the first globe pass, not pragma-gated), `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceShaders.ts:273-282`, `WebGPUModelPipelineCache.ts:3548-3550`, `WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:2125-2126`, `WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts:402-411` (scopes around specific shader/pipeline creations), grep `uncapturederror` over `Renderer/WebGPU` empty, `WebGPUContext.ts:1426` (logs `adapter.info.vendor` at init), `WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:55-76` + `WebGPUDevicePool.ts:458-511,814-819` (optional-feature negotiation), no "bare" device type (grep empty) **Meaning:** Error scopes are targeted (pipeline/shader creation, one first-globe-pass scope) rather than a debug-build wrap of every pass, and there is no `uncapturederror` listener or duplicate-message cap. Feature negotiation is real, but there is no `bare`-style device that refuses every optional feature — adding one would exercise the no-f16 / no-timestamp fallbacks in probes without special hardware, which the fork currently cannot do on demand.

<a id="census-pc-24"></a>**PC-24** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — v2.20.0 added post-build tests for the ESM/UMD bundles plus an API-surface gate that comments the public API diff and minified bundle-size changes on every PR. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/engine/releases/tag/v2.20.0> — "Add post-build tests for ESM/UMD bundles and API surface gate by @kpal81xd in #8845"
