The R-2026-08-29-1 bar gives a Tools spec with an npm runner home a place to land — the CLAUDE.md size table (7.1 / 5.6 / 6.4 MB) is currently a hand-maintained number with no regression check.

<a id="census-pc-25"></a>**PC-25** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — The engine itself has no double-precision / camera-relative (RTE) positioning; geospatial support exists only as the separate 'Earthatile' runtime for 3D Tiles (Photorealistic 3D Tiles) with a PlayCanvas integration, whose repository was last pushed 2025-10-08. Source: <https://github.com/playcanvas/earthatile> — "Earthatile is an engine-agnostic runtime for loading and navigating 3D Tiles geospatial datasets."
**Fork (Lothíriel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** NOT-APPLICABLE — `WebGPURTEAssertions.ts:1-40` (debug validators for `encodedCameraHigh/Low` packing and RTE MVP translation order), `Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:55,1297` (`encodedCameraHigh` + `Low` in `CameraUniforms`), `WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:6,21` (RTE positioning, `EncodedCartesian3`), `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceCameraUB.ts:1-15` **Meaning:** Not applicable as a gap in either direction: the claim says PlayCanvas has no RTE/double precision and its geospatial support is an external 3D Tiles runtime, so it offers nothing to the fork's high/low RTE rule (which every WebGPU renderer here implements). Confirms PlayCanvas is a renderer-architecture reference only, not a planetary-precision one.

### A.6 Babylon.js WebGPU engine

25 claims: 24 KEPT, 1 REFUTED, 0 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 9 / PARTIAL 12 / MISSING 2 / NOT-APPLICABLE 1. Target as recorded by the collector: Babylon.js WebGPU engine (requested as 8.x; the current line is 9.x - 8.x ended at 8.56.2 on 2026-03-24, 9.0.0 shipped 2026-03-26); latest version 9.23.0 (2026-08-27).

<a id="census-bjs-01"></a>**BJS-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — The WebGPU engine records each frame into two command encoders, upload then render, submitted in that order; a compute dispatch closes the current render pass, runs, and the pass is reopened. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUInternals/webGPUOverview> — "We use two command encoders: upload and render. upload is used when we need to upload data into textures, and render is used to render into render targets and for the main render pass, when rendering into the swap chain texture. They are pushed into the queue in this order: upload -> render."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:2275 (one "Scene Frame Command Encoder" per frame); :3168-3175 and :3383-3395 (a separate "TextureMipPreparation" prep encoder is finished and submitted BEFORE the frame encoder - the upload-then-render order); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUFrameCommandEncoder.ts:20-50 (compute joins the frame encoder only while no render pass is open; otherwise the caller keeps a private encoder) **Meaning:** Same upload->render two-submit order as Babylon, but the fork never closes and reopens a render pass for compute: compute is recorded before the scene pass on the frame encoder, or on a private encoder. Babylon's per-interruption pass cost is avoided by construction; the trade is extra private submits from off-frame callers.

<a id="census-bjs-02"></a>**BJS-02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Bind groups are cached per shader by resource uniqueId (buffers/textures) and sampler hash, with a fast path that returns the previous bind groups when the draw context (buffers) and material context (textures/samplers) are unchanged; group/binding location is not part of the key and cross-pool ID collisions are theoretically possible. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUInternals/webGPUCacheBindGroup> — "The cache includes an optimization that simply returns the existing bind groups if the draw and material contexts have not changed since the last cache query. The draw context holds the list of uniform and storage buffers, and the material context holds the list of textures and samplers used by the shader."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUBindGroupCache.ts:19-24, :170, :206 (key = layout identity + each entry's binding + resource identity, per-cache identity map); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeBindGroupCache.ts:11-17, :19-40 (WeakMap numeric identity per GPU resource; groups 0/1/2 keyed on backing-resource identity, dynamic offsets kept out of the key) **Meaning:** Same resource-identity keying as Babylon. The fork includes `binding` in the key and hands out monotonically assigned WeakMap ids, so Babylon's acknowledged cross-pool ID collision class does not exist here. Babylon's "draw context / material context unchanged -> return previous groups" fast path is not present as a named mechanism; the globe cache's age-based eviction is the fork's extra.

<a id="census-bjs-03"></a>**BJS-03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — The render-pipeline cache is a node tree keyed by ordered state IDs (least-likely-to-change states first) with a lowest-dirty-index pointer so lookups resume mid-tree; a string-concatenation hash-map key was tried and dropped as ~2x slower. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUInternals/webGPUCacheRenderPipeline> — "Note that we tried an implementation where render pipelines were recorded in a hash map and the lookup key was a string concatenation of the state values, see the class `WebGPUCacheRenderPipelineString`. We dropped it because the node-tree implementation was faster, around 2x faster at the time the comparison was made."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:437 (`Map<string, PipelineCacheEntry>`), :949-1110 (`generateCacheKey` concatenates name, variant, `pl:`/`pr:`/`dz:`/`mx:`/`sh:`/`dfn:` segments into one string), :66-70 (hand-serialised because the key runs on every lookup) **Meaning:** The fork has a render-pipeline cache but uses exactly the string-concatenation key Babylon measured at ~2x slower than its state-ordered node tree with a lowest-dirty-index pointer. The tree structure is absent. Only a candidate DX/perf row if `cpuPassCost` ever attributes cost to pipeline lookup; the fork already removed the serialiser walk.

<a id="census-bjs-04"></a>**BJS-04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Babylon's core shaders are now authored natively in WGSL (with GLSL compatibility maintained and a GLSL->SPIR-V->WGSL Tint/WASM fallback); users are told to write WGSL directly for faster startup and smaller downloads, and ShaderMaterial WGSL uses a special syntax (vertexInputs./uniforms./scene./mesh. prefixes, #include chunks, leftover uniform buffer). Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUWGSL> — "Currently, most of the main shaders used by Babylon.js are written in WGSL, the only shader language that WebGPU supports. That being said, we actively maintain compatibility with GLSL."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Shaders/WebGPU/ (324 `.wgsl` files authored natively, against 68 top-level GLSL files); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts:1-12 (vendored naga 27 WASM GLSL/SPIR-V->WGSL, lazy-loaded, status ACTIVE) and :26-36 (known gap: raw `czm_*` fragments must go through the preprocessor first); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUShaderTranslator.ts:1-20 (pluggable translator registry for the WebGL-compat stub path) **Meaning:** The fork already sits where Babylon 9.x landed: WGSL primary, translation only as a compatibility fallback. Babylon's ShaderMaterial prefix syntax has no direct analogue; the fork's `//>>ifdef` preprocessor and `WGSLShaderBuilder.js` fill the equivalent role.

<a id="census-bjs-05"></a>**BJS-05** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — The Frame Graph (alpha in 8.0, v1 in 9.0) is a DAG of tasks that each declare input/output resources; assigning Scene.frameGraph makes graph execution replace most of Scene.render, but the docs state the implementation is not yet fully complete and some rendering tasks are unavailable in frame graphs. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/frameGraph/frameGraphBasicConcepts> — "A frame graph is a DAG (Directed Acyclic Graph - a bit like NME / NGE) in which each node (task) represents a render pass, or more generally a task performed when rendering a frame (we have tasks that are not render passes, such as the culling task)."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPostProcessStageCollection.ts:1-27 (fixed-order integration layer over `WebGPUPostProcessPipeline`); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUAttachmentDemandRegistry.ts:8-11, :86 (a per-frame attachment-demand record written so it "can also feed a render graph" later - observational only today) **Meaning:** No frame/render graph: passes are a fixed list. The fork has laid one graph input (attachment demand as a single authority) but has no DAG scheduler or task nodes. Babylon's own "not yet complete" caveat says this is a multi-release lane, not a near-term parity item.

<a id="census-bjs-06"></a>**BJS-06** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Frame-graph textures are addressed by numeric handles rather than references so the texture manager can alias the same GPU texture to multiple handles (FrameGraph.optimizeTextureAllocation); history/ping-pong textures are a first-class handle type used by TAA. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/frameGraph/frameGraphClassFramework/frameGraphClassOverview> — "To achieve texture reuse (enabled via FrameGraph.optimizeTextureAllocation), which is one of the main goals of frame graphs, textures must be manipulated via handles (which are just numbers under the hood) and not directly through a reference (pointer) to the actual texture."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUFramebufferManager.ts:37-64, :287-293, :316, :364-379 (feature-detected `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT` usage on MSAA and unsampled depth attachments); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUEnvironmentTargetPool.ts:1-12 (pooled capture depth targets and parameter buffers for the environment-refresh path); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUTAAEffect.ts:379-394 (TAA history ping-pong as a first-class `HistorySlotId` through `WebGPUParityManager`) **Meaning:** Targets are still allocated per owner (post-process, pick, classification, TAA); there are no numeric handles and no aliasing of one GPU texture to several logical targets. The fork's memory levers are driver-side (transient attachments) plus one local pool. Handle-based aliasing would need the frame graph of BJS-05 first.

<a id="census-bjs-07"></a>**BJS-07** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; performance) — Babylon reports GPU memory savings of 40% or more in some scenes from frame-graph texture reuse. Source: <https://blogs.windows.com/windowsdeveloper/2026/03/26/announcing-babylon-js-9-0/> — "This means substantial GPU memory savings (we've seen 40% or more in some cases!) and a level of rendering flexibility that was simply not possible before."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** NOT-APPLICABLE — Reason: a vendor benchmark of Babylon's frame-graph texture reuse, not a technique; the fork has no frame-graph aliasing (BJS-05/06) to compare 40% against. Nearest fork surface: Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:6835-6845 (`getRendererStatistics` exposes "GPU memory pool usage" among lazy debug fields). **Meaning:** Usable only as an external bar once the fork measures post-process GPU memory under the multi-metric doctrine. No fork counterpart exists to mark HAS or MISSING.

<a id="census-bjs-08"></a>**BJS-08** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Clustered lighting (9.0) bins spot/point lights into 64x64 screen tiles and 16 depth slices; on WebGPU the light bitmasks live in an i32 storage buffer written with atomic OR, while WebGL2 falls back to additive blending into a float render target limited to 23 lights per batch; node materials must be created with ShaderLanguage.WGSL on WebGPU because GLSL->WGSL conversion cannot express the storage buffer. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/lights/clusteredLighting> — "The bitmasks in WebGPU are stored in an I32 storage buffer with the size `horizontalTiles x verticalTiles x numBatches`, where `numBatches` is the number of lights divided by 32."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts:1-22 (WebGPU Forward+ clustered lighting: cluster-bounds + cluster-assign compute passes over storage buffers; area lights in a parallel storage buffer); Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/ClusterAssign.wgsl:164 ("one writer thread, so no atomics needed"); Scene/Scene.js:3386-3390 (`clusteredLightingEnabled` doc: up to 1024 scene lights / 256 per cluster on WebGPU; "WebGL ignores this option") **Meaning:** The fork has the WebGPU half (compute-binned clusters in storage buffers) with a per-cluster single-writer design instead of Babylon's atomic-OR bitmask. It has NO WebGL fallback at all (Babylon emulates with additive blending into a float target, 23 lights per batch), so clustered lighting is a documented WebGPU-only feature here - the same backend-specific split Babylon documents, taken one step further.

<a id="census-bjs-09"></a>**BJS-09** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Dynamic IBL shadows voxelize casters into a <=256^3 3D texture, importance-sample the environment via CDF maps, and temporally accumulate; on WebGPU voxelization became single-pass (8.33.3, PR #17270) using textureStore into the 3D texture plus vertex pulling for provoking-vertex normals, versus layer-by-layer draw-buffer passes on WebGL2. Source: <https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/pull/17270> — "This changes the voxelization strategy for WebGPU to allow it to be done in a single pass (though, generation of mips still takes additional steps). This is possible using `textureStore` to write to arbitrary texels in a 3D texture."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — No hits for `voxeliz`, `IBL shadow` or `vertex pulling` under Renderer/WebGPU, Scene, Shaders/WebGPU. The enabling pattern exists: Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/AerialPerspectiveFroxel.wgsl, CloudNoiseBake.wgsl, VolumetricFog.wgsl (`texture_storage_3d` compute writes). **Meaning:** Dynamic IBL shadows via caster voxelisation do not exist in the fork. Single-pass `textureStore` into a 3D texture is already used by clouds, fog and aerial perspective, so a C18 voxel or C12/C13 IBL-shadow row would start from working infrastructure rather than zero.

<a id="census-bjs-10"></a>**BJS-10** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — TAA is a rendering pipeline using Halton-sequence projection jitter and a ping-pong history; ghosting is handled by disabling on camera motion by default, with optional per-pixel-velocity history reprojection and neighbourhood colour clamping recommended together. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/postProcesses/taaRenderingPipeline> — "For the best results in motion it is recommended to enable both `reprojectHistory`, which will attempt to find the pixel in the previous frame, and `clampHistory`, which acts as a fallback if the reprojection does not work:"
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUTAAEffect.ts:1-13 (history ping-pong + neighbourhood clamping), :36-48 (params UBO: `historyValid`, `previousVpRte`, `cameraDelta`), :249-263 and :774-782 (interleaved-gradient-noise jitter chosen over Halton 2/3 for temporal decorrelation; `halton()` kept at :236); Shaders/WebGPU/PostProcess/TAA.wgsl:1-35 (depth-based motion-vector reprojection + neighbourhood clamp, RTE-safe), :57-68 (per-pixel `motionTex` velocity with depth fallback), :103-132 (3x3 AABB clamp) **Meaning:** The fork ships the combination Babylon recommends (velocity reprojection + neighbourhood clamp) as the default path, not opt-in. Differences: jitter is IGN rather than Halton (a deliberate choice documented in-file), and there is no "disable TAA while the camera moves" default - the fork relies on reprojection, the stricter bar for a moving-altitude campaign.

<a id="census-bjs-11"></a>**BJS-11** (**REFUTED**; REFUTED; feature) — An FSR 1 spatial upscaler (EASU + RCAS as two fragment passes) renders the scene at reduced resolution and upscales to the canvas on WebGPU, WebGL2 and Babylon Native; being spatial it cannot ghost but cannot recover sub-pixel detail like a temporal upscaler. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/postProcesses/fsr1RenderingPipeline> — "Babylon.js implements FSR 1 as the `FSR1RenderingPipeline`, and it runs on WebGPU, WebGL 2, and Babylon Native."
**Fork:** not mapped (disposition REFUTED; the bank lists it, per the brief).
**Verifier reasoning (REFUTED):**
- Vote 1, REFUTED (source checked: https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/postProcesses/fsr1RenderingPipeline (404 Not Found, verified via WebFetch); cross-checked via WebSearch and GitHub PR https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/pull/18723): The cited URL (https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/postProcesses/fsr1RenderingPipeline) returns HTTP 404 Not Found — the page does not exist, so it cannot support the quoted text "Babylon.js implements FSR 1 as the FSR1RenderingPipeline, and it runs on WebGPU, WebGL 2, and Babylon Native." A web search for the doc page found no live equivalent page either. The only corroborating source found is GitHub PR #18723 (BabylonJS/Babylon.js, by bghgary), whose description does support the underlying technical claim (WGSL for WebGPU, new GLSL variants added so it also runs on WebGL2 and Babylon Native, isSupported = isWebGPU || engine.version >= 2), but that is a different source than the one cited, and per instructions the cited source must be checked and must exist/support the claim. Since the cited doc page does not exist, the claim as sourced is REFUTED, even though the capability itself is plausible per the PR.

<a id="census-bjs-12"></a>**BJS-12** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Order-independent transparency uses dual depth peeling (scene.useOrderIndependentTransparency, 5 passes = up to 10 layers by default), is WebGL2/WebGPU only and still flagged beta; on WebGPU better performance comes from depthPeelingRenderer.useRenderPasses=true with compatibilityMode=false. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/materials/advanced/transparent_rendering> — "This is using the dual depth peeling method and the renderer can be accessed by `scene.depthPeelingRenderer`."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUOIT.ts:1-12 (weighted-blended MRT OIT after McGuire/Bavoil 2013; rgba16float accumulation + r8unorm revealage; requires the scene OIT request AND the default-off `CesiumDebug.webgpuOIT` containment control; no dual-source variant); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUFeatureFlags.ts:50 (C4 weighted-average OIT flag); Scene/GlobeTranslucencyState.js:302 (the only depth-peel in the tree, upstream globe translucency, not general OIT) **Meaning:** OIT exists on WebGPU but by a different algorithm (weighted-blended, not dual depth peeling) and is contained off by default, while Babylon's depth peeling is opt-in and still beta. Both engines confirm robust OIT on WebGPU remains costly; the fork has no depth-peeling path to compare Babylon's `useRenderPasses` optimisation against.

<a id="census-bjs-13"></a>**BJS-13** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — GPU picking (since 8.0) renders the pickable list with unique ID colours to a texture and reads back asynchronously (pickAsync / multiPickAsync over a min-max region); 9.13.0 added depth point and normal readback, 8.51.1 added Gaussian-splat GPU picking, and 9.5.0 switched the Inspector to GPU picking. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/mesh/interactions/picking_collisions> — "The system will then render the scene onto a texture and read from that texture at the given coordinates. Each mesh is rendered with a unique color, so the picker can return the picked mesh."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Scene/Scene.js:4862 (`pickAsync`), :5141 (`drillPickAsync`), :4781-4795 (doc steering one-off picks to the async API); Scene/Picking.js:102-110, :326 (`_pickAsyncWithMode`), :842 (sync `pickPosition` remains); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPickFramebuffer.ts:1-12 (rgba8unorm pick target, `copyTextureToBuffer` + `mapAsync`; sync `end()` reads a buffer mapped in a previous frame), :1293-1303 (one-shot cold-pick warning); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:55-59, :74, :169 (splat pick pipeline and pick command via the shared pick helpers) **Meaning:** The fork has the promise-based pick architecture Babylon describes, including Gaussian-splat picking. Unlike Babylon it keeps the synchronous `scene.pick()` contract alive on top of an async readback (one-frame-stale, warned once) - that seam is exactly the Batch 1374 / Q-141 readback class Babylon sidesteps by being async-only. Depth-point/normal readback stays on the sync `pickPosition` path.

<a id="census-bjs-14"></a>**BJS-14** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Streamed Gaussian splatting (GaussianSplattingStream, experimental, WebGL2 and WebGPU) loads a spatial octree of SOG chunks at multiple LODs; 9.23.0 added an optional splat budget that caps rendered splats and apportions detail across parts, with LOD/culling evaluated for every active camera. Source: <https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/pull/18834> — "This PR adds an optional splat budget: tell a scene the maximum number of splats you want rendered, and it stays under that ceiling, automatically spending the budget where it matters most (larger, closer things stay sharp; small or distant ones quietly drop detail)."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Scene/GaussianSplat3DTileContent.js:15-40 (splats arrive as `KHR_gaussian_splatting` / `_compression_spz_2` glTF tile content under the 3D Tiles hierarchy, one `GaussianSplatPrimitive` per tileset); Scene/GaussianSplatPrimitive.js:584-600, :630-648 (backend-neutral texture-size budget; over-cap truncates and scales SSE next frame via `splatBudgetSSEScale`); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:1-19, :2773, :2875 (tileset-level render with streaming tiles) **Meaning:** The fork streams splats through the 3D Tiles hierarchy and has a hard texture-capacity budget with an SSE feedback loop, but no Babylon-style detail apportioning across parts, no SOG/octree chunk format, and no per-active-camera LOD/culling evaluation was found (no multi-camera hits in GaussianSplatPrimitive.js). Direct input for C15 GSPLAT / C18 Wave S: budget-driven LOD and multi-view awareness are the gaps.

<a id="census-bjs-15"></a>**BJS-15** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Snapshot rendering (WebGPU only) records one frame's draw calls into render bundles and replays them every subsequent frame; the gain is JavaScript-side only, FAST mode updates only the scene uniform buffer, adding/removing meshes requires a reset, and it is now supported inside frame graphs (8.25.1). Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUOptimization/webGPUSnapshotRendering> — "It works by recording the draw calls during one frame and by replaying this recording for all subsequent frames. So, the scene should be mostly static for this mode to work as expected."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts:1-15 (bundle cache, invalidation, eviction), :91-95, :299-383 (Phase 6A snapshot-mode freeze: cache admission and eviction stop while `SnapshotModeService` holds a snapshot); Scene/SnapshotModeService.js (exists; referenced from Scene/Scene.js and Scene/FrameState.js); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRendererGlobePass.ts:48-60 (the globe opaque path deliberately carries no bundle: ring-allocated dynamic offsets rotate every frame, and a per-frame rebuild measured 0.3-0.4 ms slower with worse p90) **Meaning:** The fork has a snapshot mode that freezes cached bundles, but not Babylon's whole-frame record-and-replay; and its own measurement (globe pass) confirms Babylon's caveat that per-frame churn is the worst case. Judging bundles on CPU encode cost (`cpuPassCost`) rather than FPS is already the fork's practice.

<a id="census-bjs-16"></a>**BJS-16** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Non-compatibility mode (engine.compatibilityMode=false) builds one render bundle per submesh-in-context and reuses it across frames; bundle creation cost must be amortised, occlusion queries and separateCullingPass do not work, and bundles are interleaved in a bundle list because setViewport/setScissorRect/setStencilReference cannot be recorded inside bundles. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUOptimization/webGPUNonCompatibilityMode> — "What this mode does is build an object called a bundle, which holds the commands needed to draw a mesh, more precisely a submesh, in a given context, and then reuses this bundle each time the mesh must be drawn in that context. Building this bundle has a cost, so the bundle must live long enough to make up for that cost."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts:22-58 (per-key `BundleEntry` with version, last-used frame, draw count; encoder descriptor mirrors the pass's attachment formats), :159-187 (`invalidate` / `invalidateByPrefix` / `invalidateAll`); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUEnvironmentRenderer.js:1879-1906, :2607-2610 (the one live producer: environment bundles via `getOrCreate`, invalidated on cache change); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:1534-1544 (all bundles invalidated when colour-attachment formats change) **Meaning:** Per-draw bundle caching exists as infrastructure and is used by the environment (sky/sun/moon) path only; models, tiles and the globe do not go through it. Babylon's documented bundle incompatibilities (viewport/scissor/stencil-reference not recordable; occlusion queries) are not encoded as guards in the manager - a multi-frustum or pick-pass adopter would meet them unannounced.

<a id="census-bjs-17"></a>**BJS-17** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — In non-compatibility mode Babylon issues indexed/non-indexed indirect draws (drawIndexedIndirect/drawIndirect from a per-draw-context indirect buffer) by default; indirect draws in compatibility mode are opt-in via enableIndirectDraw. Source: <https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/blob/master/packages/dev/core/src/Engines/WebGPU/webgpuDrawContext.ts> — "By default, indirect draws are enabled in NON compatibility mode only      * To enable indirect draws in compatibility mode (done by the end user), enableIndirectDraw must be set to true"
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:195-208, :527-531, :682-691 (`drawIndirectBuffer` / `drawIndirectOffset` on the command; `drawIndexedIndirect` / `drawIndirect` issued when set); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:1-13 (compute fills the indirect buffer, one `drawIndexedIndirect` executes); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:1-8 (compute cull writes `instanceCount=0` for culled objects); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:553, :616-639 (merged `drawIndexedIndirect` batches) **Meaning:** The compute-cull -> indirect-args -> single draw bridge Babylon uses by default in non-compat mode is present, but per-command opt-in (a command carries an indirect buffer or it does not) rather than an engine-wide default. Matches the meshlet-track conclusion that compute cluster culling + indirect draw is the portable route.

<a id="census-bjs-18"></a>**BJS-18** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — 9.1.0 added a public async pipeline pre-warming API (WebGPUEngine.createRenderPipelineAsync) that uses device.createRenderPipelineAsync to compile and cache pipelines for effects/meshes before first use, batching cache-state setup across an array of requests. Source: <https://github.com/BabylonJS/Babylon.js/blob/master/packages/dev/core/src/Engines/webgpuEngine.pure.ts> — "Asynchronously pre-creates one or more render pipelines so they are cached and ready      * to use without any compilation hitch when first rendered."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:759-760 (`createRenderPipelineAsync` is the cache's creation path); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeSurfacePipelines.ts:567-621 (`DEFAULT_GLOBE_PIPELINE_PREWARM`: the two measured first-frame vertex layouts), :626-647 (background creation of every prewarm variant); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUShaderModuleCache.ts:55, :312 (`prewarm(sourceId, source, defineSets, labelPrefix)` for shader modules); Renderer/WebGPU/AsyncResourceMonitor.ts:58, :289-300 (background warmup tracked separately so the scene can hibernate) **Meaning:** The fork has async creation plus targeted prewarm (globe layouts, shader-module define sets) and the C11-180 bounded scheduling, but no single public `createRenderPipelineAsync(options[])`-style entry point that batches cache-state setup for arbitrary meshes/effects. Exposing one on `WebGPURenderPipelineCache` is the concrete row the claim suggests.

<a id="census-bjs-19"></a>**BJS-19** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; tooling) — GPU frame time in the Inspector comes from WebGPU timestamp queries written at the start of the upload encoder and the end of the render encoder; Chrome must be launched with --enable-dawn-features=allow_unsafe_apis (and optionally --enable-webgpu-developer-features for unquantized timing). Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUInternals/webGPUOverview> — "This class implements GPU timing for a frame by writing a timestamp at the beginning of the upload command encoder and another as the last command of the render command encoder, then subtracting the time measured by the two timestamps."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:9-16 (`timestamp-query` feature; modern `timestampWrites` on render/compute passes; frame- and pass-level timing with readback buffering); :19-36 (per-pass `getPassTimestampWrites`, `endFrame` resolve + readback, results N frames old); Scene/CesiumDebug.js:84-85 (`cpuPassCost` / `gpuPassCost` commands); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:2282-2287 (profiling begins before the first pass so it is not omitted) **Meaning:** The fork's mechanism is finer than Babylon's (per pass, not just whole frame). No mention of the Chrome/Edge `--enable-dawn-features=allow_unsafe_apis` requirement or the 100 microsecond quantisation was found in engine source (grep empty) - that caveat lives outside `packages/` and should stand beside any GPU-timing bar per the multi-metric doctrine.

<a id="census-bjs-20"></a>**BJS-20** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — Inspector v2 (preview Oct 2025, shipped with 9.0) is a ground-up rebuild: service-oriented, React-based, extensible, ESM-first and tree-shakeable, with near-instant scene-explorer performance on large scenes, but is explicitly not yet at parity or fully backward compatible with Inspector v1. Source: <https://forum.babylonjs.com/t/introducing-inspector-v2/60937> — "Inspector v2 is esm first, meaning you can integrate it into your own projects and your bundler can fully tree shake it and code split / chunk optional features."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Scene/CesiumDebug.js:3-23 (console-accessible debug service, command catalog installed on `window.CesiumDebug`); packages/widgets/Source/: `CesiumInspector`, `Cesium3DTilesInspector`, `VoxelInspector`, `InspectorShared.js` (upstream widget inspectors, present in the tree) **Meaning:** The fork's debug surface is a console command catalog plus the inherited upstream inspector widgets; there is no service-oriented, ESM-first, tree-shakeable rebuild in the Inspector v2 sense. Babylon's parity caveat is the lesson: a debug-UI rebuild is a multi-release lane, not a batch.

<a id="census-bjs-21"></a>**BJS-21** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; platform) — WebGPU support is described as complete apart from listed exceptions: WebGPU-XR is experimental and needs XRGPUBinding projection layers, WebGPU-XR multiview, raw camera access, space warp and GPU depth sensing are unsupported, triangle-fan/line-loop are not implemented, and point size other than 1 is ignored. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/setup/support/webGPU/webGPUStatus> — "WebGPU-XR multiview, Raw Camera Access, Space Warp, and GPU-optimized Depth Sensing are not currently supported"
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUModelTopology.ts:26-35 (LINE_LOOP realised as line-list with closing segment; TRIANGLE_FAN expanded to triangle-list; both at preparation, not per draw); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPointPrimitiveRenderer.js:4 (no `gl_PointSize` on WebGPU - points rendered as quads); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPointCloudRenderer.ts:137-140, :316-322, :388-389 (per-point size carried through a multiplier and attenuation uniform); no `WebXR` / `XRGPUBinding` hits under Renderer/WebGPU or Scene; Renderer/GraphicsContext.ts:743 (multi-view via the context registry) **Meaning:** The fork has closed two of Babylon's standing WebGPU exceptions (fan/loop topologies, point size) with its own realisation code, and takes the same position on multiview (a multi-context problem, not a single-pass feature). It has no WebGPU-XR at all, experimental or otherwise - the one exception where Babylon is ahead.

<a id="census-bjs-22"></a>**BJS-22** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — Large World Rendering (9.0) is a floating-origin scheme: the active camera stays conceptually at the origin and all geometry/shader uniforms are offset by the camera position on the CPU, with useLargeWorldRendering also forcing 64-bit CPU matrix math (useHighPrecisionMatrices); there is no high/low split in vertex data, and an 8.56.2 fix preserved Float64 precision for the instance-buffer offset. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/scene/large_world> — "Babylon.js provides a floating origin system that eliminates precision issues by keeping the active camera conceptually at the world origin and offsetting all geometry and shader uniforms by the camera's position. This means that no matter how far you travel from the original world origin, the values sent to the GPU are always small and precise."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURTEAssertions.ts:1-13, :31-38 (debug validators for `encodedCameraHigh/Low` packing and RTE MVP translation order; 6 m round-trip alarm); Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:795, :876 (`translateRelativeToEye(posHigh, posLow, ...)`); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:6, :21 (RTE for splats via `EncodedCartesian3`) **Meaning:** Opposite design to Babylon: the fork keeps GPU-side emulated doubles (high/low split + `mvpRelativeToEye`) in every renderer, so planet-scale static vertex buffers never need CPU re-offsetting when the camera moves. Babylon's CPU-side floating-origin scheme is the evidence for why the CLAUDE.md RTE rule is non-negotiable here.

<a id="census-bjs-23"></a>**BJS-23** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — The GeospatialCamera (8.29.1 onward, headline in 9.0) orbits a spherical planet at the origin with globe-anchored drag, zoom-to-cursor, tilt, flyToAsync and collisions, and a GeospatialClippingBehavior sets near = altitude x 0.001 (min 1) and far = horizon distance sqrt(2Rh + h^2) plus 10% of planet radius. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/geospatial/geospatialCamera> — "The `GeospatialClippingBehavior` automatically adjusts near and far clip planes based on the camera's altitude above the planet surface. This optimizes depth buffer precision — near objects remain sharp when close to the surface, while distant objects (up to the horizon) remain visible when zoomed out."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Scene/View.js:473-479 (multi-frustum split list), :559-560 (`farToNearRatio` from `logarithmicDepthFarToNearRatio` when log depth is on); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPULogDepth.ts:7-22 (WebGPU log depth: ~0.42 m per quantum vs ~73 km hyperbolic at 350 km; producers and consumers gated together), :24-34 (`.w`-lane packing in `CameraUniforms`) **Meaning:** The fork handles globe-scale depth with the multi-frustum split plus logarithmic depth on both backends; Babylon uses one altitude-driven frustum (near = altitude x 0.001, far = horizon + 10% R). Any Babylon-vs-Cesium depth-precision comparison must be read against this difference; Babylon's clipping behaviour is a simpler comparator, not a gap.

<a id="census-bjs-24"></a>**BJS-24** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — 3D Tiles support in 9.0 is delivered through NASA/AMMOS 3DTilesRendererJS (tile traversal, LOD selection, loading) rather than an in-engine tiles runtime; it requires scene.useRightHandedSystem=true, recommends useLargeWorldRendering for globe-scale datasets, has documented gaps versus the full spec, and cannot run in the Playground. Source: <https://doc.babylonjs.com/features/featuresDeepDive/geospatial/loading3dTiles> — "The recommended solution for loading 3D Tiles in Babylon.js is the 3DTilesRendererJS library from NASA/AMMOS, which has recently added Babylon.js support. This library provides a full 3D Tiles renderer that handles tile set traversal, level-of-detail selection, and tile loading."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Scene/ (30 `Cesium3DTile*` modules including Cesium3DTileset.js:1-20 as the in-engine runtime); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRenderer3DTilePasses.ts, WebGPUModelRenderer.ts, WebGPUPointCloudLODProcessor.ts, WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts (WebGPU parity work against the native tileset); Scene/GaussianSplat3DTileContent.js:15-24 (`Cesium3DTileContent` implementation for splat tiles) **Meaning:** The fork owns a native 3D Tiles runtime with a WebGPU execution path, versus Babylon consuming NASA/AMMOS 3DTilesRendererJS with acknowledged spec gaps, a right-handed-system requirement and no Playground support. When Babylon is cited as a 3D Tiles competitor, its limitations README is the reference; this row is a strength, not a gap.

<a id="census-bjs-25"></a>**BJS-25** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; geospatial) — 9.0 shipped a Physically Based Atmosphere addon (Rayleigh and Mie scattering, ozone absorption, multiple scattering, day-night cycles) that was ported to native WGSL and made floating-origin aware during the 8.4x releases. Source: <https://blogs.windows.com/windowsdeveloper/2026/03/30/part-2-babylon-js-9-0-tooling-updates-and-new-geospatial-features/> — "Using physically accurate Rayleigh and Mie scattering models, along with ozone absorption and multiple scattering, the atmosphere produces breathtaking sunrises, sunsets and day-night cycles."
**Fork (Ivriniel, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Shaders/WebGPU/Environment/SkyAtmosphere.wgsl and Shaders/WebGPU/PostProcess/AerialPerspective.wgsl (native WGSL atmosphere); Shaders/WebGPU/Compute/AtmosphereLUT.wgsl:4-9 (Rayleigh + Mie LUT; multiple scattering term), :47-70 (scale heights, HG anisotropy, Rayleigh/Mie/ozone coefficients), :129-133 (Bruneton/Hillaire ozone tent profile); Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUAtmosphereLUT.ts:20-28 (Bruneton 2008 + Hillaire 2020 references; sun and moon LUT pairs) **Meaning:** The fork has a physically based, dual-language atmosphere (Rayleigh, Mie, ozone, multiple scattering, sun and moon LUTs) in native WGSL with RTE camera handling. Babylon's 9.0 addon is a like-for-like external comparator for the C12/C13 findings; nothing here is a gap, and the C17 light-transport proposal already sits beyond it.

### A.7 Bevy / wgpu / naga

27 claims: 27 KEPT, 0 REFUTED, 0 SPLIT. Fork: HAS 5 / PARTIAL 14 / MISSING 4 / NOT-APPLICABLE 4. Target as recorded by the collector: Bevy (0.1x) render graph and wgpu / naga (current releases); latest version Bevy 0.19 (0.19.0 announced 2026-06-19; patch 0.19.1 published to crates.io 2026-08-13). wgpu / naga 30.0.1 (published 2026-08-22; 30.0.0 on 2026-07-01). Bevy 0.19 pins wgpu/naga 29.0.3 (0.18 pinned 27, 0.17 pinned 26). (2026-06-19 (Bevy 0.19.0); 2026-08-13 (Bevy 0.19.1); 2026-07-01 (wgpu/naga 30.0.0); 2026-08-22 (wgpu/naga 30.0.1)).

<a id="census-bevy-arch-01"></a>**BEVY-ARCH-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Bevy 0.19 removed its explicit RenderGraph (nodes/edges/slots) and now expresses render passes as ordinary ECS systems running in per-view schedules (Core3d, Core2d) on the render world; ordering is done with system sets and .before()/.after() instead of graph edges. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-19/> — "Bevy's RenderGraph architecture has been replaced with ECS schedules. Render passes are now regular systems that run in schedules such as Core3d, Core2d, which are executed on the render world."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:8-30` (fixed numbered pass order 0 Aerial → 1 AO → 2 Bloom/GodRays → 3 DoF → 3.5 AutoExposure → 3.7 shimmer/cold → 4 TAA → 5 Tonemap → 6 user stages) and `:1463-1464`; `engine/Source/Scene/SceneRenderer.js:410` (`executeCommands`); `engine/Source/Scene/Scene.js:3751` (`frameState.commandList.length = 0` per frame) **Meaning:** The fork already runs the model Bevy converged on: ordered pass functions with explicit resource ownership, no DAG. A frame-graph proposal for the WebGPU post-process chain must justify itself against this simpler, working ordering.

<a id="census-bevy-arch-02"></a>**BEVY-ARCH-02** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — Bevy's render frame is a fixed phase sequence (ExtractCommands -> PrepareAssets -> CreateViews/Specialize/PrepareViews -> Queue -> PhaseSort -> Prepare[PrepareResources -> PrepareBindGroups] -> Render -> Cleanup); bind groups are only built after phase items are sorted, in a dedicated sub-set. Source: <https://docs.rs/bevy_render/latest/bevy_render/enum.RenderSystems.html> — "Prepare render resources from extracted data for the GPU based on their sorted order. Create BindGroups that depend on those data."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Scene/SceneRenderer.js:201,215` (`mergeSort(commands, backToFront…)` before execution); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRendererFrustumLoop.ts:355-363` (voxel commands sorted back-to-front before dispatch); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:1314` (`_bindGroupCache.beginFrame`), `:2106-2156` (per-tile bind group + `dynamicOffsets` built during the tile walk); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeBindGroupCache.ts:3-17` (identity-keyed per-tile cache) **Meaning:** Sorting does precede execution, but there is no dedicated PrepareResources → PrepareBindGroups phase: globe bind groups are resolved per tile as draws are emitted. The claim's relevance (a strict split would make `globeBindGroups()` stats easier to reason about) stands as a gap.

<a id="census-bevy-arch-03"></a>**BEVY-ARCH-03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Bevy keeps two phase kinds per view: a BinnedRenderPhase for opaque work, whose bins are grouped into batch sets that each become a single multi-draw call, and a SortedRenderPhase for order-dependent (transparent) work. Source: <https://docs.rs/bevy_render/latest/bevy_render/render_phase/struct.BinnedRenderPhase.html> — "The multidrawable bins. Each batch set key maps to a batch set, which is a set of meshes drawable together in one multidraw call."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Scene/SceneRenderer.js:201,215` (sorted translucent / splat phases = SortedRenderPhase analogue); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:2-18` (compute frustum cull writes `instanceCount=0` into the indirect buffer); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:255-273` (one `drawIndirect`/`drawIndexedIndirect` per surviving draw in a loop); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGPUSortKeysDispatcher.ts:13-25` (packed sort keys for a later sort pass) **Meaning:** The sorted-phase half is present and the indirect-draw sink exists, but opaque draws are not binned into (pipeline, mesh, material) batch sets. As the claim predicts for browser WebGPU, a batch set here can only collapse to N indirect draws (`:255-273`), never one.

<a id="census-bevy-arch-04"></a>**BEVY-ARCH-04** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; architecture) — Bevy's PipelineCache hands out IDs immediately and defers actual GPU pipeline creation to the Render step; it does not deduplicate identical pipeline descriptors, and asynchronous pipeline compilation is on by default except on macOS, Wasm and iOS. Source: <https://docs.rs/bevy_render/latest/bevy_render/render_resource/struct.PipelineCache.html> — "Note that the cache does not perform automatic deduplication of identical pipelines. It is up to the user not to insert the same pipeline twice to avoid wasting GPU resources."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:547-558` (structural key lookup, `hits`), `:760` (`device.createRenderPipelineAsync` in the browser), `:1097-1102` (`sh:` shader-module identity folded into the key), `:355,1265` (`wrongModuleHits` canary) **Meaning:** The fork does the two things Bevy does not: structural dedupe by module identity and async pipeline creation on the web. Nothing to copy; C11-180's async shader lifecycle is ahead of Bevy on Wasm.

<a id="census-bevy-arch-05"></a>**BEVY-ARCH-05** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — Bevy authors shaders as WGSL modules composed at the Naga IR level by naga_oil: #define_import_path / #import, #ifdef/#ifndef/#else/#endif and #if with shader defs, and virtual/override functions; bevy_shader additionally carries a WESL import resolver. Source: <https://github.com/bevyengine/naga_oil> — "construction of shaders from modules (which are themselves shaders)"
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUShaderPreprocessor.ts:3-27,66,94` (`//>>ifdef`/`//>>else`/`//>>endif` text preprocessor, pure over `(source, defines)`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WGSLShaderPreprocessor.ts:4-23,267,759-851` (`#import "path"` chunk resolution with dedupe + `#define/#ifdef/#ifndef/#else/#endif`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUShaderDefines.ts:64,931` (`ShaderDefine` / `ShaderDefineHi` bitmask registries); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts:9-20` (vendored naga 27 wasm) **Meaning:** Import + conditional compilation exist at text level (two preprocessors, one per lineage). Missing: IR-level compose, virtual/override functions, and a prune pass — depth/shadow variants remain hand-maintained `//>>else` branches. The vendored naga is the substrate if an IR-level prune is ever wanted.

<a id="census-wgpu-arch-01"></a>**WGPU-ARCH-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — wgpu is layered: the safe wgpu API sits on wgpu-core (which performs all validation and resource tracking) which sits on wgpu-hal, an unsafe, explicit, statically-dispatched abstraction that does no validation, over Vulkan, Metal, D3D12 and GLES backends. Source: <https://docs.rs/wgpu-hal/latest/wgpu_hal/> — "validation is the calling code's responsibility, not wgpu-hal's"
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `engine/Source/Renderer/GraphicsContext.ts:728` (`export abstract class GraphicsContext`); `engine/Source/Renderer/Context.js:394` (`class Context extends GraphicsContext`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:492` (`export class WebGPUContext extends GraphicsContext`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderPipelineCache.ts:738-751` (device-lost refusal sentinel lives in the renderer layer) **Meaning:** Same shape: abstract front, two concrete backends, permanent sentinels concentrated in the renderer layer rather than in feature renderers. Confirms the existing rule; no action.

<a id="census-naga-arch-01"></a>**NAGA-ARCH-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; architecture) — naga is wgpu's shader translator/validator: a front-end parses source into a Module IR, a Validator (configurable via ValidationFlags and Capabilities) produces ModuleInfo, and back-ends emit SPIR-V, MSL, HLSL, GLSL or WGSL; WGSL is a primary front-end. Source: <https://docs.rs/naga/latest/naga/> — "Naga can be used to translate source code written in one shading language to another."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUNagaTranspiler.ts:9-20,90` (vendored naga wasm with `validate_wgsl`); `wasm-naga/src/lib.rs:170` (`pub fn validate_wgsl`); `Tools/shader-pipeline/naga-wasm-tools/naga_wasm_tools.js` (tooling build) consumed by e.g. `Tools/visual-regression/cloud-coverage-response.spec.mjs:169-183` (`naga.validate_wgsl(...)` over assembled cloud WGSL); `package.json:160` (`test-build-infra` runs no naga sweep — only `q130-wgsl-derivative-uniformity.spec.mjs`, whose header `:3` states a text rule); `.github/workflows/dev.yml` and `prod.yml` (grep for `wgsl`/`naga`: no hits) **Meaning:** The fork already rides on naga and validates WGSL offline in a handful of cloud specs. The fleet-wide CI pass over every `Shaders/WebGPU/*.wgsl` that the claim's relevance names does not exist; the gap is a runner, not a substrate.

<a id="census-wgpu-feat-01"></a>**WGPU-FEAT-01** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — wgpu v28 (2025-12-18) shipped experimental mesh/task shaders (Features::EXPERIMENTAL_MESH_SHADER): full on Vulkan, passthrough on Metal/DX12; WGSL requires 'enable wgpu_mesh_shader;' with @task/@mesh entry points and draw_mesh_tasks / multi_draw_mesh_tasks_indirect. v30 completed Metal WGSL and HLSL (DX12) support. Source: <https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/releases/tag/v28.0.0> — "They are now fully supported on Vulkan, and supported on Metal and DX12 with passthrough shaders."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** NOT-APPLICABLE (native wgpu extension; browser WebGPU / Tint have no mesh-shader dialect) — grep `mesh shader\|meshlet\|wgpu_mesh_shader\|@mesh` over `engine/Source`: no hits; `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:255-273` (the everywhere path: compute cull + per-draw indirect) **Meaning:** Confirms the research-dispatch premise: the meshlet track's portable path is compute cluster-culling + indirect draw. If a WGSL dialect standardises it will look like wgpu's `enable wgpu_mesh_shader;`.

<a id="census-wgpu-feat-02"></a>**WGPU-FEAT-02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — wgpu v28 replaced push constants with 'immediates' to track the WebGPU spec: a single size across stages, no stage in the encode call, and the WGSL address space renamed from push_constant to immediate. When browsers ship immediates, the tile UB / per-command indices are the migration candidate; the UB path stays as the `//>>else` branch.

<a id="census-wgpu-feat-03"></a>**WGPU-FEAT-03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — wgpu v28 reworked multiview rendering (Features::MULTIVIEW, builtin(view_index)) to cover Metal and DX12 with validation, and added a per-pass view bitmask (SELECTIVE_MULTIVIEW) for non-adjacent layers. Source: <https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/releases/tag/v28.0.0> — "Multiview has been reworked, adding support for Metal and DX12, and adding testing and validation to wgpu itself."

Source: <https://docs.rs/bevy_pbr/latest/bevy_pbr/experimental/meshlet/struct.MeshletPlugin.html> — "requires a fairly recent GPU that supports `WgpuFeatures::TEXTURE_INT64_ATOMIC`"

<a id="census-bevy-feat-03"></a>**BEVY-FEAT-03** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Bevy 0.17 introduced Solari, an experimental hardware-raytraced (ray-query) real-time lighting/pathtracing renderer; 0.18 added specular/reflections and soft directional shadows; 0.19 improved mirrors, non-metallic materials and temporal stability. <a id="census-bevy-feat-04"></a>**BEVY-FEAT-04** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Bevy 0.17 added DLSS and DLSS-RR (NVIDIA RTX only) as an anti-aliasing/upscaling option alongside TAA/FXAA/SMAA; it currently requires the Vulkan backend on Windows/Linux. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-17/> — "For users with NVIDIA RTX GPUs, Bevy now offers yet another form of anti-aliasing: DLSS."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** NOT-APPLICABLE (vendor-native upscaler; not exposed to the browser) — grep `DLSS\|FSR\|upscal` over `Renderer/WebGPU` + `Shaders/WebGPU`: only cloud reconstruction (`Shaders/WebGPU/Environment/CloudUpscale.wgsl`, `CloudTemporalResolve.wgsl`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUTAAEffect.ts:248-262` (IGN jitter TAA); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:15-28` (TAA runs in linear/HDR before Tonemap) **Meaning:** The fork's TAA is present and the only scene-level temporal AA; there is no scene-level upscaler (cloud upscale is internal to the cloud reconstruction). The queued FSR2-style row remains the browser option.

<a id="census-bevy-feat-05"></a>**BEVY-FEAT-05** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Bevy 0.19 added render error recovery: a RenderErrorHandler policy (Recover = reinitialise the renderer, StopRendering, Ignore) for device loss, OOM and driver crashes, with panics reserved for internal bugs. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-19/> — "GPU errors previously had no recovery path — a driver crash, an out-of-memory condition, or a device loss would silently hang or crash the app."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUDeviceLossRecovery.ts:2,23-30` (state machine incl. RECOVERING / dead), `:235-244` (`maxAttempts` default 3), `:494-502` (retry loop), `:465` (`onDeviceLost` callback); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:7493,7932-7934` (context subscribes / re-exposes `onDeviceLost`) **Meaning:** Recovery is automatic with a retry budget and a subscriber callback, but there is no integrator-facing policy object choosing recover / stop / ignore. The claim's suggestion (a configurable policy on `WebGPUContext`, probe-testable) is a real gap.

<a id="census-bevy-feat-06"></a>**BEVY-FEAT-06** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Bevy ships per-pixel Order Independent Transparency for 3D with a bounded exact sort (sorted_fragment_max_count), an average-fragments-per-pixel buffer budget and an alpha threshold; the OIT buffer is shared and sized to the largest OIT camera. Source: <https://docs.rs/bevy_core_pipeline/latest/bevy_core_pipeline/oit/struct.OrderIndependentTransparencySettings.html> — "Controls how many fragments will be exactly sorted. If the scene has more fragments than this, they will be merged approximately. More sorted fragments is more accurate but will be slower."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUOIT.ts:3-15` (multiple-render-target weighted blended OIT, McGuire–Bavoil, `rgba16float` accumulation + revealage) **Meaning:** The fork ships weighted-blended (approximate) OIT only. A fragment-list bounded-sort OIT is expressible in WebGPU (storage buffers + atomics) and would be the correctness-over-memory alternative for translucent tiles/entities.

<a id="census-bevy-feat-07"></a>**BEVY-FEAT-07** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; feature) — Bevy 0.19 added contact shadows (short-range screen-space raycast toward lights), physically based screen-space reflections, LTC rectangular area lights, parallax-corrected reflection cubemaps, and vignette + lens-distortion post effects. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-19/> — "The core idea here is to perform a short-range (and thus affordable) screen-space raycast, tracing a line from surfaces towards lights, checking for nearby occluding objects."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — HAS: `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContactShadowsEffect.ts:5-13` (screen-space short ray toward the sun); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSSREffect.ts:3-10` (`scene.screenSpaceReflections`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPULTCLUTData.ts:3-8` + `engine/Source/Scene/LightTypes.ts:307,381` (`RectAreaLight`, `DiskAreaLight`) + `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts:55-57`; `engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/Model/ModelPBRComplete.wgsl:1199-1213` (`parallaxCorrectReflection`, box/sphere). MISSING: grep `vignette` and `lensDistortion\|lens distortion\|chromaticAberration` over `Scene`, `Renderer`, `Shaders`: no hits **Meaning:** Four of the five features in the claim are already in the fork; only the vignette / lens-distortion post effects are absent. Bevy is now a second MIT/Apache reference implementation for the ones present.

<a id="census-bevy-feat-08"></a>**BEVY-FEAT-08** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; feature) — Bevy's picking is a CPU pipeline: backends read PointerLocation and emit PointerHits which are merged by depth/order into hover state; the built-in mesh backend is a CPU ray cast, and there is no GPU ID-buffer picking in core. It does not have Bevy's multi-backend hit-merge: CPU ray picks and GPU ID picks are separate APIs, not merged into one `drillPick` result.

<a id="census-wgpu-perf-01"></a>**WGPU-PERF-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — wgpu exposes native multi-draw-indirect-count and non-zero first_instance in indirect draws as opt-in feature flags (MULTI_DRAW_INDIRECT_COUNT, INDIRECT_FIRST_INSTANCE); Bevy's GPU-driven path (0.16+) is built on multi-draw indirect plus bindless and is disabled on WebGL2. Source: <https://docs.rs/wgpu/latest/wgpu/struct.Features.html> — "Allows the user to call `RenderPass::multi_draw_indirect_count` and `RenderPass::multi_draw_indexed_indirect_count`."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGPUCuller.ts:2-8` (cull zeroes `instanceCount`, no compaction); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUIndirectDrawManager.ts:255-273` (loop of N `drawIndirect` / `drawIndexedIndirect`), `:5,13` (header wording "a single drawIndexedIndirect() ... multi-draw-indirect call" overstates the browser path) **Meaning:** Exactly the bound the claim describes: one indirect draw per surviving batch, no GPU-side draw-list compaction. The manager's header text at `:5` and `:13` should not be read as multi-draw.

<a id="census-bevy-perf-01"></a>**BEVY-PERF-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Bevy 0.19's 'Render Big Scenes Faster' work (unpacking batch sets on the GPU, batched depth-only prepasses, sparse mesh-uniform uploads, GPU light/probe/decal clustering, parallel mesh collection, change lists) took many_cubes from 49.47 ms to 18.77 ms and many_lights clustering ~20x. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-19/> — "In **Bevy 0.19**, it renders those cubes at 18.77ms (53 FPS)!"
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — Sparse uploads: `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTextures.ts:467-473` (cache hit returns the existing view before any upload), `:522-527` (`uploadImageSource` only on a miss). GPU light clustering: `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUClusteredLightingDispatcher.ts:3-17` (cluster-bounds + cluster-assign compute passes, recomputed only on viewport/projection or light-checksum change). Change lists: `engine/Source/Scene/Scene.js:3751` (command list rebuilt every frame) **Meaning:** The upload-guard lesson (Batch 717) and GPU clustering are already in place; the claim's relevance is partly stale on clustering (it is GPU-side, not CPU-side). The per-frame command-list rebuild is the remaining CPU-side cost the Bevy numbers point at.

<a id="census-bevy-perf-02"></a>**BEVY-PERF-02** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Bevy 0.19 replaced tick-based change detection in render phases with explicit change lists so phases no longer iterate every visible entity per frame. Source: <https://bevy.org/learn/migration-guides/0-18-to-0-19/> — "Previously, Bevy required rendering phases to iterate over all visible entities to determine which objects changed via ticks. This became a bottleneck, so Bevy now uses _change lists_ instead."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Scene/Scene.js:3751` (`frameState.commandList.length = 0`); `engine/Source/Scene/SceneRenderer.js:989` (`passes[j].commandList.length = 0`); retained opt-in: `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:318-324` (optional pre-encoded render bundle; the moon supplies one), `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURenderBundleManager.ts:2-7` **Meaning:** The command list is rebuilt from every primitive each frame; retained render bundles exist only as an opt-in for static renderers. Dirty-delta change lists (the C9 W2 retained-terrain direction) remain the gap, and matter at dense 3D Tiles / entity counts.

<a id="census-bevy-perf-03"></a>**BEVY-PERF-03** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; performance) — Bevy 0.19 made bindless partial: it only requests the binding-array kinds a material actually uses and fixed the sampler-limit check, yielding about +15% on Metal and up to 57 MB less RAM; most materials, including StandardMaterial, need no buffer arrays. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-19/> — "Bevy now also skips creating binding array slots for resource types a material doesn't use."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** NOT-APPLICABLE (browser WebGPU has no binding arrays (grep `binding_array` over `Shaders/WebGPU` + `Renderer/WebGPU`: no hits)) — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeSurfacePipelines.ts:385-387` (`imagery4` reduced variant carries a distinct layout + module); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:676` (`_imageryReduced = slotCount < MAX_IMAGERY_LAYERS`), `:567` (`GLOBE_IMAGERY_REDUCED` define) **Meaning:** Nothing to adopt until binding arrays ship in browsers. The claim's lesson (minimal per-feature layouts, no maximal shared layout) is already the fork's practice via the layer-count specialisation.

<a id="census-bevy-tool-01"></a>**BEVY-TOOL-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — Bevy's RenderDiagnosticsPlugin records per-pass CPU/GPU time and pipeline statistics via timestamp/statistics queries, but GPU timings work only on Vulkan and DX12; on Metal, WebGPU and WebGL2 only CPU time is recorded. 0.17 added a frame-time bar graph and 0.19 a DiagnosticsOverlayPlugin. Source: <https://docs.rs/bevy_render/latest/bevy_render/diagnostic/struct.RenderDiagnosticsPlugin.html> — "Timestamp queries and pipeline statistics are currently supported only on Vulkan and DX12. On other platforms (Metal, WebGPU, WebGL2) only CPU time will be recorded."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUTimestampProfiler.ts:9-13` (`timestamp-query`, `timestampWrites` per pass); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRendererFrustumLoop.ts:364` (`_cpuPassProfiler.beginPass("voxels")`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUFrameStatistics.ts:8-17` (frameCount / drawCallCount / triangleCount CPU counters); grep `pipeline-statistics` over `Renderer/WebGPU`: no hits **Meaning:** Per-pass GPU timing on the web is ahead of Bevy's Wasm story, and CPU pass timing exists. Pipeline-statistics queries are absent from WebGPU (counts stay CPU-estimated, as the claim says). A frame-time bar-graph overlay was not verified this session (the FPS overlay was not opened).

<a id="census-wgpu-tool-01"></a>**WGPU-TOOL-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; tooling) — wgpu-core has built-in API tracing (feature 'trace', trace_path on request_device) whose recordings replay in the 'player' crate; validation output from Khronos layers is redirected to the console, and RenderDoc/PIX/Xcode are the recommended GPU capture tools per backend. Source: <https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/wiki/Debugging-wgpu-Applications> — "API tracing is built into `wgpu-core` under the 'trace' feature"
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** MISSING — grep `replay` over `Tools/*.mjs` + `Tools/visual-regression/*.mjs`: hits are unrelated spec text (`c11-168-…`, `c11-205-…`, `c12-29-s5-…`, `cloud-tour-sequences.spec.mjs`, `backup-worker-deliverables.mjs` — names only, not opened); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUDrawCommand.ts:318-324` (render bundle is the only "recording" and is not serialisable); grep `apiTrace\|traceCommands\|commandTrace` over `WebGPUFrameCommandEncoder.ts` + `WebGPUContext.ts`: no hits **Meaning:** No record-and-replay of the command stream exists. The claim's relevance (JSON-recorded draw/compute lists replayed in a Playwright harness) is a real tooling gap for bit-reproducible regression cases.

<a id="census-wgpu-plat-01"></a>**WGPU-PLAT-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; platform) — wgpu is a pure-Rust WebGPU implementation that runs natively on Vulkan, Metal, D3D12 and OpenGL, and on WebGL2 (via naga GLSL translation) and browser WebGPU under wasm; v30 (2026-07-01) added explicit surface color-space selection for HDR/wide-gamut output and stopped defaulting integer shader I/O to flat interpolation. Source: <https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu> — "runs natively on Vulkan, Metal, D3D12, and OpenGL; and on top of WebGL2 and WebGPU on wasm."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — Dual target: `engine/Source/Renderer/GraphicsContext.ts:728`, `engine/Source/Renderer/Context.js:394`, `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUContext.ts:492`. Integer I/O: `engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/Model/ModelPBRComplete.wgsl:814-817` (`@location(10) @interpolate(flat) featureId0`); regex sweep for unannotated integer `@location` members found only `:732` (`joints0: vec4<u32>`, a vertex INPUT) and `:3992` (`SnapFragOutput.payload: vec2<u32>`, a fragment OUTPUT) — neither crosses a stage **Meaning:** The fork's WGSL carries explicit flat interpolation on its stage-crossing integer varyings, so naga-30-based tooling (transpiler, offline validation) will accept it as-is. The dual-target answer (disable GPU-driven features on the weaker backend) matches the feature-renderer fallback pattern.

<a id="census-geo-01"></a>**GEO-01** (KEPT; CONFIRMED; geospatial) — 64-bit floats in shaders are a native-only wgpu feature (SHADER_F64) documented as 16-64x slower than f32; in WGSL it is a naga-specific extension (naga_ext_f64) that 'might become an official extension', and Bevy's Transform uses f32 Vec3 with no double-precision path in core. Source: <https://docs.rs/wgpu/latest/wgpu/struct.Features.html> — "Enables 64-bit floating point types in SPIR-V shaders. Note: even when supported by GPU hardware, 64-bit floating point operations are frequently between 16 and 64 _times_ slower than equivalent operations on 32-bit floats."
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** HAS — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPURTEAssertions.ts:7` (assertion that `encodedCameraHigh`/`Low` slots are not swapped); `encodedCameraHigh` present in 85 WGSL files under `Shaders/WebGPU` (grep count); `engine/Source/Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:55,63` (`(center3DHigh - encodedCameraHigh) + (center3DLow - encodedCameraLow)`) **Meaning:** Confirms the fork's RTE high/low pairs + camera-relative RTC are the only portable planet-scale precision strategy; there is no GPU f64 path to wait for. Bevy's `big_space` is floating origin on the CPU, the same idea.

<a id="census-geo-02"></a>**GEO-02** (KEPT; PLAUSIBLE; geospatial) — Bevy's procedural atmosphere gained a raymarched path for accurate views from above the atmosphere (0.17), and 0.18 generalised it into a ScatteringMedium asset for arbitrary and non-Earth atmospheres plus atmosphere-aware sunlight/PBR shading; in 0.19 Atmosphere became its own entity. Source: <https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-18/> — "Bevy 0.18 introduces a new ScatteringMedium asset for designing atmospheric scattering media of all kinds: clear desert skies, foggy coastlines, and even atmospheres of other planets!"
**Fork (Findegil, HEAD `01226c648a`):** PARTIAL — `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUAtmosphereUniforms.ts:28,43-47,56-57` (`rayleighCoefficient`, `mieCoefficient`, scale heights, `mieAnisotropy` sourced from `scene.atmosphere`); `engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUAtmosphereLUT.ts:3-10` (sun + moon transmittance/inscatter LUT pairs with parameter UBOs) **Meaning:** Scattering is parameterised (DP-H47) rather than baked, but as Earth-shaped coefficients on `scene.atmosphere`, not a general scattering-medium asset for non-Earth or weather-driven media. The above-atmosphere raymarch path was not verified this session (sky WGSL not opened).
